# experimentalModule — Refactoring intent, target state, and gap analysis

Provenance method: every claim is tagged.
- `[stated: file:line]` — explicit in source (comment, name, or strategy.txt).
- `[observed: file:line]` — code fact, verified by reading.
- `[inferred]` — reconstruction of intent from converging evidence; **needs validation by Vixy** — if an inferred goal is wrong, everything derived from it below must be re-derived.
- `[vixy: 2026-07-11]` — provided directly by Vixy (review of this document's first version); highest-authority intent source.
- `[defect]` — found during analysis; listed in §5 regardless of comfort.
- `[open]` / `[resolved]` — decision state; resolved entries keep their answer visible (traceability), they are not deleted.

Document status: `experimentalModule` is halfway between a draft and a specification [vixy: 2026-07-11]. Headers are Vixy's formalization medium — the closest representation of what is wanted [vixy: 2026-07-11]; therefore ModularBody.hpp's comment blocks (relations, shadow projection, depth buffers, threads — lines 95-137) are normative intent, and implementation-in-header is partly a drafting artifact, not only a G10 choice. This file is the consolidated extraction + gap/decision tracker; on divergence between it and a header, neither silently wins — reconcile explicitly.

Document maintenance invariant [verified 2026-07-22, not declared]: **no state is dropped without its transition being recorded** (supersession-with-record: strikethrough-not-delete, rename-trails `ex-Axx`/`was :N`, explicit supersession markers, dashboard flips citing their journal entry). §11 journal entries are append-only — corrections annotate and preserve the original text; §13-class tables and status lines mutate in place, their transitions journaled. What can be trusted and why: verified by exhaustive audit of every line ever deleted from this file across its full pre-split git history (238 deleted lines; matched exact → substring → token-overlap → per-line manual review; all classified information-preserving, zero orphaned). Trust boundary: **exhaustive through code-repo commit `c523e3b1`** (the 2026-07-22 repo split); after it, discipline-maintained — the CC-harness repo's own history is the re-verification instrument (same audit, re-runnable).

Comparison baseline: old path = `src/bodyModule` (`Body`, `ProtoSystem`, `SolarSystem*`), new path = `src/experimentalModule`. Both are alive simultaneously by design (§1.3).

---

## 1. Current situation

### 1.1 What the old path is (the problem being refactored away)

- `Body` is a god class: ~700-line header, 9 friend classes, and one hardcoded member per feature — `trail`, `hints`, `axis`, `planetGrid`, `orbitPlot`, `halo`, `atmExt` [observed: bodyModule/body.hpp:653-659]. Every body carries every feature slot whether applicable or not; adding a feature means modifying `Body` itself.
- Behavior specialization by inheritance: `body_bigbody`, `body_moon`, `body_smallbody`, `body_star`, `body_sun`, `body_artificial`, `body_center` [observed: bodyModule/ directory]. Nature is encoded in the class taxonomy, so feature combinations (ringed star, artificial with atmosphere…) require new classes or flags — the structure must deform to accept new cases.
- Rendering plumbing lives inside each body: per-body command buffers `cmds[3]`, `drawState`, `virtual selectShader()=0` [observed: body.hpp:379,596-599]. Astronomy, rendering, UI (hints/labels/translation), and selection (ObjectBase) are one class.
- System-level behavior is split into aspect managers each iterating all bodies (`solarsystem_color/display/scale/selected/tex`) [observed: bodyModule/ directory] — behavior far from the data it depends on (violates I2/I4 of programmation-principles).
- Fixed two-level hierarchy: `parent` + `satellites`, `is_satellite` flag [observed: body.hpp:614-615,628]. No arbitrary nesting, no multiple systems as first-class citizens; special cases (Earth, Moon, Sun) spread through the code.
- Lifetime: `shared_ptr` web + `enable_shared_from_this` [observed: body.hpp:116].

### 1.2 What the new path is

Files: `ModularBody` (tree node + orchestrator), `BodyModule` (feature unit), `ModularSystem` (system root, itself a `ModularBody`), `ModuleLoader`/`ModuleLoaderMgr` + `modules.cpp` (data-driven instantiation), `ModularBodyPtr` (safe long-lived reference), `Camera` (observer with reference-body semantics), `Renderer` (sole Vulkan owner), `AsyncHub` (tick hub), `EnvironmentModule` (stub, scope now defined — §3.8), `ModularObject` (bridge to old `ObjectBase` UI), plus concrete modules in `bodyModules/`, `meshModules/`, `orbitModules/`. Exactly one `BodyModule` family implemented so far: MESH via `BasicMesh` [vixy: 2026-07-11; observed: meshModules/].

### 1.3 The migration strategy itself (dual path)

- `SSystemFactory` holds **both** paths and updates both every frame [observed: ssystem_factory.cpp:393-395]; `drawModularSystem` selects which one draws [observed: ssystem_factory.cpp:381-388] and auto-toggles every 1000 ms [observed: ssystem_factory.cpp:397-401].
- Purpose: **visual comparison** of the two paths — the old path is the reference, the alternation makes divergence directly visible to the eye [vixy: 2026-07-11].
- ~~Active investigation: Moon position divergence~~ **RESOLVED (2026-07-11, predictive-model validation — §11.15)**: five structural causes named, fixed, and measured to float epsilon (relative positions old==new ≤ 7e-6 deg / ≤ 2.3e-7 distances at two dates; predicted matrices == observed at ~5e-8 both paths). The WANT/GOT/creation prints (investigation artifacts) are removed.

---

## 2. Extracted intent

**Product intent — the two roots** [vixy: 2026-07-17, stated directly; this is the root, not an extraction — everything in §2.0/§2.1 is downstream of it]:

> **Every decision is rooted at two core things.**
> **(R1) Potential expansion** — providing the widest potential, to enable as much as possible; *"what I look for, which optimise the widest spectrum of needs and use cases — it's mostly universal."*
> **(R2) The use case: small interactive planetariums** — *"what makes spacecrafter what it is, and why it came into existence in the first place."*

R1 is the criterion and is substrate-independent (it holds outside spacecrafter, and outside code). R2 is the domain it is instantiated in, and the project's identity/origin. R1 without R2 has no direction (expand potential toward what?); R2 without R1 is a feature list. **Neither is negotiable against convenience, and together they are the highest-authority source in this document** — above §2.0's domain constraints, which they force.

**Correction of this block's first draft (2026-07-17, same day — recorded because the error is instructive, not to be tidied away)**: it opened with *"Physical truth is spacecrafter's main priority, because it is for educative purpose"* and treated that as the root. **That is a consequence, mis-elevated.** The falsifier is in the shipped data: `[Solar_System]` and `[Goldilocks_Zone]` are declared as **bodies** — `type = Asteroid`/`Planet`, `radius = 1`, `orbit_semimajoraxis = 0`, `albedo = 0`, `hidden = true` [observed: ssystem.ini] — pedagogical constructs with no physical referent, wearing the body primitive because it is general enough to carry them with no special mechanism. Under "physical truth is the root" they are anomalies; under R1+R2 they are exactly right, and they are **evidence the data already exploits the generality R1 demands**. Truth is where R1 lands in the model layer — not the root itself (derivation in (a) below). A root that its own shipped data contradicts was never the root.

Consequences, each marked by derivation status — they resolve decisions this file previously had to escalate:

- **(a) Physical truth is R1 AT THE MODEL LAYER — the carrier, not the root [derived; the derivation is load-bearing and must be re-run, not recalled]**. *Why* truth: a physically-true model answers questions **nobody has asked yet** — it is the single choice that maximises the enabled use-case spectrum, because it is not fitted to any of them. A model fitted to a look enables exactly the cases it was fitted to and forecloses the rest. So truth is not terminal; it is **what R1 selects wherever a model is what's being built** (educative purpose is R2 supplying the domain in which unanticipated questions are the whole point). This is why §6.8 → (A), and it is a stronger reason than "truth is the priority": (A) wins because it *generalises*, and being true is how.
  **Two layers, and they do not conflict** — the apparent tension (a true model vs. a `Goldilocks_Zone`) dissolves on the layer distinction: **the model layer** is governed by truth (fitting forecloses); **the presentation/tool layer** is governed directly by enablement — grids, axes, orbit lines, trails, `Solar_System`, `Goldilocks_Zone` are non-physical **by design and correctly so**. A true model does not prevent drawing a pedagogical construct over it; it is what makes the construct **trustworthy**. Never "correct" a tool for being non-physical, and never fit a model to make a tool easier.
  **Consequence for §12's replacement criterion [subordinated]**: the A/B screenshot channel is the **instrument** for detecting *unintended* divergence, never the goal. Where the old path forecloses (physically wrong model, or a gate), the new path **must** diverge — **A3-class entries ("new is right, old is broken") are wins, not parity debt**. Read §12's criterion as *"legacy commands produce visually identical output **except where old forecloses and the divergence is named with its mechanism**"*. Both parts required to accept a divergence: (1) it expands potential (in the model layer that means: nearer truth), (2) it is attributed to a named cause — never "by design", never fitted (§11.19 residual-absorption class). Live instances this legitimises rather than tolerates: the fov-0.05 Mars black disc (§11.26), the "moon" generalisation on non-Earth references (§11.27(4)), the >20% HINT label (§11.24a), the Sun's 7.25° obliquity (§11.34), and §6.8 → (A).
- **(a2) Gates foreclose, parameters expand — a general test, not a ring decision [derived from R1; first surfaced as shadow-paths D8]**. The `ring_shadow` resolution (*"replace the dead `ring_shadow` with visual fidelity to properly generalize with other bodies (otherwise it doesn't cast on child bodies)"* [vixy: 2026-07-17]) is **R1 applied to a data key**, and the reasoning transfers unchanged: a boolean that *gates a capability* forecloses every case its author did not enumerate (a ring that "doesn't cast" cannot cast on the planet's moons either); a key that *parameterises* a capability (`ring_shadow_color`, default = the derived old-parity constant) is additive and forecloses nothing. **Test to apply at every data key and every enum**: does it declare a capability/parameter (expands — keep) or an identity/permission (forecloses — retire, let fidelity decide)? Same shape as `useParentPrecession` (§11.34: a flag whose declared role is dead and whose real role is a rendering switch) and as §6.3's `BodyType` residual (`// Deprecated` at ModularBody.hpp:79; `MINOR_BODY` survives precisely because it declares a *capability* — shadow exemption, cluster-optimisable — while SPHERICAL/SINGLE/CUSTOM_BODY look like *identities*; that entry is now derivable rather than escalatable — re-derive, don't assume).
- **(b) User scripts are live-show content ⇒ immutable [R2 + derived]**. Planetarium operators' scripts cannot be migrated, so any convention correction must be **structural in the engine**, never a data re-authoring. This is the root reason behind §6.8/§11.34's route (previously recorded as a constraint with no stated origin) and behind the `body action load` argument that ruled out the declaration-only fix. Under R1 the point sharpens: breaking existing scripts *destroys* potential already realised — the worst possible R1 violation, since it removes use cases that demonstrably exist.
- **(c) Scriptable AND scriptless-with-full-potential = R1 applied to the control surface [stated]; script-only reachability is a defect [derived]**. Two channels, neither foreclosing the other — that is *why* both exist, and why "scriptless" is not a convenience mode but a full-potential one. Any capability reachable only from a script is a gap against the core intent, not a nit. ~~**Live instance: `freeMode` is command-unreachable**~~ **CLOSED (2026-07-17, §11.36)**: `camera action free_mode state on|off` lands (coreLink → Camera::setFreeMode); the whole reference-transition layer executed at runtime for the first time and carries the scene-E verification. ~~A successor instance of the same class found the same day: `reloadSystem` has zero command-reachable callers (§11.36 suspended).~~ **ALSO CLOSED (2026-07-21, §11.55)**: `body action reload` lands (coreLink → SSystemFactory::reloadCurrentSystem), state-preserving per Q27, and **both** channels were exercised on the running app rather than argued from the shared dispatch. It also **raises the bar on the S6/§9 command-surface audit**: the criterion is not "every old command still works" but "every capability is reachable dynamically", which is a strictly larger surface — the audit must enumerate *capabilities*, not *commands* (a capability with no command is exactly what a command-keyed audit cannot see; §11.33's spec-8 inventory is the same blind spot one layer down).
- **(d) R2's "small interactive planetarium" ⇒ reactivity is terminal, not a perf nicety [derived]**. Interactive + adapting to public reaction means the operator acts live and unplanned, so latency is a **correctness** property of the product, not an optimisation. This is the **root reason** for constraints §8/§10 previously justified only by hardware/D5: C3 (draw thread never blocks), the dedicated ACTIVE-priority worker lane (§8.2.10 — "a guaranteed free lane for the needed-now class"), never build-on-first-use in-frame + base-variant-always-ready (§10.2), the no-stall publish-swap discipline. Derived bottom-up from the substrate AND top-down from R2 — two independent derivations to the same constraints, which is why they are not negotiable against convenience.
- **(e) "Small" is a load-bearing word in R2 [derived — do not drop it]**. Small interactive planetariums run on modest, single-machine hardware and are operated live by one person, not a render farm with a pipeline team. This is the origin of the D5 hardware-range constraint (4–64 cores, the VRAM degradation ladder), of the "don't precompile the 2 GiB feature cross-product" rule (§10.2), and of why the whole threading design targets *one machine's* core count rather than assuming scale-out. It also bounds R1: potential is expanded **within reach of the actual deployment**, not in the abstract — a capability that needs hardware the venues do not have is not enabled potential for spacecrafter's use case. R1 says "widest potential"; R2's "small" says "reachable on the box in the room".

- **(f) Actionable diagnostics [vixy: 2026-07-21, §11.51(d) — stated on A31, general by its own wording]**. When an input doesn't match any valid state: the log entry lists **which states are valid**, traces it **as an error**, and states **what it fell back to** — written so that *"an arbitrary user looking at it could understand which actions to take in order to resolve the problems, not only what is the problem"*. Action-oriented, not description-oriented: a diagnostic that names the problem but not the fix still leaves the user in unmapped space. First client: the loader's frame/enum validation (B28); applies to every new diagnostic surface from here on (incl. A32's file-shadowing case, §11.51(a)).

### 2.0 Domain constraints that force the design [vixy: 2026-07-11]

These are facts about the problem space, prior to any architecture. Most of the structure below is their consequence; each carries its propagation edges.

- **D1 — Child body size is generally negligible relative to parent body size.**
  → per-body depth slices instead of one global depth range (§3.6; `Renderer::clearDepth(zCenter, boundingRadius)` [observed: Renderer.hpp:18-19]);
  → self-shadow drawing doubles as depth-prefill of grounded bodies' slices with the parent body — a grounded body cannot occlude correctly against a parent whose scale dwarfs its whole depth slice (§3.1; matches "parent's depth trace is drawn in each DepthBuffer" [stated: ModularBody.hpp:123]).
- **D2 — Body size is generally negligible relative to the distance between a parent and its children.**
  → same depth-slicing consequence as D1: a depth range covering parent-child distance leaves no precision for the body itself;
  → `[inferred]` together with D1, forces the tree of *local* coordinate frames and observer-relative matrices (G2/G3): float32 global coordinates cannot hold AU-scale distances and meter-scale surface detail simultaneously. **Needs validation.**
- **D3 — There can easily be thousands of bodies, but very few are actually visible at a time (the rest halo-only), often exactly one.**
  → halo-only is the *common case*, full drawing the exception — G4's gating is the normal mode, not an optimization edge (§ constants ModularBody.hpp:80-93; halo fallback ModularBody.hpp:308-312);
  → depth buckets are assigned only to the few bodies needing one (`notableBody`, §3.6);
  → frame-coherent O(n) resort is viable because the sorted set is mostly stable [stated: ModularSystem.cpp:70-72].
  Border case: an upscaled asteroid ring makes *many* small bodies visible simultaneously — this is what `MINOR_BODY` exists for: mass-instanced, simple bodies that do **not** cast shadows between each other (in reality too small for that to be observable) [vixy: 2026-07-11] → connects `BodyType::MINOR_BODY` [observed: ModularBody.hpp:37] to the replication/cluster traits (`MBT_REPLICATED`, `MBT_CLUSTER` [observed: ModularBody.hpp:47-51], `BMT_REPLICATED` [observed: BodyModule.hpp:46]) and partially answers §6.3.
- **D4 — A whole-body texture at surface-level resolution does not fit in VRAM.**
  **Second, independent forcing reason [vixy: 2026-07-20]: it does not fit in a TEXTURE, either** — a single whole-body surface map exceeds the max texture dimension (*"not loading the whole body as a single too-large texture (above 32k)"*). This is a hard API/hardware cap, not a budget: unlike VRAM it is never relieved by better hardware, so module substitution near the surface is forced even on a machine with unlimited memory. Recorded because D4's original derivation rested on VRAM alone, which is the *softer* of the two constraints — the design was right for a reason weaker than the one available.
  → approaching the surface requires **module substitution**, not just parameter scaling: a different `BodyModule` takes over drawing, switching from whole-body texture to visible-surface-segment-only, activating whenever the difference becomes visible and relevant [vixy: 2026-07-11];
  → this is the reason the regime lists (far/near/grounded/in) exist as *substitution* points and not only as work gates (G4), and why "Surface BodyModule (shown instead of Outer BodyModule…)" is a distinct relation [stated: ModularBody.hpp:104];
  → surface-segment streaming is the most demanding client of G5's async loading (segments must appear as the camera moves, without stalls).
- **D5 — Hardware spans roughly an order of magnitude in every dimension: 4–64 cores, 2–32 GiB VRAM, 1k–8k screens** [vixy: 2026-07-11].
  → no fixed budgets or thread counts anywhere: pool sizes, VRAM budget, LoD caps are parameters with degradation ladders;
  → at the 4-core floor, a preempted lock-holder can stall the render thread for a scheduling quantum → render-thread boundaries must be lock-free, not merely briefly-locked (§8).
- **D6 — Resource asymmetry** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: textures are the hard case — loaded *and* unloaded dynamically by what is load-bearing now/soon, 2–3 LoD levels, lowest always resident, highest by relevance or anticipation. Models are VRAM-cheap: build-once (memcpy from native-form cache when available; building from source is slow), no eviction pressure.
  → residency management is a texture problem; models need only the build/handoff path;
  → the LoD ladder means handoff is "upgrade/downgrade between resident levels", never "appear from nothing" — together with `drawLoaded`, something drawable is always resident.
- **D7 — Completions are rare but bursty, never sustained; resources are heavily shared** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: a script can load thousands of bodies of which few are visible; hundreds of small completions/second at 8k is possible as a peak, never as a steady state. Some textures and most models are heavily shared across bodies.
  → the handoff must absorb bursts without per-frame cost in the idle case (lock-free chains grow unbounded; drain budget as safety valve, never the sizing case);
  → the unit of loading is the **resource**, not the (body, module) pair: request deduplication + completion fan-out to all attached bodies is mandatory, refcounted lifetime included (`AsyncBuilder::useCount` is the existing pattern).
- **D8 — The AS-IF rule (fidelity criterion): from the user standpoint the software must behave *as if* everything were physically exact and fully computed; any internal deviation (lazy, frozen, skipped, iterative) is valid exactly as long as no user-reachable observable can tell** [vixy: 2026-07-22, two statements — the DECISIONS_PENDING D2 answer ("physically exact, or visually identical to the physically identical" = the VISUAL projection of this rule) + the same-day as-if completion (§11.76); NB: DECISIONS_PENDING D-keys are decision keys, unrelated to this list's D-numbers].
  → sharpens §11.52(b): parity/fidelity is judged perceptually *because* observable-equivalence is the acceptance bar — not because approximation is acceptable in itself; script-fetch and warp-to are observable channels EQUAL to the screen;
  → **use-site barrier** [vixy: 2026-07-22, §11.76]: the moment a position is *used* — fetched from script, warped to (the class [derived]: any user-reachable read — draw/show, selection, save included) — it must be computed; a previously-frozen body is **recomputed with 4 extra iterations** of the multi-frame iterative calculation (convergence restoration; the value 4 is [vixy]-specified). **Freezing is LEGAL under this barrier** — the constraint is "never expose a stale read", not "keep ticking";
  → performance mechanisms licensed [vixy: 2026-07-22]: multi-frame iterative position calculation (visually identical for human vision), and skipping work for bodies neither shown nor distinguishable (hidden, or halo indistinguishable from the main body) — enabling tens of thousands of bodies at 160 Hz with single-threaded position/visibility update;
  → ~~surface tension with §11.54 (B19: hidden bodies DO advance, tester-mandated Q13) — [derived reconciliation]: "aren't updated" = exempt from full per-frame work, never position-frozen; zero-update would violate Q13's "where it should be now"~~ **SUPERSEDED [vixy 2026-07-22, §11.76(c)]: the reconciliation is the use-site barrier, not mandatory ticking — zero-update between uses is valid; Q13's "where it should be now" is satisfied at the use (show = a use). §11.54's continuous translation-only tick is ONE valid mechanism, not the mandate; its regression harness stays discriminating either way (it reads positions through a use channel — barrier-compatible)**;
  → same rule, other domain: §11.66's exhaustive-save semantics ("save→quit→reload as-if we continued") is this rule applied to session persistence — one product principle, stated independently twice;
  → data consequence: garbage physical data (the B14 tilt classes) violates the rule on every channel wherever a body can be resolved or queried → drives the DECISIONS_PENDING D2(a) widening (§11.75(b)).
- **D9 — Data is the product; the field is frozen** [vixy: 2026-07-22, §11.77]. Code = open-source; **the data is what is paid** (pro = open-source spacecrafter + data feeding features/capabilities — the funding; the lower plan is FREE and sufficient for entry — small planetariums have small funds, and enthusiasts need no paid data: capability + documentation suffice, §11.77(f)). Deliveries are frozen individually; updates are OFFLINE — no automated fetch in the general case — operated by hand, taking the latest *sources*, not data ⇒ **in-field data is immutable-in-practice**.
  → **backward compatibility is forced, not preferred**: the code must keep rendering every in-field data version correctly, indefinitely — the §11.52 legacy-format guarantee, Q26's "not in the data → never activated", and B28's derived-default frame now carry their forcing argument;
  → data is the capability-delivery vehicle: B24/B25/B27's data-declared capabilities are the channel through which the paid product lights up features — grammar and key spellings are PRODUCT SURFACE, not internals;
  → the §11.66(b) write-back preservation contract is business-grounded: the writer touches paid, hand-authored product data — corruption there is not a bug class, it is a product-destruction class;
  → corrections/improvements propagate FORWARD only (into `spacecrafter-data` → future deliveries), never into the field (no fetch channel exists) — the B14 class: fix upstream, tolerate in-field garbage forever (the loader's derived defaults carry it).
- **D10 — Optimize the potential** [vixy: 2026-07-22, §11.77 — stated with its own uncertainty marker: *"Maybe I'm wrong, only time could tell me I guess"*]. *"I always optimise the potential, what is not needed by (almost) everyone is not what's not needed, to me."* The enthusiast minority pushing past tested/thought capabilities is a first-class constituency: it is (i) the **discovery source** — surprising scripts reveal what the software can become, and this refactoring EXISTS because that loop already fired once — (ii) the **pro-product ceiling** — composition capability bounds how rich the paid data can get, so potential-optimization and the funding model are one objective, not two — and (iii) the **niche is structurally defended** [§11.77(e)]: the small interactive planetarium is a small-but-unfilled segment that large planetarium software cannot want (audience size kills real interactivity), so the flexibility has no large-player substitute.
  → qualifier that keeps it safe [derived]: the generality optimized is STRUCTURAL (orthogonal composable primitives — combination space without per-feature width), never feature-width; I1–I6 + the parity discipline are the counterweight against unbounded-framework drift;
  → cost bound, already landed: the median path stays simple — §11.50(b)'s two authoring paths (legacy untouched, composition opt-in) IS this bound;
  → falsifiable-in-flight [derived — time is the terminal judge, not the only signal]: leading indicators = scripts keep discovering new combinations; pro-data richness per authoring effort rises; default-path simplicity holds; enthusiast population grows **once capability AND documentation both exist** — the gate is documentation [vixy: §11.77(f)], entry cost being already minimal (free tier), so pre-docs flatness is confounded, not invalidating. The inverse observations invalidate;
  → documentation is the D10-completing work item [vixy: sequenced after core-ready, §11.77(f)]: capability present but undocumented is potential locked — the sequencing is a recorded decision, its trigger is core-readiness.

### 2.1 Goals

Each goal states the evidence that forces it.

**G1 — Composition over inheritance: all per-feature variability lives in `BodyModule` instances; `ModularBody` never knows feature specifics.**
Evidence: abstract `BodyModule` interface with draw/shadow/trace hooks [observed: BodyModule.hpp:58-103]; `ModularBody` holds anonymous component lists, no feature members [observed: ModularBody.hpp:689-697]; no `ModularBody` subclass except `ModularSystem` (a structural, not feature, specialization) [observed: ModularSystem.hpp:7]. Residual hardcoding is quarantined and named as such (`applyHardcodedContent`, `BodyType::EARTH/EARTH_MOON` "Bodies with hard-coded specificities") [stated: ModularBody.hpp:42-44, ModularSystem.cpp:272-280].

**G2 — One uniform, arbitrarily deep hierarchy: galaxy ⊃ systems ⊃ bodies ⊃ moons ⊃ surface objects, with systems as bodies.**
Evidence: `ModularSystem : public ModularBody` [observed: ModularSystem.hpp:7]; grounded/orbiting/inner/hidden child lists [observed: ModularBody.hpp:680-683]; `milkyway` as root system holding solar systems [observed: ssystem_factory.hpp:593, ssystem_factory.cpp:273]; `boundToSurface` bodies (surface objects in the same tree) [observed: ModularBody.hpp:745, ModularSystem.cpp:198-199]. ← D1+D2 `[inferred]`: local frames per node are what keeps float32 viable across scales.

**G3 — Seamless navigation across scales: the observer holds a reference body, switches reference by area of influence, without visual discontinuity.**
Evidence: `Camera::switchToBody` "without moving" [stated: Camera.hpp:20-23]; `calculateSwitchCompensation` "for a seamless change of body reference" [stated: ModularBody.hpp:439-440]; `findBetterReference` + `areaOfInfluence` [observed: ModularBody.hpp:492-500,728].

**G4 — Visibility- and distance-driven work gating *and module substitution*: compute only what the observer can distinguish, and draw each regime with the representation built for it.**
Evidence (gating): cheap `preUpdate` visibility classification before any full update; invisible subtrees only get their translation refreshed [observed: ModularBody.hpp:200-242,391-402]; explicit pixel-size and distance thresholds as named constants [stated: ModularBody.hpp:80-93]; draw degrades to halo-only below thresholds [observed: ModularBody.hpp:275-314] — the common case, per D3.
Evidence (substitution): far/near/grounded/in component lists [stated: ModularBody.hpp:694-697]; surface-segment module replacing whole-body module near the ground ← D4 [vixy: 2026-07-11].

**G5 — Asynchronous, prioritized resource lifecycle: the render thread never waits on loading.**
The core design problem here — mostly *searched*, not yet solved — is **how to transmit completed loads from the loading thread to the render thread without data race and with minimal overhead** [vixy: 2026-07-11].
Evidence: priority ladder LAZY/BACKGROUND/PRELOAD/ACTIVE [stated: strategy.txt:1-5, ResourcePriority ModularBody.hpp:139-146 — naming unified, legacy `NOW` renamed, verified in tree 2026-07-11]; `isLoaded`/`preload` on modules and `drawLoaded` degraded-draw path [observed: BodyModule.hpp, ModularBody.hpp:272-273,306-307]; `MAX_LAZY_TEXTURE_SIZE` [stated: ModularBody.hpp:90-91]; thread inventory [stated: ModularBody.hpp:131-137]; `AsyncHub` "used to reduce constraints" [stated: AsyncHub.hpp:7].
The handoff sketch in strategy.txt ("Taskable: ModularBody" + "ModularBody drawing: Update position / **Execute tasks**" [stated: strategy.txt:7-13]) — loader threads post completion-tasks to the body, render thread executes them at draw — reading **confirmed** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: it was the best strategy found at the time, explicitly not claimed best possible. Superseded by the §8 proposal (global drain replaces at-draw execution, closing the D3 starvation channel). ← D4: surface-segment streaming is the client that makes this load-bearing.
Constraints for the rework [vixy: 2026-07-11]: **C1** no race between loading-side changes to a body and drawing; **C2** no destruction of a body while a task on it is pending; **C3** draw thread never blocks; **C4** loading thread may block.

**G6 — Data-driven instantiation: which modules a body gets is deduced from its data, loaders compete, registration is centralized.**
Evidence: `deduceBodyModuleList` from params [observed: ModularBody.cpp:301-312]; `isLikely` competitive selection, highest bidder wins [stated+observed: ModuleLoader.hpp:10-11, ModuleLoaderMgr.cpp:46-64]; all registration in one place, `modules.cpp` [stated: ModuleLoaderMgr.hpp:17].

**G7 — Generalized shadow system replacing special-cased eclipse handling.**
Evidence: shadow trait bits [observed: BodyModule.hpp:45-56]; the four drawing types (§3.1) [vixy: 2026-07-11]; per-body `shadowAbsorbtion` (Earth's `{0,1,1}`) [stated: ModularBody.hpp:117-118]; shadow-projection and depth-buffer strategy [stated: ModularBody.hpp:120-129]; self-shadow resolutions [observed: ModularBody.hpp:92-93]. Old path: moon-specific `tex_eclipse_map`, `sun_half_angle` [observed: body.hpp:637,640]. Scope boundary ← D3: `MINOR_BODY` mass-instanced bodies are exempt from inter-body shadowing [vixy: 2026-07-11].

**G8 — Safe non-owning references without a shared_ptr web.**
Evidence: `ModularBodyPtr` registry with `redirect(from, to)` on body removal, "prefer raw pointer when short-lived" [stated: ModularBodyPtr.hpp:9-10,39]. Invariant I5 (reference-without-ownership legal because the target notifies/redirects on destruction), replacing `enable_shared_from_this`.

**G9 — Centralized rendering: `Renderer` is the only Vulkan surface in the module; bodies and modules describe, the renderer executes.**
Evidence: `Renderer` owns command buffers, depth management, halo drawing [observed: Renderer.hpp]; no Vulkan member in `ModularBody`; modules receive `Renderer&` [observed: BodyModule.hpp:73]. Extended responsibility ← D1/D2/D3: depth-range partitioning across visible bodies (§3.6).

**G10 — Performance as a structural driver, not an afterthought.**
Evidence: frame-coherent O(n) resort with documented rationale [stated: ModularSystem.cpp:70-72] ← D3; `casify` string switch [observed: ModularSystem.cpp:11-15]; `findBody` last-hit cache with a cache-bypass variant for one-shot searches [stated: ModularBody.hpp:515-522]; `StringID` interning over string keys [observed: ModularBody.hpp:623]; hot paths inlined in headers [observed: ModularBody.hpp draw/preUpdate/update].

**G11 — Old-path compatibility during migration, not after.**
Evidence: `ModularObject : ObjectBase` bridge [observed: ModularObject.hpp]; "Compatibility methods" on Camera [stated: Camera.hpp:130]; deprecated fields carried and explicitly marked in the new structs [stated: ModularBody.hpp:74-77,705,730].

## 3. Target state — how it should be (header-level contracts)

Headers are the specification (programmation-principles I1) — doubly so here, since headers are the medium in which this design is being thought [vixy: 2026-07-11]. Each class below is defined by what it promises and what it must not know. Anything a caller needs that is not stated here is a defect of this spec, to be fixed here first.

### 3.1 `BodyModule` — the unit of feature composition
- Contract: a drawable/loadable feature attached to a body. Knows *how* to draw itself in a given regime; never *when* — regime selection belongs to `ModularBody` (I4).
- **Four drawing types** [vixy: 2026-07-11]:
  1. **Color** (`draw`, with `drawNoDepth` as its minimalist depth-less variant for small screen sizes) — the visible image.
  2. **Self-shadow** (`drawSelfShadow`) — dual purpose: (a) fill the self-shadow depth buffer; (b) prefill the depth-buffer slice of each grounded body with the parent body ← D1 (the grounded body's whole depth slice is negligible at parent scale; without prefill, parent-vs-grounded occlusion is wrong).
  3. **Shadow** (`drawShadow`) — stencil map of the shadow this body projects onto *other* bodies.
  4. **Trace** (`drawTrace`) — depth-like pass that cuts a hole where the orbit line must be hidden by the body (the new-path analog of old `drawOrbit(cmdBodyDepth,…)`).
- Lifecycle: `isLoaded()` is a non-blocking query; `preload()` is a hint, never a stall; `update()` returns true when it no longer needs updates (self-deregistration semantics); `draw*()` must be callable with partial resources only via the `drawLoaded` path.
- Ordering guarantee (now stated in the header, 2026-07-11): `update()` must have run before `compare()`/`getBoundingRadius()` are meaningful — `boundingRadius` is undefined until then.
- Trait bits declare rendering-pipeline needs (depth, shadows, batching); the renderer reads traits, modules never touch the pipeline directly.

### 3.2 `ModularBody` — tree node and orchestrator
- Owns: identity (name), tree position (parent/children by relation), transforms (orbit + rotation elements), cached observer-relative state (mat, distance, screenSize, visibility flags), component ownership (`components` by slot) and routing (four regime lists).
- **Frame contract (completed 2026-07-17, §11.35)**: translations flat (ephemeris vectors are root-aligned), orientations ACCUMULATED — a body's `mat` carries its accumulated equatorial frame from the single authority (`accumulatedBodyPosToBody`; self unconditional, ancestors iff not system-centered, stop at bound seams), the same frame the camera declares the observer in. Composing an orientation from `computeBodyPosToBody` directly re-splits the concept (the §11.34 class).
- Must not know: any concrete module type, any Vulkan object, any UI concept.
- Update pipeline (per frame): `selectiveUpdate` → `transformParentToBodyPos` → `preUpdate` (visibility classification, cheap — the common exit, per D3) → full `update` + `recursiveUpdate` only if visible.
- Position updating is to become **iterative across frames** [vixy: 2026-07-11]: one refinement iteration per frame, reusing the previous frame's position — visually equivalent to full per-frame convergence (the eye tracks fast movers with less precision than slow ones; fast movers lose precision exactly where it isn't seen). This is what keeps the synchronous update part cheap enough to live on the main loop's cadence tick (§8.2.2).
- Draw pipeline: regime selection by screenSize/distance thresholds (§ constants ModularBody.hpp:80-93) → dispatch to the appropriate component list (substitution point, per D4) → halo fallback below full-visibility threshold (common case, per D3).
- Slot model: `components[StringID]` is the **ownership** home (unique per slot, replacement erases the old module's routing) [observed: ModularBody.hpp:611-622]. The four regime lists are **routing** only (raw pointers, no ownership).
- Loading contract (order is load-bearing): `ModuleLoader::load()` routes the new module into regime lists via `add*Component`; `ModuleLoaderMgr::loadModule` then installs ownership into the slot; `slot()` erases only the *replaced* module's routing [observed: ModuleLoaderMgr.cpp:59-64, BasicMeshLoader.cpp:46]. Now stated in ModuleLoader.hpp/ModularBody.hpp headers (2026-07-11) per I1.

### 3.3 `ModularSystem` — coordinate and sorting root
- A `ModularBody` with `isNotIsolated = false` (update/draw not transmitted upward) [observed: ModularSystem.cpp:44, ModularBody.hpp:739].
- Owns: the distance-sorted flat body list of its subtree (frame-coherent resort ← D3), the star (light source) designation, and body loading from `ssystem.ini`-format data.
- Hardcoded content is only ever introduced through `applyHardcodedContent` and only when the data says `hardcoded=true` [observed: ModularSystem.cpp:195-196].

### 3.4 `ModuleLoader` / `ModuleLoaderMgr` / `modules.cpp`
- Loaders compete via `isLikely` (0 = cannot, 255 = certain, first max wins) [observed: ModuleLoaderMgr.cpp:48-57]; a loader can identify its own products (`isLoaderOf`) for future reload/invalidations.
- All loader registration lives in `modules.cpp` exclusively — adding a module family never touches core files.
- Orbit loading mirrors the same pattern keyed by `coord_func` string with a default fallback [observed: ModuleLoaderMgr.hpp:20-28].

### 3.5 `Camera`
- Owns the observer state (reference body, view rotator, free/surface modes) and the reference-switch logic; consumes `findBetterReference`/`calculateSwitchCompensation` from bodies.
- Is the entry point for the new-path frame: `update(jd, dt)` then `draw(renderer)` drives system update, sort, and draw [observed: ssystem_factory.cpp:381-395].
- Its position relative to the current body also selects the active environment (§3.8).

### 3.6 `Renderer`
- Sole owner of command buffers, depth-buffer strategy (the four buffers stated at ModularBody.hpp:125-129), and tone-mapped halo emission. Everything else describes; only the renderer records.
- **Depth-range partitioning** ← D1/D2/D3 [vixy: 2026-07-11]: the full depth range must be *split between visible bodies according to their needs*, because no single range can hold parent-scale distances and child-scale details (D1/D2), and only a handful of bodies need a bucket at any time (D3). `notableBody` is the designed input: the per-frame list of bodies large enough on screen to need a depth bucket [stated: ModularBody.hpp:756; vixy: 2026-07-11]. The consumer is unwritten (§5.3); when written, it must also drain/clear the list each frame.

### 3.7 `ModularBodyPtr` / `ModularBodySelector`
- Long-lived references must use `ModularBodyPtr` (registry + `redirect` on removal); short-lived ones use raw pointers within a frame [stated: ModularBodyPtr.hpp:9-10]. Selection side-effects (pointer count, isSelected) go through the friend classes only.

### 3.8 `EnvironmentModule`
- Scope [vixy: 2026-07-11]: **everything "outside" whose rendering depends on the camera's location relative to the body** — milkyway (earth-centered 2D version vs 3D version), atmosphere, landscape, and so on.
- Structure already reserved for it: grounded environment takes priority over InAoI environment [stated: ModularBody.hpp:107-108]; `enterEnvironment()`/`leaveEnvironment()` are the (currently empty) transition hooks [observed: ModularBody.hpp:576-579].
- Note the propagation: the milkyway 2D/3D switch being an environment concern means "backdrop vs world" is a *camera-relative state*, not a property of the object itself — consistent with G2 (the galaxy is a body like any other; only its rendering mode depends on where you stand).
- Convergence updates [vixy: 2026-07-11, in-tree annotations in EnvironmentModule.hpp + plan review]: visibility gating (the old BodyDecor questions) is **aggregated by the system**; simultaneous environments draw in **fixed per-category order** (landscape, milkyway 2D/3D, atmosphere-from-ground, …) — a frame-sequencing input to the Renderer, distinct from the batch occlusion contract (§10.3 decision 6).

### 3.9 Header hygiene targets (applies module-wide)
- Every contract currently discoverable only from implementations gets written into the header owning it (I1). Done 2026-07-11: loader routing order (§3.2), `update`-before-`compare` (§3.1), `findBody` miss-is-exceptional (§5.6). Still pending: `halfFov` must-set-before-update [stated but easy to miss: ModularBody.hpp:525-526].
- `TEXMAP*`/`TEX` macros are texture utilities, not body concepts — wrong home in ModularBody.hpp [observed: ModularBody.hpp:19-30]; move next to `s_texture`/big-texture machinery (I2).
- Debug I/O has no place in headers (currently in `preUpdate`).

## 5. Defects — status tracked, none silently dropped

1. **`BMT_BASIC_SELF_SHADOW == BMT_RGBA8_SELF_SHADOW` bit collision** — **FIXED** by Vixy (renumbered 0x20/0x40/…/0x200); verified in tree 2026-07-11 [observed: BodyModule.hpp:51-56].
2. **`altitudeRelativeToRadius = Utility::isFalse(param["solid"])`** — **LITERAL-STRING BUG FIXED by Vixy** [vixy 2026-07-17: "corrected already for long"]; **verified in source 2026-07-17**: reads `param["solid"]` [observed: ModularSystem.cpp:471]. This entry's prior text was STALE (claimed the literal-string form still live) — the doc was the unreliable source, not the code; my 2026-07-17 critical-path report carried the stale claim forward from here (FM-2, doc→report propagation: a tracker entry is a cached conclusion and needs the same re-verification as any other).
   **~~Residual observation~~ REFUTED + RESOLVED (2026-07-20)** — kept in full because the refutation is the instructive part.
   *The 2026-07-17 claim was*: `solid` is declared by no shipped body and read by only this line → `param["solid"]` == `""` → `isFalse("")` hits `default: return false` [observed: utility.hpp:171-182] → `altitudeRelativeToRadius == false` for every shipped body → `getAltitudeReference()` returns `0` not `scaledRadius` → **observer altitude measured from body CENTER, not surface**. It closed with "either the scenes don't reach this path or the reference cancels; unresolved, do not assume benign."
   **It was neither. The chain has a broken link in its MIDDLE, and the claim is false at runtime** [measured 2026-07-20, by reading the ctor]: `ModularBody::ModularBody(ModularBody*, ModularBodyCreateInfo&)` **never copies `info.altitudeRelativeToRadius`** — the member-init list stops at `bodyType`/`isHaloEnabled` and the ctor body never assigns it [observed: ModularBody.cpp:54-88]. So the member keeps its in-class default **`= true`** [observed: ModularBody.hpp:1303] for **every body unconditionally**, and `ModularSystem.cpp:730` computes a value into a createInfo field **that has no reader** — which is exactly what the field's own `// Deprecated` mark [observed: ModularBody.hpp:81] was recording. Runtime behavior today = **surface-relative altitude for every body**, which is why every landing scene measured green (§11.16 Earth-100 m/50 km, §11.36 scene E's `set home_planet` + `moveto` landing at R_mars+100 m *exactly* — that assertion is only satisfiable with `altitudeRelativeToRadius == true`, and it was already sitting in the tracker as the counter-evidence this entry did not reconcile against).
   **Method failure named (verification-surface reachability, one layer in from §11.46's)**: the 2026-07-17 pass verified the *source* end of the chain (the key is read, `isFalse("")` is false) and the *sink* end (`getAltitudeReference` branches on the member, consumers subtract it) and inferred the middle. Data→expression and member→consumer were both mapped; **expression→member never was**. A value's path is not verified by verifying its endpoints — the FM-9 form of it: a chain audited at both ends is *not* an audited chain.
   **INTENT RESOLVED [vixy: 2026-07-20]**, verbatim: *"it depends on body. In the original state, altitude was from surface, but in some cases we wanted altitude from center. In fact, some scripts did create two bodies, the wanted one, and another one to anchor and go inside the wanted one. This is a patch over a system which should have handled both cases gracefully in scripts which is why a flag should exists."* Answers the three questions: **(a) absent-key default = surface-relative** (`true`) — the original state, and what immutable scripts (§2(b)) depend on; today's accidental behavior is already correct, so **there is no behavioral divergence to fix** (see §11.6 correction); **(b) polarity is INVERTED in the dead expression** — the use case is *going inside* a body, so `solid ⇒ surface-relative` and `solid = false ⇒ center-relative`, whereas `isFalse(param["solid"])` yields surface-relative for `solid = false`. Correct form is the house idiom already used two lines below for `hardcoded` (absent → parity default, explicit `false` blocks): `!Utility::isFalse(param["solid"])`. **[polarity is DERIVED from the key's name + the stated go-inside use case, not stated verbatim by Vixy — the one inference in this entry, flagged for sign-off]**; **(c) no data catch-up needed** — every shipped body is solid; the key exists for *script-created* bodies (`body action load` feeds the same param map), which is precisely where the two-body patch lives. **The `// Deprecated` mark on the createInfo field is now contradicted** — the field is not deprecated, it is unwired; the flag is R1-additive (§2(a2): parameterises, forecloses nothing) and its purpose is to retire a patch that exists in user scripts today. Implementation → §13.B.
   **What the staged "fix" would have done** (why this entry mattered): landing §11.6's pending change as staged — wiring the expression through *without* correcting polarity — flips **every shipped body** to center-relative, i.e. `moveto altitude 100` lands 100 m from the planet's core and every landscape/atmosphere threshold shifts by one body radius. The trap was that both halves (unwired field, inverted polarity) are individually invisible and cancel to correct behavior; fixing either one alone breaks it.
   **MECHANISM SUPERSEDED — the flag is to be RETIRED, not fixed [vixy: 2026-07-20]**, verbatim: *"I remember why I marked it deprecated, it was because I added a different field with the intent to separate navigation surface radius to body surface radius, which made the flag redundant. A non-solid is something with a null surface radius, in some ways."* So the `// Deprecated` mark was **correct and load-bearing**, not stale — the previous paragraph's "the field is not deprecated, it is unwired" is itself now corrected: it is deprecated *because* a better mechanism was chosen, and merely not yet landed. **The successor is a per-body NAVIGATION SURFACE RADIUS, distinct from the body/render radius** — found in the tree as drafting residue, commented out at both ends [observed: `//float scaledInnerRadius;` ModularBody.hpp:1259; `//.innerRadius=Utility::strToFloat(param["min_distance"], radius*1.002f)/...` ModularSystem.cpp:721]. `getAltitudeReference()` collapses to `return scaledInnerRadius;` — **no flag, no branch** (I2/I4: one value, one authority); solid = nav radius at the surface, "non-solid" = nav radius 0 ⇒ altitude from centre, which is Vixy's "null surface radius". **Why it beats the boolean, under §2(a2)**: the boolean is a two-valued *identity* (solid / not) and forecloses every intermediate; the radius is a *parameter* and additionally covers the cases neither value enumerates — a gas giant whose navigable "surface" is the cloud deck below 1·r, a heightmapped body whose safe floor is above 1·r, a shell enterable to a given depth. Same single value, strictly more space. **The boolean's polarity question (b) is therefore MOOT** — the expression is deleted, not corrected; only the *default* survives as a live question, and it is now a scalar, not a polarity.
   **The navigation law is the third consumer, and it constrains the default [vixy: 2026-07-20]**, verbatim: *"The script navigate naturally by applying a multiplier on the current altitude, for joystick navigation. This is why base altitude is set to 1km by default, even though it should be more clean to set it to 0km or better, to implement the surface mode when close. […] In free movement, the velocity scale with the distance to min_radius and in free movement only it should be clamped. The other is by natural scaling."* Both laws are already in the tree and **already key on the altitude reference**, so landing the nav radius wires them for free [observed: Camera.hpp:74-105]: anchored/natural = `multAlt(coef)` → `coef = distanceToReference()*(coef-1)` (a *multiplier on current altitude* — Vixy's word "natural"); free = `moveRelLon/Lat` → `lon *= distanceToReference() * 5.f`, i.e. **velocity ∝ (distance − min_radius)**, exactly as stated. `distanceToReference()` is `distance − reference->getAltitudeReference()` [observed: Camera.cpp:24-27], so ONE per-body scalar drives: altitude zero-point, natural-navigation step, free-movement velocity, landscape/atmosphere thresholds, and (to add) the free-movement clamp. That is the I2 argument for the scalar, measured rather than asserted.
   **Consequence — altitude 0 is a FIXED POINT of the natural law, and that is what the 1 km base altitude patches**: at altitude 0 `distanceToReference() == 0` ⇒ `multAlt` multiplies zero ⇒ joystick altitude control is dead at the surface. The configured init altitude is non-zero for exactly this reason [observed: sandbox `[init_location] altitude = 75`; vixy cites 1 km as the usual default]. **So the default nav radius must be `radius` exactly (altitude 0 AT the surface) AND the non-zero base altitude must stay until surface mode lands** — the clean replacement is not "set it to 0", it is the near-surface draw regime [vixy], which owns a navigation law where altitude 0 is a legitimate resting state. B10 must therefore NOT remove the base-altitude workaround; that retires with §12 row 16 (D4), not with the nav radius. Note the same law explains the non-solid case with no special-casing: with min_radius = 0, velocity ∝ distance-from-centre, so the observer approaches the centre asymptotically — "can't cross a solid surface" and "can descend inside a hollow one" are the *same* law under two radii, which is the sharp form of why the scalar beats the boolean.
   **Clamp scope RESOLVED [vixy 2026-07-20]: free movement ONLY.** Anchored/natural navigation needs no clamp — the asymptote is the clamp. Answers the (ii) question below.
   **(4) CONFIRMED + a new requirement [vixy: 2026-07-20]**: *"The multiplier should be on the proximity factor, so either relative to the true center or relative to the closest position, which make ground_radius the best candidate. With an anti-stuck mechanism to prevent being stuck and unable to takeoff when setting the height to 0 (an issue some users genuinely had and reported)."* So `multAlt` keys on `ground_radius` — the derived answer stands, on a better reason than the one I gave: the multiplier expresses *proximity*, and proximity is measured to the closest reachable position (centre when `ground_radius == 0`), not to the measurement origin. **The anti-stuck requirement is empirical — a reported user defect, now §5.18** — and it changes the fix's shape twice: (i) **the split alone does NOT fix it.** With the defaults (`datum == ground == radius`), height 0 puts the observer exactly at `ground_radius`, so the proximity factor is 0 and the multiplicative law has nothing to multiply — the reported bug survives the whole redesign unless an explicit floor lands with it; (ii) **it is not only takeoff.** All three interactive movers read the same zero — `multAlt` (radial) *and* `moveRelLon`/`moveRelLat` (free-mode lateral, `*= distanceToReference() * 5.f`) — so at proximity 0 the observer cannot move in ANY direction, not merely up. That makes the floor a property of **one shared proximity-factor authority** (I2 — Vixy's own phrasing, "the multiplier should be on the proximity factor"), consumed by all three, never three separate guards.
   **The anti-stuck constant ALSO already exists, unwired** [measured 2026-07-20]: `constexpr double MIN_MOVEMENT_SPEED = 0.125; //! Minimal speed while under the area of influence of a body, in body_radius/s` [observed: ModularBody.hpp:90-91], **zero consumers in the whole tree**. Its units are the right ones — relative to `radius`, which stays defined when `ground_radius == 0` (an epsilon relative to `ground_radius` would vanish exactly in the enterable-body case, i.e. at a body's centre, so radius-relative is forced). **But its VALUE needs a decision, not just wiring**: 0.125 body_radius/s is ≈797 km/s at Earth's surface, which reads as an AoI-traversal floor (don't crawl across a sphere of influence), not as a ground-scale anti-stuck epsilon. Those are plausibly two different constants for two different purposes; an anti-stuck floor around 1e-6·radius (≈6 m on Earth) escapes geometrically within a second or two without making surface navigation unusable. **Do not adopt 0.125 by default — it is unverified intent, like the other two artifacts in this same header.**
   **Pattern worth naming (three for three)**: `altitudeRelativeToRadius` (unwired — ctor never copies it), `scaledInnerRadius` (commented out), `MIN_MOVEMENT_SPEED` (declared, no consumers). This corner of the header is a design that was thought through and never landed, which is exactly why reading it as *current behavior* produced a wrong conclusion three times in this entry's history. Header-as-thinking-medium (§6.2 [vixy: 2026-07-11]) has this cost: intent and implementation are indistinguishable by inspection, so anything read here needs its consumer verified before it is treated as live.
   **FINAL SHAPE — TWO scalars, not one; names CONFIRMED [vixy: 2026-07-20]**: *"The two as separate is probably the best thing to choose and the datum_radius can default to radius, same for ground_radius, which preserve backward compatibility and enable more flexibility in the boundaries, exactly what I was looking for structurally, in fact. I just didn't know where to cut exactly."* → **`datum_radius`** = the radius altitude is measured FROM (role i); **`ground_radius`** = the radius you cannot get closer than (role ii); **both default to `radius`** ⇒ every shipped body and every legacy script is bit-identical to today, since today's single reference is `scaledRadius` and both roles collapse onto it. The naming difficulty was the diagnostic, not the obstacle: two roles resisted one name because they are two values (limits-as-design-data — the limit was in the relation, not in the candidates).
   **What the split buys, by case** (the argument for it, re-derivable): (a) solid planet — datum=ground=radius, today's behavior exactly; (b) enterable body (the two-body-patch replacement) — datum=0, ground=0: altitude reads distance-from-centre, descent to the centre allowed; (c) **terrain clearance — datum=radius, ground=radius·1.002**: altitudes stay legacy-exact while free flight cannot be driven into a mountain. **This retroactively resolves finding (i) below**: the drafted `radius*1.002f` was not a wrong number, it was the right number *assigned to the wrong role* — as a datum it broke `limLandscape` on Earth, as a ground radius it costs nothing and was what the draft wanted all along; (d) gas giant — datum at the cloud deck (altitude reads height above cloud tops, negative below), ground at 0 or a depth limit. Case (d) is only expressible BECAUSE the two are independent — a single value cannot put the measurement origin above the floor.
   **Consumer assignment [(1)-(3) follow from the definitions; (4) is DERIVED — flagged, not confirmed]**: (1) `getAltitudeReference()` → `datum_radius`, feeding `moveto altitude`, the altitude readout, and the landscape/atmosphere `lim*` thresholds; (2) the free-mode descent clamp → `ground_radius`; (3) free-mode velocity scaling → `ground_radius` (Vixy's own words attach it to the floor: *"the velocity scale with the distance to min_radius"*, min_radius being the floor concept at that point in the conversation); (4) **proposed**: the anchored/natural `multAlt` base → `ground_radius` too, NOT the datum. Reason: `multAlt` multiplies its base, so its zero is a fixed point and its sign flip is a control reversal — keyed on the datum it degenerates in case (d) (below the cloud deck, altitude goes negative and the joystick reverses), keyed on the ground it asymptotes at the floor and can never cross it. Consequence worth having: in anchored mode this gives a *soft* floor (the joystick can't reach the ground) while `moveto altitude -X` still can — safety on the interactive path with no capability foreclosed (§2(a2)). Under (a) all four collapse to today's single value, so this is regression-free by construction and only observable once the two radii differ. **Sign-off wanted on (4) before landing.**
   **Two findings the successor must carry** [measured 2026-07-20]: (i) [**superseded by the split above — the number was right, the role was wrong; kept because it is what exposed the two-role structure**] **the drafted default `radius*1.002f` is NOT legacy-compatible as an altitude zero-point** — it puts `moveto altitude 0` at 0.002·R above the surface, i.e. **12.76 km on Earth**, which is *above* `limLandscape = 10000 m` [observed: EnvironmentModule.hpp:64] ⇒ the landscape would stop showing at altitude 0 on Earth. If the nav radius is the altitude zero-point, its default must be `radius` exactly (scene E's R_mars+100 m assertion is the same constraint). The 1.002 margin only makes sense as a *separate* minimum-approach/descent floor — plausibly what `min_distance` was named for; (ii) **there is no descent floor today** — no clamp on `distance` anywhere in Camera [measured: `Camera.cpp` sites 228/383/402/601, no min/max/clamp], so nothing currently prevents descending below any surface. That means the nav radius today affects the altitude *zero-point only*, and whether it should *also* become a floor is a live design question, not an implied consequence. The two-body script patch is thereby explained precisely: going inside was always physically possible via negative altitude; what was missing was a *sane script surface* for expressing it without knowing R.
3. **`notableBody` grows unboundedly** — **FULLY RESOLVED (consumer live 2026-07-16, §11.30; drain resolved D4 2026-07-11)**. Purpose: input to the Renderer's depth-range splitting (§3.6) [vixy]. Lifecycle: cleared at `dispatchUpdate` start (every frame, whichever path draws — a draw-side-only drain was unbounded during old-path toggle phases), filled by update(), consumed by `Renderer::beginDraw` (bucket merge + orbit union, §11.30).
4. **Debug artifacts in hot/committed paths** — **CLOSED (2026-07-19, §11.45 tracker hygiene)** [measured: grep `std::cout` in ModularBody.{hpp,cpp} = 0; `#ifndef NDEBUG`/auto-toggle = 0; `Body::computeDraw`/`applyHardcodedContent` cout = 0; git status shows no experimentalModule/bodyModule debug modifications — the working tree is clean of these instruments]. Were: `std::cout` in inline `preUpdate` (header), in `Body::computeDraw`, in `applyHardcodedContent`; `#ifndef NDEBUG` removed from the auto-toggle — all reverted (the intervening waves added+reverted their own debug byte-identically, T5/T6 notes).
5. **Hardcoding keyed on `englishName`** — **AUDITED + KEY-DESIGNED (2026-07-22, §11.73 = B27 steps 1–2); emit/consume → B25/B24**. Any body named "Moon"/"Earth" with `hardcoded=true` becomes EARTH/EARTH_MOON; TODO exists [stated: ModularObject.cpp:68]. Full site inventory (all six hardcoding classes, whole-tree grep) + per-site §2(a2) capability key in §11.73: Earth apparent-sidereal-time → NEW `sidereal_time` selector; Earth shadow default → EXISTING `shadow_color`; EARTH_MOON → RETIRE (0 new-path consumers); Sun day-length → `isStar()`. Exemption = `*_special` orbits. No product code changed here (consume co-delivers with B25's emit).
6. **`findBody` exception on miss** — **RECLASSIFIED: not a defect — undocumented expectation (now documented)**. Misses are exceptional *by design*: callers look up names they expect to exist; the exception fires only for absent bodies [vixy: 2026-07-11]. Header comment added 2026-07-11 per I1. Residual nit: `catch (...)` could be `catch (std::out_of_range)` for precision; equivalent in practice here.
7. **`DedicatedBodyModuleSlot` is dead** — **RESOLVED** (removed, Phase B 2026-07-11; StringID slots are the slot identity, BodyModuleType the loader-family selector — both documented).
8. **`BodyModule::loaded`** — **RESOLVED** (removed from the base, Phase B 2026-07-11: module-internal state, moved into BasicMesh; `isLoaded()` is the contract).
9. **`boundingRadius` read-before-write hazard** — **RESOLVED** (ordering contract + zero-initialization, Phase B 2026-07-11).
10. **Chain translation signs inverted** — **FIXED (2026-07-11, triplet resolution — §11.15)**. `transformParentToBodyPos` subtracted ecl going down and added going up: every body point-reflected through the reference (measured: eye_root_new == −eye_root_old; the Moon-behind-observer effect; the `[-Y,X,Z]` note's real ancestor). Now +ecl down / −ecl up, bound folds exact inverses.
11. **Per-hop tilts contradicted the ephemeris frame** — **FIXED**. Ephemeris outputs (VSOP87/ELP82/ell-orbit-with-baked-parent-rotation) are root-aligned vectors; the chain applied `computeBodyPosToBody` per hop, so children translated in the parent's tilted frame (the confirmed E2 class). Now: flat chain, tilt decorates only the body's own `mat`; frame contract written in ModularBody.hpp.
12. **Up-hop rotation was not the inverse of the down tilt** — **FIXED** (subsumed by 11; kept for the mechanism). `zxrotation(prec−node, −obliq)` = the order-swapped FORWARD tilt: zxrotation(z,x) is already the transpose of xzrotation(x,z) at the same angles, so negating the angles double-inverts. `computeBodyToBodyPos` is the documented exact inverse; measured |U·D−I| = 0.
13. **`BinaryOrbit::secondary` never wired in the new path (EMB)** — **FIXED**. Earth sat at the Earth–Moon barycenter (inter-path Earth delta = −ratio·moon_ecl, 4-digit magnitude match). Generic mechanism: the orbit declares its secondary's name (`binary_secondary` param; EarthOrbitLoader defaults "Moon"), `loadBody` wires it, `~ModularBody` unwires (I5). After fix: |ecl_old−ecl_new| ≈ 3e-8.
14. **`hide()` hid the parent's FIRST child instead of `this`** — **FIXED** (missing `&*it == this` test that `show()` has). With standard data this froze Mercury→Neptune + Mars + Ceres…Vesta (each `hidden=true` body hid an earlier sibling: Ceres→Mercury, Juno→Earth, Vesta→Mars, …) — the real mechanism behind §11.14b(vii) "Mars gets nothing".
15. **Camera surface-placement longitude sign** — **FIXED**. zrot(+lon) placed the observer at azimuth axisRot − lon; old (validated exact against its own composition) is sidereal + lon. East-positive now; `setBoundToSurface` transitions were already consistent with the flipped sign. Earth's spin source fixed via the `hardcoded` default (absent key → old-parity name-keying; explicit false still blocks): the generic rot formula was −49.01° off apparent sidereal.
16. **Light-travel-time asymmetry** — **FIXED**. Old retards each body by |body−observer|·(AU/c/day) (solarsystem_display.cpp computePositions); new evaluated at coordinate time (Mars: 1.7e-4 AU tangential input delta). `ModularBody::flagLightTravelTime`, dual-routed from `SSystemFactory::setFlagLightTravelTime`, same previous-frame-distance semantics. Its first run exposed an **uninitialized `distance` member** (garbage → NaN jd → non-converging Kepler solve → frozen main loop); now `= 0`, matching old's zero-initialized first frame.
17. **`isSystemCentered()` ancestor positions never examined** — **FIXED (2026-07-17, §11.35)**. The loop tested `this->eclipticPos` at every level (missing `body->` — loop-invariant condition), so "at the center of his system" degenerated to "own ecl==0 AND chain tops at a SYSTEM": an ecl==0 body under an OFF-CENTER parent read as system-centered. Reachable: script-declared constructs parked at a planet center (`orbit_semimajoraxis = 0`, the §2 `[Solar_System]` pattern under a planet); consequence pre-fix: such a body and its satellites lost ancestor-tilt participation in the accumulated walks. Found by reading during the §11.35 consolidation (the walk consumes the predicate); shipped bodies unaffected (the only ecl==0 bodies sit under the Sun, whose ecl is also 0 — the bug was masked by exactly the case it was written for).

18. **Observer stuck at height 0, unable to take off** — **MECHANISM FIXED by B10 (2026-07-22, §11.71); floor VALUE suspended for Vixy.** The shared `Camera::proximityFactor(escaping)` authority floors the outward/lateral step to `ANTISTUCK_ESCAPE_FLOOR·scaledRadius` (≈6.4 m on Earth, a placeholder), consumed by all three movers — at height 0, `proximityFactor(false)=0` (the old fixed point) but `proximityFactor(true)>0` ⇒ escapable in every direction [measured: 6.38 m floor, 0.638 m/multAlt(1.1) step vs 0]. NB the live keyboard path is not yet exercised — `Camera::multAlt` is unwired (0 callers) and moveRelLon/Lat reach only UI keys; the fix is correct for when they run. Original entry (user-reported [vixy 2026-07-20: "an issue some users genuinely had and reported"]; rode B10, §5.2): Mechanism: the interactive movers are multiplicative on the proximity factor, which is zero at height 0 — `multAlt` [observed: Camera.hpp:99-105, `coef = distanceToReference()*(coef-1)`] and, in free mode, `moveRelLon`/`moveRelLat` [observed: Camera.hpp:74-89, `*= distanceToReference() * 5.f`]. Zero times anything is zero, so the state is a **fixed point in every direction, not only upward** — the report says "can't take off"; the observer also can't move laterally. Reachable two ways: a script/config setting height exactly 0, and repeated descent multiplying the factor down to float underflow. **Not fixed by the datum/ground split alone** (with the defaults, height 0 sits exactly at `ground_radius` ⇒ factor still 0): it needs an explicit floor in the shared proximity-factor authority. Recorded here because until now this defect's only home was a sentence in conversation — it had no traced entry, and the workaround (the non-zero base altitude, §5.2) was in the config where nothing points back to the defect it patches.

19. **RA/DE convention divergence at the ModularObject surface** — **OPEN, out-of-scope of B9 (found by it, 2026-07-22, §11.60(h))**. The RA/DE analog of the azimuth divergence B9 fixed: `ModularObject::getRaDeValue`/`getInfoString`/`getShortInfoNavString` read `Camera::observedPosToRaDe(body->getObservedPosition())`, which disagrees with the old path's `Body::getRaDeValue` (`Utility::rectToSphe(getEarthEquPos)`). Measured on the same frame [harness/artifacts/b9_postfix/*.navstr]: Moon **old `RA 04h57m15s / +26°41'47"`** vs **new `03h44m18s / +14°40'05"`** (~18° in RA, ~12° in DE) — NOT a small residual, a frame/convention delta (candidate: J2000 vs of-date, or an observer-frame rotation). Feeds `getSelectedRA/DE` and the SA/GHA/LHA nav fields (which are all derived from the RA). **Invisible today for the same reason as B9**: ModularObject is uninstantiated in production (§11.60(g)), so `getSelectedRA/DE` currently uses the old `Body`. NOT fixed here — B9's DoD is azimuth only, and the single-authority discipline forbids improvising a second convention. Needs its own probe (does the delta hold clean across bodies/states, like az did?) then a single conversion authority in the same idiom as `ModularObject::altAz()`. A natural next-task companion to B9.
