    - **(c) Answers that exceed their question — new items, recorded rather than absorbed**: (1) **Q22 → A30 (new)**: the tester does not want "a star with a surface", they want *"to select a star and get there by switching to the galaxy mode and then to the stellar_system mode (if existing or **created from scratch** if there is no specific one)"* — i.e. star-system **reachability + default generation**, which is a §6.9-scale capability, not the A7 render family; logged in `FEATURE_REQUESTS.md` (first entry, the channel's first real exercise) with A30 as its tracker row and the scope call left to Vixy. (2) **Q5 → B4**: anchors are edited by hand *and* created from scripts, cross-session persistence **not** needed — but *"it could be useful to have a command that saves it in the anchor.ini"*; logged as the second request, folded into B4's design rather than promised. (3) **Q12 → B10**: the two-body patch **is** in live scripts (§2(b): the strongest possible confirmation the replacement must be complete), and the command surface is requested as **`radius ground` / `radius datum`** — which raises an I2 question the answer does not settle: whether the *data keys* (`ground_radius`/`datum_radius`) should be re-spelled to the same word order so there is one vocabulary. (4) **Q6 → B10(iv-b)**: *"When we are close to the surface, we know where we are and we have to stop coming down as it doesn't move"* — the anti-stuck defect described as a **learned workaround**, i.e. it has been lived with rather than reported; that is the signature of a defect class that never generates a bug report, and it is the second independent confirmation of §5.18.
    - **(d) The questions' own failures — carried, not tidied**: **A28/Q17 was the one question that did not survive contact**: the tester answered *"Unclear question. Is eclipse shadows the shadows casted on a body? And ring shadows is the size of the shadow of Earth at Moon distance?"* — the second guess is wrong (ring shadow = a ring system casting onto its planet and the planet onto its rings, i.e. Saturn), so the answer that followed it (*"separated, ring default off, eclipse default on"*) is **built on a wrong referent and is not usable for the ring half**; only "must be separated" and "eclipse on" survive. Root: the question used the engine's internal vocabulary (`ring_shadow`) as if it were domain vocabulary, in a document whose stated precondition is *implementation knowledge NOT assumed* — a precondition the document stated and then violated in one item. Structural consequence for round 2: every question naming an engine concept must name the **observable** instead (what is on screen, on which body, when). Second failure, milder: Q1 offered two cases and two options per case with no labels distinct from the case letters, which is what made A9 unresolvable — **an answer's ambiguity is the question's defect**. And: the tester asked two questions *back*; answering them is part of round 2, not optional (a companion file that only asks is a closed channel — the same self-imposed blind spot as any one-way protocol).
    - **(e) Status changes to already-landed work, and the one thing that did not change**: the scene-E `e_in` first-run miss is reclassified from documented-flap to defect-with-known-cause (A19/B15). §11.41's invisible-tick verification stays correct and is **not** contradicted by A1: A10 (body hidden → keeps updating, so its trail keeps recording) and A1 (trail flag off → stop recording, restart fresh) are answers about two different gates, and the adjacent-looking pair is recorded here precisely because reading them as one would produce a wrong implementation. Nothing in this round invalidates a landed port; three answers (Q25 20% threshold, Q19 moon generalisation, Q7 escalation policy) **ratify** landed behavior that had been carrying "suspended" status, which is the cheapest possible outcome and the reason the round was worth its latency.

49. **Vixy round-2 decisions on the §11.48 escalation (2026-07-21, same day).** Four answers; two close rows, one states intent that was *planned but never formulated*, one sends a question back for context. Each recorded with what was verified rather than accepted.
    - **(a) A9 CLOSED — and it closes with ZERO code [vixy 2026-07-21, verified in source before recording]**: *"drop-while-tracked → follow parent, drop-while-anchored → parent, that's already handled by the ModularBodyPtr transparently."* **Verified**: `~ModularBody` calls `ModularBodyPtr::redirect(this, parent)` when `pointerCount` is non-zero [observed: ModularBody.cpp:151], `redirect` rewrites **every** registered pointer from→to [ModularBodyPtr.cpp:32-45], and Camera holds BOTH roles as such pointers — `ModularBodyPtr reference; ModularBodyPtr target;` [observed: Camera.hpp:246-247]. So both roles already land on the parent. **The §11.45(c) blocker dissolves rather than resolves**: it was *"the uniform redirect can't distinguish target from reference without structure"* — true, and irrelevant once both roles want the same destination. **The structure was never needed; the question was.** Class worth keeping: a blocker stated as a *mechanism* limitation ("can't distinguish") that was really an *unknown-intent* blocker — the mechanism only had to distinguish if the answers differed. **One unasked-for observable rides the same call and is named here rather than discovered later**: `redirect` also transfers *selection* (`from->deselect(); to->select()` [ModularBodyPtr.cpp:34-38]), so dropping a selected moon leaves its **planet selected**. Consistent with the answer's direction, but nobody asked for it — flagged, not silently absorbed.
    - **(b) A30 RESOLVED as stated intent, not as a feature request [vixy 2026-07-21]**: *"This was planned but not well formulated."* The design, verbatim in substance: **everything behaves as-if it were a ModularBody, and only 3D stars are candidates** — the as-if is the whole point, because making them all real ModularBody instances *"would be too expensive"*. Two populations: (i) **authored systems** — declared in stellar-system files, *"the same way the stellar system is, but with a different file for each"*, **already instantiated** at load, so several systems coexist and the solar system stops being privileged; the system is *"defined by the sun OR some body managing the system itself if needed which hold the sun as child body or as grounded body, to decide depending on constraints"*. (ii) **everything else** — stays a 3D star and **dynamically becomes a ModularSystem on need**, *"with the same visual as the 3D star and the same behavior"*; with no matching file, that system is *"only the star focused with the color it has in the 3D star description, nothing more."* **What this settles that the tester's phrasing did not**: "created from scratch" means *reachable*, NOT *fabricated* — no invented planets; the generated system's entire content is the star itself, coloured from the catalogue entry. **What it forces**: promotion must be **observationally invisible** (same visual, same behavior) — that is a verification obligation of exactly the §11.19-class ("the terminal observable is the screen"), and it is the same continuity requirement as B22's cross-fade at the resolved↔dot boundary, one level up. Direct feed to B5/§6.9: this IS the star half of the executor-mode dissolution, so the two must be designed together, not sequenced. **Open sub-decisions, listed so they are not improvised at implementation** (moved to A30's row): the system-root shape (star-as-root vs container-holding-star, *"to decide depending on constraints"* — a derivation with sign-off, not a free choice); the promotion trigger and whether promotion is reversible or sticky (lifetime of a promoted system the observer has left); and the **file↔star matching key** — matching by name is the §2(a2)/A29 identity-keyed hazard arriving in a new place, so the key must be catalogue-stable (HIP-class), decided before the first file exists rather than after shows depend on it.
    - **(c) A2 — resolution channel moved, not resolved [vixy 2026-07-21]**: *"I'll look at it in the USER_QUESTIONS.md and resolve it directly (with direct conversation with the tester if needed), in the meantime the other points can progress."* The conflict (tester's *"if it's not in the data it will never be activated ever"* vs §2(c)'s every-capability-dynamically-reachable) stays **suspended**, and correctly so — it is a divergence between two authorities, and the resolution is convergence between them, not a third party's arbitration. Nothing downstream of A2 is started; everything not downstream proceeds.
    - **(d) A28 — the question came back a second time, and that is the signal** [vixy: *"I am not certain to exactly grasp what you meant… more context is needed for me to answer it better"*]. Two rounds, two parties, same item: the defect is in **how the item is presented**, not in either reader. Root, and it is the same one as §11.48(d) one level up: the item has only ever been stated in the vocabulary of the flag that implements it (`experimental_shadows`, `ring_shadow`), never as *what is on screen and when*. Context supplied in this session's reply and folded into the row; the item is not "awaiting Vixy" until the presentation is fixed, because a question that has failed twice is not waiting on an answer.
    - **(e) B14's referential caveat [vixy: *"those values depend on a referential and this referential has to be the right one, otherwise good values produce invalid measurement"*] — investigated immediately, because it also tests LANDED work.** Four measured facts: (1) the loader accepts `rot_pole_ra`/`rot_pole_de` in **J2000 equatorial**, rotates by `mat_j2000_to_vsop87`, and derives `obliquity = π/2 − de`, `ascendingNode = ra + π/2` — i.e. an **absolute** ecliptic-frame declaration [observed: ModularSystem.cpp:684-702]. (2) `accumulatedBodyPosToBody` composes **parent frames**, i.e. treats a body's own declaration as **parent-relative** [observed: ModularBody.hpp:489-500]. (3) The two would contradict each other — except the walk skips any ancestor with `isSystemCentered()` [ModularBody.hpp:493], so a planet's parent factor is identity. (4) In the shipped data, **`rot_pole_ra` appears on exactly the 7 planets, and nothing else; all 65 `rot_obliquity` bodies are the rest** [measured: default_ssystem.ini]. **Therefore: the data is self-consistent today and no landed work is wrong** — absolute declarations sit exactly where the parent factor is identity-by-guard, relative ones everywhere else. **The hypothesis this session opened (planets double-counting the Sun's now-live 7.25°) is REFUTED by fact (3), and is recorded refuted rather than dropped.** **But it constrains B14 hard**: writing IAU pole RA/DE for the 28 moons would inject *absolute* values into a slot the walk reads as *parent-relative* — precisely the failure Vixy named, and it would look correct in the file. Two routes, and the choice is a data-model decision (→ new row **A31**): convert each pole into the parent's frame and write obliquity/node (data-side, hand-computed, recurs for every future body), or let the loader accept a pole declaration and do the frame conversion itself (structural, one authority, **bit-identical for the 7 existing planets** since their parent factor is identity — the single rule covers both populations with no special case). **A physical argument bears on it and is why this is not merely stylistic**: the copy-paste cluster is dominated by *irregular* moons (Nereid, Pasiphae, Carme, the Uranian irregulars), which are exactly the class **not** locked to their planet's equator — for them a parent-relative declaration is not just inconvenient, it is physically the wrong frame, while regular inner moons (Laplace-plane-locked) are naturally parent-relative. So the frame is per-body physics, not a file convention, and the parent's own precession makes a hand-computed static value correct at one epoch only.

50. **Vixy round-3: A28 dissolved, A2 resolved as TWO paths, dual-path default flipped (2026-07-21).**
    - **(a) A28 DISSOLVED, not answered — and the residual is a different question** [vixy: *"the new path won't have an experimental_shadows toggle because it's built with it as the only shadow casting system, or should be; it's only for the old path which will be removed once full feature parity"*]. The object both rounds argued about is an **experiment gate**, not a feature switch: it exists to A/B against old and retires with old, so "what should its shipping semantics be" has no new-path answer to give. **Do not over-read it into the tester's request**: they asked for *separated eclipse/ring* control, and that is about **operator show/hide**, which this answer does not address in either direction. Recorded as A28-narrowed rather than closed — silently reading "no experimental flag" as "no operator control" would delete a capability the one user who answered explicitly asked for (§2(b) class), and reading it the other way would invent one. shadow-paths D3 updated with the same distinction (a runtime display toggle is operator control, additive; the retired `ring_shadow` was an authoring gate, subtractive — §2(a2) separates them).
    - **(b) A2 RESOLVED — the conflict dissolves into TWO authoring paths [vixy 2026-07-21]**: *"two path are ideal. One using the legacy file format, another one which does the same thing and more, by composing them by modules… Legacy compat to use legacy data is required, but I think we mustn't prevent others enthusiasts from doing what they want."* **This is the correct shape of the resolution and it is worth naming why**: the conflict was tester-intent (*"if it's not in the data it will never be activated"*) vs §2(c) (every capability dynamically reachable), and both were right **about different populations** — the tester speaks for legacy data + legacy workflow, §2(c) speaks for what the engine must not foreclose. A single path had to sacrifice one; two paths sacrifice neither, and the §11.48(b) "reading boundary" question evaporates because each reading now has its own home. Legacy path = deduction-from-keys, unchanged, **required**. New path = explicit module composition, *"does the same thing and more"* — this is the ex-§6.7 D2 grammar, no longer optional and no longer a general-grammar-for-its-own-sake (its justification is now a stated population: people who wanted to do things the legacy format could not express *reliably*).
      **The migration mechanism, and it is more than pedagogy** [vixy]: *"Ideally, the old file generate the new one with an extension `.disabled` so that the equivalence can be quite visible and the new model became natural to modify for new data which could never have landed by design."* Three properties in one mechanism: (i) **inert by construction** — `.disabled` is not loaded, so generating it can never change what a user sees; (ii) **self-documenting in the reader's own data** — the new model is taught by showing the user their own bodies expressed in it, which is transmission at the recipient's model rather than at the sender's; (iii) **derived, and therefore an equivalence TEST** — if loading the generated file does not reproduce the legacy load exactly, the new grammar has a gap, and that is a mechanical, always-available coverage check over the whole shipped corpus (a stronger verification surface than any hand-written case list, and it costs nothing extra once generation exists). **Open sub-decisions → A32**: the new format's name/extension; where generated files land (writing next to user data touches their directory); regeneration policy (every load / on source change / only when absent); and the **I2 trap** — if a legacy file and its enabled generated twin both exist, two authorities describe the same bodies, so the rule must be explicit (enabling retires the legacy file, or the loader refuses both) rather than discovered by whoever hits it first.
    - **(c) Dual-path default FLIPPED + `beta_features.ini` created [vixy 2026-07-21, implemented this session]**. Was: `drawModularSystem = false` with a 1 s auto-alternation — *a comparison harness running as the product default*, which every unconfigured launch experienced as flicker. Now: **new path, pinned, by default**; alternation is opt-in. **Where the flag lives was a real question and it is settled by a code fact, not taste**: `config.ini`'s key set is a **migration contract** — `CheckConfig::checkConfigIni` rewrites a user's config on version mismatch and injects every schema key [observed: mainModule/checkConfig.cpp:467-497] — so a key added there is expected to live forever, and retiring an experiment would either orphan keys in every user file (`checkUselessSection` is commented out) or keep dead ones. Experimental flags have the **opposite lifetime** by definition. Therefore a separate optional file: **`beta_features.ini`**, never written back, **absence == every default**, retire-by-deleting-a-line, and it doubles as the enumerable inventory of what is currently experimental. Vixy's constraint carried into the header: *a flag lands there only when the choice is actually relevant to someone*, not one per experiment by reflex.
      **Implementation** [all header-documented, I1]: `SSystemFactory::RenderPathMode{NEW,OLD,ALTERNATE}` + `setRenderPathMode` (member defaults `drawModularSystem=true`, `pathPinned=true` — the no-configuration behaviour, kept consistent with `NEW`); `AppSettings::loadBetaFeatures` returns false when the file is absent **after an explicit presence check**, because `InitParser::load` calls `exit(-1)` on a missing file [observed: tools/init_parser.cpp:54-64] — correct for `config.ini`, fatal for an optional one; `Core::setRenderPathMode(string)` **refuses an unrecognised value with a log line instead of falling back silently** (a typo'd flag must be visible, not absorbed); read in `App::init` beside the other config reads (one place where startup state comes from files). Lifetime verified: `ssystemFactory` is constructed in `Core`'s ctor (core.cpp:100), `core` in App's ctor (app.cpp:159), both before `App::init` (app.cpp:572); no other site writes either flag.
      **PRECONDITION THIS CREATES, and it is not hypothetical**: defaulting to the new path makes every remaining OLD-ONLY seam a **live regression for anyone on defaults** — and §11.48 established that two of them are *in active use by the tester who answered*: **view_offset (tilted dome geometry, B17) and the equatorial sky-lock (B18)**, plus the runtime per-name/per-body COLOR seam (A3). Under §2(b) that is realised potential being removed. **The fallback flag is the mitigation** (`render_path = old` in `beta_features.ini`) and it is exactly why the flag had to land in the same change as the default flip, not after it — but the mitigation is a fallback, not a fix: **B17/B18 are now the highest-priority implementation rows**, ahead of the rest of the round-1 batch.
      **VERIFICATION STATE — stated, not implied**: build green (exit 0, full link). **The terminal observable is NOT verified this session**: no X authorization and no Xvfb in this environment (`SDL Could not create window… video driver (offscreen)`), so "an unconfigured launch shows the new path and never alternates" and "`render_path = old|alternate` is honoured" remain **unmeasured**. They are cheap to measure (freeze time, two `body action screenshot` ≥ 2.5 s apart must be byte-identical under the default, and must differ under `alternate`) and are the first thing to run in an environment with a display. A default-behaviour change verified only by reading the code is exactly the coverage-beyond-reachability class — recorded as open, not as done.
      **[CLOSED 2026-07-21, §11.53]** — measured on a display, six fresh launches: the default pins the new path and never alternates, `alternate`/`old` are honoured, an unknown value is refused with the log line and the default kept. **Two corrections this paragraph earned**: (i) the criterion proposed here (*"two screenshots ≥ 2.5 s apart must be byte-identical under the default, and must differ under `alternate`"*) is **unsafe in its second half** — 2.5 s is a quarter of the 2 s toggle period, so it differs only 50 % of the time (§11.53(d)); use an odd multiple of 1.0 s; (ii) **"byte-identical" is false even for a correct build** — the new path is not bit-stable on a frozen scene (§11.53(e) → B30); the sound discriminator is px>32 (0 same-path vs 133…136 cross-path).

51. **Vixy round-4: six decisions (2026-07-21) — A32, A30(i), A29, A31, A6, A3.** One message, answering the blocking-question sort delivered the same day. Five rows close, one narrows; three implementation rows open (B27–B29); one §2 directive lands; one §12 plan revision. Verbatim quotes are load-bearing — re-read them before acting on any summary here.
    - **(a) A32 RESOLVED — precedence + location + ownership split** [vixy: *"The new format is always taken in priority over the legacy file (note: modular system files must be in ./modularSystem [in spacecrafter, the cwd is always set to ~/.spacecrafter]), the extension .disabled prevent it from being taken as candidate and enable a user to modify the .disabled file and drop the .disabled to use the modular system features that simply — because the .disabled is generated to ensure it is semantically equivalent to the original one. A feature which may also be used to save a system on-the-fly as well by targeting without the .disabled or under a different name from scripts, just in case."*]. Contract: (1) **precedence** — new format wins whenever both are candidates (a third option; neither "enabling retires the legacy" nor "loader refuses both" from the row's framing); (2) **location** — `~/.spacecrafter/modularSystem/` (not beside legacy user data — the row's directory concern dissolves); (3) **adoption workflow** — `.disabled` = excluded from candidacy; user edits it, drops the extension, done; (4) **the writer is dual-use** — generation at load AND on-the-fly save from scripts target the same serialization (one writer authority, I2 → folded into B25's scope). **[derived] Ownership split makes regeneration safe**: `X.disabled` is machine-owned (regenerable at will), the extension-dropped `X` is user-owned (generator never touches it) — cadence is an implementation choice bounded by the stated equivalence promise (at minimum on-source-change; every-load satisfies it trivially). **[derived, from (d)'s log directive] Shadowing must self-name**: when an enabled new-format file wins over a legacy file, log which file won, which was shadowed, and the action to fall back — otherwise a user editing the legacy file gets silence, the exact class (d) prohibits. ~~**Residual (minor, one word)**: the *enabled* format's extension/name was not specified~~ **RESOLVED [§11.52(c)]: `.ini` — twin = `.ini.disabled`; format is parsing-deep only, never capability.**
    - **(b) A30(i) RESOLVED — system node + star-as-child; star-as-root CLOSED** [vixy: *"the more stable answer is — a ModularSystem to represent the system and anything bound to a system, and a star. The declaration in the file with no parent or parent=System thus means something, for the sun in the solar system, it means that the parent of it is the system and there could be multiple such defined without conflict possible by design. The referential is clean and well-defined. So in every cases, having a system + a star in the system is preferable."*]. Recorded as a **constructed scheme**, not a casual reply (Vixy: *"I took some time to think+remember about details and construct the scheme to provide this clean answer"*). Consequences: absent-parent / `parent=System` is *meaningful grammar* (the system node), so the generator maps legacy parentless bodies cleanly; **multi-star systems are conflict-free by construction** (feeds A7 when its scope arrives); "anything bound to a system" (oort-class floor content, §6.9) is likewise a system-node child — consistent with the B5 mapping rule. **(ii) promotion trigger/reversibility and (iii) file↔star matching key remain OPEN** — untouched by this answer; row stays in §13.A, narrowed.
    - **(c) A29 CONVERTED — the enumeration source is the CODE; de-hardcode everything except orbit** [vixy: *"it can be fetched in the code, few are hardcoded but it's especially visible with the special orbit (moon_special, earth_special) which are dedicated to those bodies, and the moon have some hardcoded states which should ideally land cleanly in the modular system version. Ideally, everything hardcoded except orbit should land without hardcoding in the .disabled file generated, without regression."*]. The row stops being a Vixy-enumeration question and becomes a **code audit + capability-key design** (Fable territory → **B27**), with an acceptance criterion B25 covers mechanically: the generated `.disabled` reproduces every hardcoded behavior *without* hardcoding, no regression. **Exemption boundary**: special ORBITS stay as selectable orbit kinds — they are analytic ephemerides, not data-expressible [observed: `coord_func` values `earth_special` orbit.cpp:794, `lunar_special`/`emb_special` protosystem.cpp:569/557, `sun_special` ssystem_factory.cpp:344, `stellar_special` protosystem.cpp:107]. **Seed inventory [observed 2026-07-21 grep — NOT exhaustive; B27's first job is completing it]**: `ModularSystem::applyHardcodedContent` (ModularSystem.cpp:873 — Earth→`BodyType::EARTH`+derived shadowAbsorbtion, Moon→`EARTH_MOON`); ModularObject.cpp:71 englishName=="Sun" (the §5.5/§11-log-8 TODO — this answers its class); solarsystem.cpp:89-103 SUN/MOON/EARTH gates; body.cpp:434/606; body_moon.cpp:72 `isEarthMoon`; strToBodyType CASE("Sun",STAR)/CASE("Moon",CUSTOM_BODY) (ModularSystem.cpp:42/48). Each site → the §2(a2) test (declare the *capability*, retire the *identity*), generator emits the key for bodies that had the behavior.
    - **(d) A31 RESOLVED — loader declares BOTH frames and converts; plus a general diagnostics directive** [vixy: *"Let the loader declare both coordinate system and convert cleanly, this is more flexible and transparent to the user. The default should ideally been written into the file, when missing, for those critical information, because they determine what is possible. And when something is wrong (didn't match any), a log entry should tell which state are valid while tracing it as an error and saying what it fallback to. Logs should be written in a way that an arbitrary user looking at it could understand which actions to take in order to resolve the problems, not only what is the problem."*]. Three contents: (1) **frame declaration** — the data declares its coordinate system (absolute pole or parent-relative), the loader converts; one conversion authority; bit-identical for the 7 existing planets = the regression criterion → **B28**; B14's blocker shifts from decision to sequencing (B28 first, then IAU/WGCCRE values declared in the absolute frame). (2) **default materialization** — missing *critical* keys get their default written into the file, because they determine what is possible. **Unambiguous for generated files; AMBIGUOUS for user-owned files** — write-back into legacy ssystem.ini touches user data (the concern A32's own question named), and scripts are immutable by §2(b) so they are out by construction. ~~**Sub-question suspended for Vixy: does default-materialization write into legacy user files, or only generated ones?**~~ **RESOLVED [§11.52(a)]: user files too — under the three-clause write-back contract (only-when-needed, atomic temp+rename, whole-file clean precondition).** (3) **actionable diagnostics is a GENERAL directive**, not A31-scoped — landed as §2(f); first client is the loader's frame/enum validation.
    - **(e) A6 RESOLVED — the INSTANCED hint IS the fold; B24's grammar shape delivered in passing** [vixy: *"Yes, that's what the INSTANCED hint was intended for. It may be both autodetected AND manually definable in the .ini file describing the system. For the modular body system declaration, there is two types to declare, ModularBody and BodyModule. BodyModule refer to the ModularBody by body= and relation=."*]. (1) TAIL + row-5 RING_ASTEROID converge on **ONE Renderer instanced-batch service**; the live TAIL batch (§11.43) is the seed to *generalize, not duplicate* (I2); INSTANCED is both autodetected and manually declarable (a capability key, §2(a2)-conform). Row 5's contract exists; its remaining gate is S4 only. (2) **Grammar shape (exceeds A6 — routed to B24)**: two declarable types, `ModularBody` (the node) and `BodyModule` (the feature); a BodyModule binds via `body=` and `relation=`. Residuals at B24 design time (derive + sign off, not Vixy-blocking): the value domain of `relation=`, and the spelling of the manual INSTANCED hint.
    - **(f) A3 RESOLVED by a PARITY META-RULE + old-path lifetime EXTENDED** [vixy: *"everything in the old path shall work identically in the new path, so that the new path could transparently substitute for the old one. Both path will be kept for backward compatibility 'in emergency', to react if something happens to be broken in the new path, whenever it will be genuinely used with public."*]. Two contents: (1) **the meta-rule** — for semantic/behavioral contract questions of this class, *old's observable IS the specification*. A3's residual (runtime color across reload) becomes an observation task: measure old, reproduce → **B29**; the §11.42 color-authority suspension dissolves the same way (observable = old's global sky-manager colors; the internal home — instance members + re-bake — stays the new path's choice); the S6 runtime-COLOR seam is implementable. Tester R8 is demoted to future-preference input (annotated in USER_QUESTIONS.md, question kept). (2) **plan revision** — the old path is NOT removed after switchover: kept as emergency fallback through genuine public use. §12's removal step revised in place; every "retires with the old path" item (clouds/bodyTrace, old shadow VUID instances, `experimental_shadows` gate, B8's relocation trigger) keeps its trigger — the trigger just fires later; and keeping old BUILDING and A/B-testable is now a standing capability, not scaffolding. **Tension to confirm, reported not absorbed**: read literally, "everything identical" collides with the ratified A3-class divergence channel (§2(a) physical truth > old-pixel parity — TAIL extent, §11.24a name-on-disc, §6.8(A) itself). Proposed reading [derived, was PENDING]: the meta-rule governs the *semantic/command surface and defaults*; the named-divergence channel remains the path where §2(a) mandates truth. **CONFIRMED + SHARPENED [vixy, §11.52(b)]: parity is perceptual-level ("reasonable vision capabilities") and conditioned on old being physically exact; old defects must NOT be reproduced and are tracked as defects — the ratified divergences stand without re-examination.**

52. **Vixy round-5: A31b write-back contract, parity criterion sharpened, enabled extension (2026-07-21, same day).** The three §11.51 residuals that were Vixy's close; verbatim quotes are the authority.
    - **(a) A31b RESOLVED — user-file write-back AUTHORIZED, under a three-clause contract** [vixy: *"write-back only when needed, and write as a different file then rename to ensure write succeed (ex : if disk is full, it could discard everything, we don't want this). It must only be operated on clean file, without anything ill-formed, otherwise it risk destroying ill-formed syntax."*]. **⚠ CLAUSE 3 SUPERSEDED [vixy 2026-07-22, §11.66(b)]: the "clean-file precondition ⇒ NO write-back" (protect-by-abstention) is REPLACED by protect-by-PRESERVATION — rewrite preserving malformed + unused fields + comments, with inline comment annotation of errors/defaults/valid-values. Clauses 1 (only-when-needed) and 2 (atomic temp+rename) STAND. The write-back itself moved from B28 to B31.** Original text kept below for provenance: (1) **Only when needed** — a critical key is absent (§11.51(d)'s criterion: keys that determine what is possible); never a routine rewrite. (2) **Atomic** — write a sibling temp file, then rename; the disk-full case must discard the *temp*, never the original [derived: rename atomicity holds same-filesystem only, so the temp lives in the target's directory, not /tmp]. (3) **Clean-file precondition, WHOLE file** — any ill-formed content anywhere ⇒ NO write-back for that file (a rewrite would destroy what didn't parse), plus a §2(f)-conform diagnostic naming what is ill-formed and the action to fix it. [derived design direction for B28, from the mandate's reason: prefer *text-preserving insertion* (add the missing key, leave every other byte) over model re-serialization — it minimizes the destruction surface the precondition guards against; the precondition stays mandated regardless.] Scripts remain out by §2(b), unchanged.
    - **(b) Parity meta-rule CONFIRMED + SHARPENED — perceptual parity, conditioned on physical exactness** [vixy: *"it must be as-if it was true from reasonable vision capabilities, and only if it was already physically exact. Defect which didn't reliably represent reality shouldn't hold and must be tracked as such."*]. Two-condition criterion: (1) equivalence level = **reasonable vision capabilities** — perceptual, not bit-level (the A/B channel's px>N thresholds were already this in practice; now it is the stated criterion, not a tolerance); (2) parity domain = behaviors where old was **already physically exact**; where old had a defect that didn't reliably represent reality, the behavior **must NOT be reproduced** and **must be tracked as a defect**. The §11.51(f) tension dissolves: the named-divergence channel is not an *exception* to the meta-rule — it IS the meta-rule's mandated tracking mechanism for the defect class; the ratified divergences (TAIL extent §11.43, name-on-disc §11.24a, §6.8(A) itself) all sit in the defect class and stand without re-examination. [derived reading, labeled: for pure semantic-surface questions with no physical referent — e.g. B29's reload semantics — the physical-exactness condition is vacuous and parity governs unconditionally; transparent substitution is the operative intent there.]
    - **(c) Enabled extension RESOLVED: same name minus `.disabled`; `.ini` for now** [vixy: *"The enabled format extension is the same without the .disabled extension, it will be .ini at first to keep the initial convention for now. If something better land, we'll change, that's lightweight change because it's a format change, not a structural content change, so it would only change the parsing, never the capability."*]. Concretely: `modularSystem/X.ini` = enabled, `modularSystem/X.ini.disabled` = generated inert twin; adoption = strip `.disabled`. **The stated principle is load-bearing for B24's design**: format is parsing-deep only — a format change must never be a capability change ⇒ the parser must be a thin, swappable layer over the capability model (I1: the capability model is the contract, a format is one implementation of it). Parser/content separation in B24 is now a requirement, not a style choice.

53. **B26 — dual-path default flip VERIFIED on screen; the recorded CHECK was itself defective (2026-07-21, Claude Opus 4.8, dispatch-2026-07-21-opus §1 task 1).** Pure verification, **no product code changed**. Six fresh launches, one per case, on `DISPLAY=:2` [measured: `xdpyinfo` exit 0, X.Org 24.1.6, 1804×976]. Scene, identical in every run: Earth surface 48.85°N/2.35°E alt 100 m, `date jday 2461233.5`, `timerate rate 0`, tracked Moon at `moon_scale 30`, landscape/atmosphere/star_twinkle/fps/labels/subtitle off, config projection FISHEYE, `init_fov = 180`, captures 2048² RGB. Harness: `harness/b26_run_case.sh` + `b26_default_flip.py` + `b26_analyze.py` + `b26_probe.gdb`; artifacts `harness/artifacts/b26/` (PNG+log, gitignored) and `harness/artifacts/b26_measurements.json` (committed).
    - **(a) Instrument mapped BEFORE any verdict.** Channel = `body action screenshot` → `App::takeScreenshot` → `SaveScreenInterface::takeScreenShot`, an app-side Vulkan readback of the rendered image [observed: interfaceModule/app_command_interface.cpp:3576-3581; appModule/app.cpp:1011-1014; appModule/save_screen_interface.cpp:146-172] — window focus/compositing cannot reach it, so they are not confounders [derived from the channel]. **Files actually written**: 28 per run, distinct mtimes at the commanded 0.25 s cadence [measured: 22:42:46.718651888 … 22:42:48.966626079 for c1 stab00..09]. **Content is the live frame, not black/stale**: mean RGB [0.744, 0.734, 0.717], max 255, 602 890 non-zero px of 4 194 304, and it tracks commanded state (pin-old vs pin-new differ by 47 955 px) [measured]. **NOISE FLOOR** — two captures of the *same* state 3 s apart: max per-pixel |Δ| = **0** (c1, c2, c3, c5), **4** (c4), **12** (c6); **px>32 = 0 in all six** [measured]. **COUNTERFACTUAL** — a pair *known* to differ (same camera, path pinned old vs new): max|Δ| = **71/255**, px>0 = 47 843…47 964, px>8 = 1 153…1 156, **px>32 = 133…136**, mean|Δ| = 1.93e-2 [measured, all six runs]. **Confounders named and controlled**: simulation time frozen AND PROVEN frozen — two `body action dual_dump` headers ~12 s apart carry the identical jd (2461233.500013901) in every run; ASmooth eases settled (15 s after `set moon_scale 30`, plus a **120 s-settle control run**, c6); the auto-playing `~/.spacecrafter/scripts/fscripts/startup.sts` (it sets `timerate rate 1` [observed: the file]) neutralised by re-asserting `timerate rate 0` after it, with the jd witnesses as the positive confirmation.
    - **(b) WHICH PATH IS RUNNING — evidence that is not the pixels.** The app was launched UNDER gdb (ptrace_scope = 1 blocks attach) with a breakpoint on `App::takeScreenshot` printing `core._M_ptr->ssystemFactory->{drawModularSystem,pathPinned}` **read from process memory at the instant of every capture** [harness/b26_probe.gdb]. Per case [measured, `PROBE` lines in `artifacts/b26/gdb_*.log`]: **no file → `draw=1 pinned=1` at 27 of the 28 captures**, the 28th being the deliberate pinned-old reference; `render_path=new` → identical; `render_path=old` → `draw=0 pinned=1` (except the two deliberate `flag experimental_path on` references); `render_path=alternate` → **`pinned=0`** with `draw` flipping 0/1 in runs of four 0.25 s shots = the 1000 ms toggle [observed: bodyModule/ssystem_factory.cpp:452-459]. **Cross-instrument agreement 24/24**: the pixel-derived NEW/OLD classification of the alternate burst (`ONNNNOOOONNNNOOOONNNNOOO`) matches the memory-read flag shot for shot — two independent channels, same answer.
    - **(c) The four cases (each = a fresh launch; the file's state IS the case).** Lookup site `AppSettings::getBetaFeaturesFile` = `getConfigDir() + "beta_features.ini"` = **`/home/claude/.spacecrafter/beta_features.ini`** [observed: tools/app_settings.cpp:125-128; confirmed at runtime by the log line *"Loaded experimental settings from /home/claude/.spacecrafter/beta_features.ini"*, which is ABSENT in the no-file runs and PRESENT in the four with-file runs — the read/no-read discriminator]. (1) **default, file absent** [measured: `ls` → ENOENT]: 24/24 shots NEW, **all 14 pairs at 2.5 s and all 20 at 1.0 s px>32 = 0** ⇒ no alternation. (2) **`render_path = alternate`**: 7/14 pairs at 2.5 s and **20/20 at 1.0 s** differ at px>32 = 135 ⇒ alternation live. (3) **`render_path = new`** (the *presence* path with the *default* value — a file that is read but ignored would pass (1) and (2) and still be broken): identical to (1), 0/20 at every lag. (4) **`render_path = old`** (added, not required by the DoD, because "new" is indistinguishable from ignoring the file): 24/24 shots **bit-identical to the pinned-old reference** (max|Δ| = 0) ⇒ the value is genuinely honoured in a third direction. (5) **`render_path = bogus_typo`**: `Core::setRenderPathMode`'s refusal fires *and* is observable — log *"beta_features.ini: unknown render_path 'bogus_typo' (expected new|old|alternate) - keeping the default (new)"* [measured] and the run behaves exactly as (1) (`draw=1 pinned=1`, 0/20 pairs differ). §11.50(c)'s "must be visible, not absorbed" is met.
    - **(d) THE RECORDED CRITERION IS DEFECTIVE — "≥2.5 s apart must differ under `alternate`" is a coin flip, not a test.** The toggle is a 1000 ms square wave, i.e. **period 2.0 s** [observed: `downCounter = 1000` / `delta_time`, ssystem_factory.cpp:452-459], so a 2.5 s separation is a **quarter-period** offset, not a half-period one. Measured on the alternate burst: lag **1.0 s → 20/20 pairs differ**; **2.0 s → 0/16** (same phase, always); **2.5 s → 7/14** (exactly 50 %); **3.0 s → 12/12** [measured]. My own run's nominal pair happened to land on the differing half — had it landed on the other, a *correct* build would have been recorded as a failure. **Corrected criterion, and it is what the harness now implements**: separation = an **odd multiple of 1.0 s**; discriminator = **px>32**, which measured **0 for every same-path pair in all six runs** and **133…136 for every cross-path pair** — a separation with no overlap, whereas `max|Δ|` (12…31 vs 71) and `px>8` (0…5 vs 1153…1156) do not separate as cleanly and "byte-identical" fails outright (next item). Class: a verification criterion derived from the *mechanism's nominal period* without checking the *sampling* it implies — the sampling-theorem form of the §11.33 reachability class.
    - **(e) RESIDUAL, measured and NOT fixed: the new path is not bit-stable on a frozen scene; the old path is.** Under a fully frozen scene the new-path render changes in **discrete steps that persist for seconds** (exact-equality classes over a 24-shot/6 s burst: `0 | 1×13 | 2×10`), magnitude **max|Δ| ≤ 31/255 on ≤ 3 898 px of 4 194 304 (0.09 %), px>32 = 0, mean|Δ| ≤ 9.1e-4**; the changed pixels are spread over the whole frame (radius 20…1030 px of a 1024 px fisheye), at low brightness (mean 25/255), **both signs** (+4 916 / −4 835), total frame luminance moving by 271 of 9.21e6 = **0.003 %** ⇒ a global sub-LSB luminance shift crossing rounding boundaries, not a geometry or texture change [measured, c6]. **The old path shows max|Δ| = 0 across all 24 shots and all lags** (c4) — so the mechanism is inside the new path, not in the instrument. **Convergence REFUTED, predict-then-observe**: predicting "a settling transient shrinks with settle time", the 120 s-settle run should have been quieter than the 15 s one — it was **not** (3 793 px / max 31 vs 628 px / max 12) ⇒ persistent, not transient. Hypothesis, labelled and NOT acted on: the per-frame `environment->update(*camera, jd, delta_time/1000.f, drawModularSystem)` chain feeding tone/adaptation on the new path [derived from the whole-frame, low-brightness, both-signs signature — **unproven**, the discriminating experiment was not run]. Out of scope for B26 (a verification task); recorded as **ledger row B30**. Against §11.52(b) it is far below "reasonable vision capabilities" (0.09 % of pixels, none above the px>32 discriminator) — it does not weaken (c)'s verdicts, and it *does* falsify §11.50(c)'s literal prediction that the two shots would be **byte-identical**.
    - **(f) Out-of-scope observations, recorded not fixed.** (1) `InitParser::getStr(section,key,def)` emits `L_WARNING "Init_parser def_str <section>:<key>"` on **every** call, including a fully successful read [observed: tools/init_parser.cpp:104-108] — a warning that carries no problem, the inverse of the §2(f) directive; it fires once per beta-file launch. (2) **The flip made four harness claims stale** — they assert the 1 s auto-toggle as the *default* [observed: harness/README.md:9, ab_orientation.py:11-12, dual-dump.sts:8 and :22]; `ab_orientation.py` in particular now clusters 24 shots into one phase unless `render_path = alternate` is set. Corrected **in place, documentation only** (each site gains the opt-in note + the §11.50(c)/§11.53 pointer), because the staleness is a direct consequence of the change being verified.
    - **(g) Hygiene.** `~/.spacecrafter/beta_features.ini` **removed** at the end — the shipped state is "absent == every default" and a leftover file would silently re-specify the next run's case [measured: `ls` → ENOENT]. `config.ini` md5 **03fbee59bc3ec506c58f0a3f1e1d73df** and `ssystem.ini` md5 **fb87a774e728706e9d4e1959c386bb23** — both unchanged from the §11.47 recorded values. Six clean `shutdown action now` exits, `[Inferior 1 … exited normally]` in all six gdb logs — **no §11.15d fire this session** (a sixth data point for ledger B7, on the f98de9f1 binary, mtime 2026-07-21 15:44).

54. **B19 — hidden-body ticking: the row's premise was FALSE, the ratified behavior did not hold, and it now does + is locked (2026-07-21, Claude Opus 4.8, dispatch-2026-07-21-opus §1 task 2). CLOSES the §11.15b(b) suspension** (*"hidden bodies (`hide()`) are update-frozen in new but computed in old — display-only-hide vs full-freeze is a semantic choice"*), answered by Vixy at §11.48(a) A10 / USER_QUESTIONS Q13, verbatim: ***"It should be where it is now."*** Files: `ModularBody.hpp` (+`updateHiddenBodies`, 2 call sites), `ModularBody.cpp` (3 call sites), `harness/b19_hidden_tick.py` (new), `harness/README.md`. Artifacts: `harness/artifacts/b19/`.
    - **(a) THE FINDING — the row said "current behavior ratified, no behavior change"; measurement says the new path froze hidden bodies dead.** `hide()` parks a body in `parent->hiddenBodies` [observed: ModularBody.cpp:389-408], a list that **no update walk visited** — not `recursiveUpdate`, not `selectiveUpdate`, not `recursiveTranslationUpdate`, not `dispatchUpdate` [observed: ModularBody.cpp:184-249 + ModularBody.hpp:569-632, exhaustive grep of `orbitingBodies` = 14 sites, none paired with `hiddenBodies`]. The old path is the opposite by construction: `SolarSystemDisplay::computePositions` iterates **every** body of the map, hidden or not [observed: bodyModule/solarsystem_display.cpp:343-357]. Measured on the pre-fix binary (f98de9f1 tree, `harness/b19_hidden_tick.py`), over 20 simulated minutes with the body hidden: **Moon moved_old = 1306.81 km, moved_new = 0.00 km; Phobos (under hidden Mars) moved_old = 2576.26 km, moved_new = 0.00 km; ΔlastJD_new = 0.000000000 d** — 18 of 32 assertions red. So B19 **was** a behavior change, and the tracker row was a cached conclusion that no one had re-verified (§5.2 class, again).
    - **(b) It was worse than "frozen at hide time": 14 bodies ship `hidden = true` and had NEVER been positioned.** [measured: pre-fix `b19_base.json`] `Pluto`, `Ceres`, `Pallas`, `Juno`, `Vesta`, `Eris`, `Haumea`, `Namaka`, `Hiiaka`, `Makemake`, `Sedna`, `Arrokoth`, `Solar_System`, `Goldilocks_Zone` all carried **`lastJD = 0`** — the ctor evaluates the orbit at the parent's then-`lastJD` [observed: ModularBody.cpp:80-85] and nothing ever revisited them, so their position was the one they have at **JD 0** (4713 BC). New-vs-old position error, pre-fix → post-fix, same scene (jd 2461233.5): **Pluto 5 751 229 598.7 km → 94.7 km · Eris 2 362 129 737.1 km → 219.0 km · Vesta 595 634 826.8 km → 19.3 km · Ceres 264 080 024.3 km → 1.9 km · Charon (child of hidden Pluto) 22 616.2 km → 0.000 km**. The residual is the ordinary old/new float-ulp + light-travel class (§11.15 P4: 6–55 km on AU chains), and it **scales with heliocentric distance exactly as that class predicts** (Sedna 511.6 km at ~84 AU, Venus 4.4 km at 0.7 AU) — predicted magnitude and mechanism, not a fitted acceptance.
    - **(c) The fix is the EXISTING mechanism applied to the one list it had missed (I6: the class, not the instance).** `updateHiddenBodies(jd, flat, surface, skip)` [ModularBody.hpp] walks `hiddenBodies` with `recursiveTranslationUpdate` — **translation-only**: eclipticPos / mat translation / distance current, no rotations, no visibility classification, no module updates. That is not a new policy: the requirement was already written for this class in the `selectiveUpdate` else-branch — *"Old-path parity: positions of non-drawn bodies stay queryable and sortable (the old path updates every body every frame)"* [observed: ModularBody.hpp:594-596] — and a hidden body is simply a non-drawn body. Called from **5 sites**, each handing the frame its visible twin uses (per-relation: HIDDEN_GROUNDED → accumulated surface frame, HIDDEN_ORBITING/INNER → flat position frame): `recursiveUpdate` (surface = `mat`), `selectiveUpdate` else-branch, `recursiveTranslationUpdate`, `dispatchUpdate` invisible-reference branch (surface = `mat_local_to_body`, which **is** `flat · accumulatedBodyPosToBody(jd)` by construction), and the up-chain parent loop (with the same `b.get() != body` exclusion the three visible loops carry — **a hidden body can be the camera reference**, and it is already updated as the walk's root).
    - **(d) The regression lock, and it is discriminating BY MEASUREMENT, not by claim.** `harness/b19_hidden_tick.py` (entry point; `DISPLAY=:2 build-claude/src/spacecrafter` fresh, then `./b19_hidden_tick.py`; exit 0/1 + `artifacts/b19/b19_result.json`). Observable = **`ecl` = `ModularBody::eclipticPos`** [dumped: ModularBody.cpp:569-570], written **only** by `transformParentToBodyPos`/`transformBodyToParent` immediately after the orbit evaluation [observed: ModularBody.hpp:404-405 and 539-540] — a freeze optimisation stops calling exactly those, so `ecl` keeps its hide-time value; it is the state a re-shown body is drawn at, not a proxy. `lastJD` is dumped as a corroborating witness, never as the criterion. Four families: **A membership** (`relation` flipped 4→1→4; without it a mistyped hide passes every other assert vacuously — the dump iterates the name registry, which contains hidden bodies), **B time control** (all 9 dumps' header jd == commanded to **0.0e+00 d**), **C advance** (new vs the OLD path's own advance over the same interval), **D "where it is now"** (|ecl_new − ecl_old| at t1, tolerance **calibrated in-run** from never-hidden controls Venus/Jupiter/Io: max disagreement **33.73 km** → tol 337.34 km). Post-fix, **59/59 assertions pass** (9 time + 18 membership + 32 per-body C/D/E), exit 0: hidden-leg Moon moved_new **1308.12 km** vs moved_old **1308.12 km**, |new−old|@t1 = **0.004 km** against a **~1308 km** freeze signature (5 orders of margin); Phobos **2578.82 km**, |new−old| = **0.000 km**. Both entries of the reversible pair, entry 2 starting from the state entry 1's show produced: hidden-2 Moon **1307.92 km** / 0.011 km, Phobos **2593.16 km** / 0.000 km. Shown legs (same assertions, nothing hidden) pass in BOTH binaries — that is what proves the red legs are the guarded behavior and not the instrument. **Discrimination proven a SECOND time on the fixed code**, as the DoD required: a one-line `return;` at the head of `updateHiddenBodies` (temporary, reverted) → rebuild → same script → **16 red, all four hidden-leg families, shown legs green, exit 1**; revert → rebuild → **59/59 green, exit 0** [measured: `/tmp/.../b19_mut.log` vs `b19_final.log`; `git diff` on product source back to exactly the 54 added lines of this entry].
    - **(e) Time control, proven positively (§11.53 method).** `timerate rate 0` is sent **before** the epoch, not after: the first run set the epoch first and the 1 s gap leaked **+1.390e-05 d = 1.20 s** of simulated time into the first two dumps (the auto-played `startup.sts` sets `timerate rate 1`) [measured, first run's assertion-B failures]. With the order corrected, **every** dump's header jd equals its commanded value at **0.0e+00 d**, so the only jd movement in the run is the four commanded 20-minute jumps — and the per-body `ΔlastJD` witnesses land at **0.0138889 d ±2.6e-07** (the residual is the light-travel retardation *changing*, i.e. a correct term, not noise).
    - **(f) Rare path ×2 and the reference case, live.** hide→show→hide→show in one uninterrupted run (above). Separately probed: **hiding the camera reference itself** — `body name Earth hidden true` while standing on Earth: Earth relation 4→1, keeps advancing (`lastJD` tracks the jd jump, new-vs-old **1.943 km**), its still-visible child Moon keeps ticking (**0.004 km**), camera reference stays `Earth`, no crash, `show` restores relation 4 [measured: /tmp/ref_{a,b,c,d}.json]. That is the `skip` argument doing its job — without it the up-chain walk would re-run the reference's subtree from its parent after it had already been updated as the walk's root.
    - **(g) No regression at the two verification heights that this could plausibly move.** Scenes A–D (`drive_scenes.py` + `predict.py`, fresh launch, `init_fov = 340`): P2 mat-residuals ≤ **1.7e-07**, P3 old-vs-new angles ≤ **2.4e-05 deg** and relative distances ≤ **2.2e-07**, P4 observer parity **12.55 / 21.29 / 21.10 / 55.07 / 25.94 km**, P5 differentials ≤ **1.74e-07** with the same named causes — the §11.16/§11.17 recorded class, unchanged. Scene E (`scene_e_spine.py`, fresh launch): **13/13 OK**, including both second entries and the Mars landing at 2.270821e-05 AU. `orientation_check.py` on the scene-A dump: **17 parity-restored / 48 named-divergent**, P-d **0.0000 deg** — the §11.35 spectrum, unchanged. Zero VUID across all four launches (`debug_layer = true` [observed: config.ini:307]); clean `shutdown action now` exit, no §11.15d fire. `config.ini` restored byte-identical after the `init_fov` change [measured: `diff` → empty]; `beta_features.ini` absent throughout.
    - **(h) Cost, stated because it is the thing the ratified answer bought.** The walk now evaluates the orbit of every hidden body every frame — with the shipped data that is **14 bodies + their subtrees** (Pluto's 4 moons, Namaka/Hiiaka) that previously cost nothing. This is the *deliberate* opposite of A1/B11's answer for trails (*"start fresh… to avoid performance issues"*): Vixy gated **recording** on display but explicitly did **not** gate **position**, and B11's own row already names the distinction (*"body hidden ⇒ still updating ⇒ trail still recording — reading the two as one gate produces a wrong implementation"*). If S9/B1 iterative updating later wants this cost back, `b19_hidden_tick.py` is the gate it has to pass.
    - **(i) Boundary NOT crossed, recorded.** This entry moves **positions only**. Whether a hidden body's *modules* tick — trail sampling in particular — is **B11's** question, not this one; `recursiveTranslationUpdate` runs no module update, so today a hidden body's trail does not accumulate. Stated here so B11 does not read §11.54 as having answered it.
    - **(j) Out-of-scope defects found, recorded not fixed.** (1) **`body … action hide` / `action show` DO NOT EXIST** — the `body` action dispatch is `load|remove|clear|drop|initial|preload|dual_dump|screenshot`, anything else falls to *"unknown action argument"* [observed: interfaceModule/app_command_interface.cpp:3554-3577]; the real spelling is **`body name <X> hidden true|false`** [observed: same file, 3592-3600]. Verified live: `body Sun action hide` leaves `relation = 4`, `body name Sun hidden true` gives `relation = 1` [measured: /tmp/syntax_probe{,2}.json]. **Two tracker consequences**: the §9 seam table row *"Hide/show body | `body action hide/show`"* carries a spelling that cannot work, and **§11.44's rare-path evidence** *"hide/show Sun (`body Sun action hide/show`) ×2"* traversed **nothing** — the "app survived every one" claim is true and empty. Corrected in the §9 row (below); §11.44's line is left as written with this pointer, per the do-not-rewrite-history rule. (2) `dumpTracePaths`'s own comment claims Pluto's *"lastJD stays fresh through recursiveTranslationUpdate"* [observed: bodyModule/ssystem_factory.cpp:569-572] — false for Pluto specifically, which is hidden, not merely invisible: it read `lastJD = 0` until this entry. Every `dumpHops` tilt/spin matrix for Pluto/Charon in the §11.34/§11.35 record was therefore evaluated at JD 0; the *spin* term is jd-dependent, so those two rows of `orientation_check.py`'s spectrum rest on a stale input (the tilt/commutator terms are pole-constant and unaffected — which is why the 115.60° Charon figure re-measures identically today). Comment left in place; flagged for whoever re-opens the orientation spectrum.

55. **B16 — `reloadSystem` exposed as `body action reload`, state-preserving; and the reload's first casualty was an I5 violation nobody had a reason to notice before (2026-07-21, Claude Opus 4.8, dispatch-2026-07-21-opus §1 task 3). CLOSES the §2(c) command-reachability gap opened at §11.36** (*"`reloadSystem` has zero command-reachable callers — unexercised by construction"*), answered by Vixy at §11.48(a) A20 / USER_QUESTIONS Q27: expose it, **keep current state (camera + date), do not reset to start-up**. Files: `interfaceModule/app_command_interface.cpp` + `base_command_interface.hpp` (the command), `coreModule/coreLink.{hpp,cpp}` (the link), `bodyModule/ssystem_factory.{hpp,cpp}` (`reloadCurrentSystem` — the state-preservation authority), `experimentalModule/ModularSystem.hpp` (`reloadSystem` guarded + returns bool), `experimentalModule/Camera.{hpp,cpp}` (`rebindReference`/`rebindTarget`/`getTrackedBody`; `tracked` added to `dumpTrace`), `experimentalModule/EnvironmentManager.{hpp,cpp}` + `ModularBody.cpp` (the I5 fix). Harness: `harness/b16_{run.sh,probe.gdb,reload.py,overrides.py,channels.py,reload_check.sts}`; artifacts `harness/artifacts/b16*/`.
    - **(a) Spelling and where it hangs, with the reason** [derived from the surrounding grammar, not taste]: **`body action reload`**. The `body action <verb>` slot **already carries system-scope verbs** — `clear` drops every script-added body [observed: app_command_interface.cpp:3564-3566 → `removeSupplementalSolarSystemBodies`] and `initial` resets every body's parameters and hidden state [observed: 3567-3568 → `ProtoSystem::initialSolarSystemBodies`, protosystem.cpp:958-967] — so a system-scope `reload` is a sibling of two existing verbs, not a new grammar branch. A new top-level `system` command was the alternative and was rejected on that evidence (it would have made the vocabulary say that `clear`/`initial` are per-body, which they are not). `W_RELOAD` added to `base_command_interface.hpp` beside `W_PRELOAD`.
    - **(b) "Reaches its handler" proved from the RUNNING PROCESS, three independent runs, count-matched.** App launched **under** gdb (ptrace_scope=1 blocks attach) with `harness/b16_probe.gdb`, breakpoint on `SSystemFactory::reloadCurrentSystem` [measured: run 1 → 3 commands / **3** `PROBE … ENTERED`; run 2 (overrides) → 1 / **1**; run 3 (channels) → 2 / **2**], plus the handler's own log line `System 'SolarSystem' reloaded (observer state kept)` at matching app-clock stamps. The 1:1 count IS the discrimination against the §11.54(j) silent-swallow class — a spelling that does not exist produces zero breakpoint hits.
    - **(c) "The reload actually reloaded" — the mutation test, and the file restored byte-identically.** The running app loads **`~/.spacecrafter/ssystem.ini`**, resolved as a *relative* path against the process cwd, which `main.cpp:194` sets to `~/.spacecrafter/` [observed: `std::filesystem::current_path(appDir)`; `ModularSystem::loadSystem` opens `filename` with a plain `ifstream`, and the Solar system is created with the literal `"ssystem.ini"` — ssystem_factory.cpp:119]. The other six `ssystem*.ini` in that directory are not candidates for the new path. Mutation: `[moon] radius = 1737.4` → `3474.8` (×2) [md5 `fb87a774…` → `894a0ccd…`], reload, then restore [md5 back to **`fb87a774e728706e9d4e1959c386bb23`**, asserted in-driver]. Measured on the live tree (`dual_dump`, new-path `boundingRadius` in AU): **1.18460794e-05 → 2.36921551e-05 (ratio 2.0000000) → 1.18460775e-05**; `screenSize` **0.00312182261 → 0.00624371925 → 0.00312182214**. On the composed screen (2048², FISHEYE, tracked Moon centred, selection pointer OFF — see (h)): baseline vs mutated **px>8 = 160**, baseline vs restored **px>8 = 0**, against a no-reload control pair of **px>8 = 0** — a separation with no overlap, using the B30-mandated threshold criterion rather than bit-equality. **Threshold note, because the criterion is scene-dependent and must not be copied blind**: B26's px>32 discriminator does NOT apply here — the tracked Moon's disc is ~6 px across and dim, so the ×2 mutation's max|Δ| is only **16**, i.e. px>32 = 0 on *both* sides. px>8 is the discriminator for this scene (control 0, mutation 160), and the control pair is what establishes that 8 is above the floor.
    - **(d) State preservation MEASURED, five dumps, two runs.** Directly measured from `Camera::dumpTrace` + the dump header: simulated date `jd` **2461233.500013901 in all five, bit-identical**; `reference` **Earth**, `tracked` **Moon**, `boundToSurface` true, `freeMode` false, `mount` equatorial, `heading` 0, `halfFov` 1.57079637, `longitude` 0.0410152376, `latitude` 0.852593362, `distance` 4.26359038e-05 AU — **every one bit-identical across three consecutive reloads**. Body count **90 → 90**, `null-new` (old-path body with no new-path twin) **0 → 0**. Residual, with its magnitude PREDICTED from its cause: `alt`/`az` move by ≤ **1.19e-07 rad** / ≤ **1.55e-06 rad** (0.32″ ≈ **0.001 px** at this fov), against a no-reload control spread of 8.9e-08 / 1.20e-07 rad — the tracking loop re-plans toward the target **every frame** with a 5 s eased profile [observed: Camera.cpp:264-266], so it has no exact fixed point; the prediction that the excursion decays back to control scale on the next sample is confirmed (third reload: `az` Δ = 1.79e-07 rad). **Not measured, inferred**: nothing — `tracked` was added to `dumpTrace` in this entry precisely so the tracked-body re-seat would be measured rather than argued.
    - **(e) The reversible pair, both entries, and the case that was supposed to break it.** Three reloads in one uninterrupted session, the second starting from the state the first produced (mutated data), the third from the state the second produced (restored data) — plus a second run with one reload and a third with two. **The camera's reference body was Earth every time — i.e. a body the reload destroys and recreates**, which is the `ModularBodyPtr`/I5 case the row was aimed at. It does not break, *because* the state is re-seated by name: `ModularBodyPtr::redirect` alone leaves the reference **valid** (it lands on the surviving parent, the SolarSystem node) and that is **not** the same as leaving it **preserved** — the distinction is the whole content of `reloadCurrentSystem`. Also traversed: reload while the reference **is** the system node itself (`ref=SolarSystem`) — works, the node is not among the destroyed children.
    - **(f) THE DEFECT THE ROW UNCOVERED — a cross-frame non-owning body cache with neither lifetime guarantee nor destruction notification (I5), fixed at the class.** `EnvironmentManager` keeps `activeChain` (raw `ModularBody*`, the previous frame's reference chain) as the diff base for enter/leave edges, and dereferences every entry absent from the new chain to fire `leave()` [observed: EnvironmentManager.cpp:31-37]; `lastReference` is a second, compare-only raw pointer [observed: :136]. Any body destroyed **between two frames** leaves a freed pointer in that base — a system reload does it to the whole chain at once, and `body action drop` on any chain ancestor is the same bug with a rarer trigger. Fix: `ModularBody::~ModularBody` **pushes** the destruction (`EnvironmentManager::notifyBodyDestroyed`, I3 — the owner notifies, the dependent never polls), which drops the entry (a destroyed body's members die with it, so *dropping* is the correct edge: there is nothing left to `leave()`) and nulls `lastReference` (a recycled address would otherwise read as "same reference" and swallow the landscape/atmosphere-model edge). This is I5's own remedy clause applied — a non-owning reference is legal *if* destruction-notified — not a guard at the reload site. Sweep: `activeChain`/`lastReference` are the **only** cross-frame non-owning `ModularBody*` holders [measured: grep over `src/experimentalModule/*.hpp` + `ssystem_factory.hpp` — the others are `ModularBodyPtr` (redirected), `lastFit`/`selectedBody` (nulled in the dtor), `notableBody` (per-frame), `sortedSystemBodies` (dtor-deregistered)].
    - **(g) The guard, and its actionable diagnostic (§2(f)).** `ModularSystem::reloadSystem()` now returns **false** when `systemFilename` is empty instead of clearing the children and reloading nothing — the universe/milkyway spine nodes and the anchor-only systems from `createSystem` have no source file, and the unguarded form would have **destroyed the spine** (`clearChildren()` on the milkyway removes every system in the galaxy). Verified live, not by reading: free-flight escalated to the Universe reference, `body action reload` → *"Cannot reload the system 'Universe': it was not loaded from a data file (it is a container level of the hierarchy, not a system description). Move the observer into a system loaded from a file (e.g. 'set home_planet Earth') and reload from there."* [measured: spacecrafter.log 000000134180, L_ERROR], and the tree is intact afterwards (90 bodies, 0 null-new on the way back down).
    - **(h) §2(c), both channels, both exercised.** One command registration serves them both — the script executor and every live channel share `AppCommandInterface::executeCommand` [observed: script_mgr.cpp:311/330 (script); app.cpp:737 mkfifo / :746 TCP; ui.hpp:203 + joypad_controller.cpp:224 (UI)] — so this is one artefact reaching two populations, which is exactly what §2(c) asks for. **Both were run, not argued**: live/scriptless over TCP, and `script action play filename b16_reload_check.sts` whose one line is `body action reload`; the gdb probe counted **2 entries for 2 invocations** and both dumps show the state kept. **The config channel does NOT apply and no key was invented**: a reload is an imperative act with no startup value to configure, and §11.50(c)'s constraint is that a flag lands in a file *only when the choice is actually relevant to someone* — nobody chooses "reload" at startup, that is just loading. (Contrast B17/B18, where "both channels" does mean config + command, because those are settings.)
    - **(i) SUSPENDED FOR VIXY — what "keep current state" does NOT cover today, measured rather than assumed.** A reload rebuilds every body from the file, so **body-scoped runtime overrides are reset to the file values**, while the OLD path (which is not reloaded at all) keeps its own. Measured [`harness/b16_overrides.py`, artifacts `b16ovr/`]: `flag moon_scaled on` + a live Moon `scaling` of **5.0000** → `boundingRadius` **5.92303877e-05 → 1.18460775e-05 AU (back to ×1)** across the reload; `body name Mars hidden true` → new-path `relation` **1 (hidden) → 4 (orbiting)**, i.e. the hide is undone, **while the old path still holds Mars hidden** — the two paths desync until old retires. The same mechanism explains the one ulp-scale figure in (c): the launch-built Moon's `boundingRadius` 1.18460794e-05 vs the reload-built 1.18460775e-05 (1.6e-07 relative ≈ 1.3 float32 ulp) is the `ASmooth`-eased `scaling` asymptote versus an exact 1. **The question, with the options seen**: does "keep current state" extend to body-scoped runtime overrides (scale seams, hide/show, per-body colours/skins/orbit-trail toggles), or is the file the authority for everything body-scoped and only the observer's own state is kept? Options: (1) file wins for bodies — today's behaviour, simplest, and arguably what "reload" *means*; (2) re-apply the recorded overrides after the rebuild — needs a per-body override ledger that does not exist and would have to be built (it is also the same ledger B29's colour-authority row needs); (3) re-apply only the global scale seams (moon/sun/planet_scale), which is the cheap 80% and the arbitrary one. **Not decided here.**
    - **(j) Named limitation, recorded not fixed.** The command reloads the **new path only** — `ModularSystem::reloadSystem` has no old-path counterpart (`ProtoSystem::load` is a load, not a reload, and nothing dismantles the old system). Under the flipped default (new path pinned, §11.53) the visible result is correct for every unconfigured user; a user on `render_path = old` gets a reload that changes nothing they can see. This retires with the old path and was not worth an old-path teardown that will be deleted.
    - **(k) No regression, at the heights this could plausibly move** (fresh launches, `init_fov = 340`, FISHEYE, `debug_layer = true`). Scenes A–D (`drive_scenes.py` + `predict.py`): P1 exact ≤ **8.48e-17**; P2 mat-residuals ≤ **1.09e-07**; P3 old-vs-new angles ≤ **1.32e-05 deg**, relative distances ≤ **2.20e-07**; P4 observer parity **16.32 / 13.44 / 27.14 / 55.06 / 14.17 km**; P5 ≤ **2.06e-07** with the same named causes — the §11.16/§11.45 class, unchanged. `orientation_check.py /tmp/gen_a.json`: **17 parity-restored / 48 named-divergent**, P-d **0.0000 deg**, identical to §11.54's figures. Scene E (`scene_e_spine.py`, fresh launch): **13/13 OK**, both second entries, Mars landing 2.270821e-05 AU. **Zero VUID on every launch** [measured: `grep -c VUID ~/.spacecrafter/log/vulkan.log` = 0]. Clean `shutdown action now` exits, **no §11.15d fire** on this binary (data point for ledger B7). `config.ini` restored byte-identically [md5 `03fbee59bc3ec506c58f0a3f1e1d73df` in and out]; `ssystem.ini` restored byte-identically; `beta_features.ini` absent throughout; the temporary `.sts` removed from the user script directory.

56. **B11 — the TRAIL RECORDING GATE: the display flag now gates recording, re-enable starts FRESH, and it is a SECOND gate independent of hidden/visible (2026-07-22, Claude Opus 4.8, dispatch-2026-07-21-opus §1 task 4). CLOSES the §11.41 suspension** (*"the display/recording separation is the old TODO, SUSPENDED for Vixy"*), answered by Vixy at §11.48(a) A1 / USER_QUESTIONS Q14, verbatim: **display gates recording — `flag object_trails off` STOPS accumulation, and re-enabling starts FRESH; stated reason: the performance cost of accumulating a trail nobody sees.** Files: `bodyModules/TrailModule.{hpp,cpp}` (the gate + `resetTrail` single authority + the harness `dumpState` + `accumulateCount`), `BodyModule.hpp`/`ModularBody.cpp` (the `dumpState` virtual + its default + the `trail` array in `dumpTrace`). Harness: `harness/b11_{run.sh,probe.gdb,trail_gate.py}` (new); artifacts `harness/artifacts/b11/`.
    - **(a) TWO GATES, and the row was right that reading them as one produces a wrong implementation.** The prior port (§11.41) gated accumulation on the DISPLAY FADER interstate [observed: pre-change TrailModule.cpp:166 `if (interstate >= 0.001f)`], which is neither of the two gates Vixy actually named: it coupled recording to the *fade animation*, so an `off` kept accumulating for one fade-length and a re-enable inside the fade never restarted. The fix makes accumulation gate on `want` = the flag / per-name override [TrailModule.cpp update()], leaving the fader as the DISPLAY gate that `draw()` reads (fade-out unchanged). The visibility axis is a SEPARATE gate owned elsewhere: a hidden body still records because `ModularSystem::drawTrails` sweeps every EVALUATED body [observed: ModularSystem.cpp:492-512, `if (body.distance == 0) break` is the only skip], hidden ones included since `hide()` does not remove from `sortedSystemBodies` and B19 keeps their `eclipticPos`/`lastJD`/`distance` current [§11.54]. So NO second walk and NO scheduling change was needed (the B1/S4 exclusion was never approached): the interaction the task flagged resolves because recording lives in the system phase, not in the per-body module-update walk — one authority (I2), the class fixed not the instance (I6).
    - **(b) THE 2×2 MATRIX, measured, the row's independence claim verified not assumed** [measured: `harness/b11_trail_gate.py` under `b11_run.sh`, exit 0, 94/94 assertions; deltas over 6 `date jday` jumps of 15 sim-days each, `timerate rate 0`, new path pinned; `dpoints` = trail buffer growth, `dAcc` = entries into `TrailModule::accumulate`]. Subjects carry a TRAIL module (deduce: non-satellite, non-Artificial, `orbit_visualization_period>0` — so Mars/Venus/Jupiter, NOT Moon/Phobos). Mars hidden (`relation`=1) the whole matrix, Venus/Jupiter visible (`relation`=4); selection pinned to the **Sun** so the star-selected global path is taken, not the non-star focus filter that would empty the matrix [observed: ssystem_factory.hpp:327].

      | | trail flag ON | trail flag OFF |
      |---|---|---|
      | **body visible** (Venus, Jupiter) | dpoints=**+6**, dAcc=**+1080**, recording=**true** | dpoints=**0**, dAcc=**0**, recording=**false**, points=**0** |
      | **body hidden** (Mars, rel=1) | dpoints=**+6**, dAcc=**+1080**, recording=**true** | dpoints=**0**, dAcc=**0**, recording=**false**, points=**0** |

      The two axes are orthogonal at the measured level: within a flag column the hidden row equals the visible row exactly (both +6 on, both 0 off — the visibility axis is inert on recording); across flag columns the same body differs (+6 vs 0 — the flag axis IS the gate). **Hidden Mars records identically to visible Venus** (both +1080 accumulate calls) — the §11.54 boundary ("hidden-body module ticking is B11's, not answered here") is now closed in the affirmative.
    - **(c) "The WORK stopped, not just the drawing" — DoD-4, from two independent instruments.** (1) Dump counter: `TrailModule::accumulateCount` (entries into `accumulate()`) is **FROZEN** across every OFF interval while simulated time advanced 2 jumps — `dAcc = 0` measured, so the accumulation code provably did not execute (a draw-only gate would leave it running). (2) The running process: the accumulate breakpoint (`b11_probe.gdb`, armed on each flag-ON) fired **6 `PROBE accumulate RAN` lines, each only after a `PROBE planetsSetFlagTrails b=1`** and never during an OFF interval. `resetTrail()` empties `points` outright (not a stale mark), so the memory the CPU stopped paying for is also freed [observed: TrailModule.cpp resetTrail()].
    - **(d) "Starts FRESH" measured, both entries of the reversible pair, twice** (on→off→on→off→on, cycle 2 from cycle 1's produced state). Per cycle per body: pre-off buffer had **≥2** points (there was history to lose), OFF → **points=0, recording=false**, re-enable → **points=1** (starts from 0, not from the pre-off count), and the first post-re-enable point sits at the body's CURRENT state — **|head − ecl| = 0.000000 km** and `head.jd` == the body's own `lastJD` (light-time-corrected, so it does NOT equal the observer header jd — the light-travel offset scales with distance, measured Mars ~6 min / Venus ~9.5 min / Jupiter ~37 min; `flag_light_travel_time=true`). The re-enable head jd is strictly greater than the pre-off head jd, i.e. no pre-off sample survived. It then regrows to **1+6** points over the next 6 jumps. **Sub-fade toggle** (off, then on 0.5 s later, inside the ~1 s fader): still **points=1** — a fader-gated implementation would have kept the old buffer here, which is exactly the assertion that discriminates the fixed design from the prior one.
    - **(e) Command spelling verified from the running process, both the working and the bogus form.** `flag object_trails on|off` → `FLAG_NAMES::FN_OBJECT_TRAILS` → `CoreLink::planetsSetFlagTrails` [observed: app_command_interface.cpp:927-931]: **11 `PROBE planetsSetFlagTrails` lines for 11 commands sent, 1:1**. The row's shorthand "`flag trails off`" does NOT exist — sent live, it produced **zero** probe lines and left the trail recording (points grew +2 over 2 jumps, recording stayed true) — the §11.54(j) silent-swallow class again, so the shorthand is a documentation spelling, not the code's. Hide/show is `body name <X> hidden true|false` → `CoreLink::setPlanetHidden` (2 probe hits for the 2 hide/show commands).
    - **(f) Terminal observable on the composed screen (2048², FISHEYE, tracked Sun, pointer OFF, `timerate rate 0`), with a calibrated threshold and its measured noise floor** [measured: `harness/artifacts/b11/b11_screen_*.png`]. **Noise floor** (two shots of the same on-state 1.5 s apart): **max|Δ| = 0, px = 0 at every threshold** — this frozen tracked scene is bit-stable (B30's ≤31/255 instability did not fire here; MEASURED not assumed, which is why the floor is stated). **Trail present vs absent** (flag on vs off at the SAME date, only the flag toggled + fade): **25 274 px>32, max|Δ| = 101**, the diff a band of trail arcs across the field; the off frame is not black (**2 715 085 nonblack px**), so the comparison is content-vs-content, not masked fiction. **Fresh re-enable reveals NOTHING**: reon vs off = **0 px at every threshold** (identical) — the 1-point buffer draws nothing (`draw()` returns at `n < 2`), so re-enabling does not flash a grown-while-off trail. Any threshold discriminates against a 0 floor; px>32 = 25 274 (present) / 0 (absent, fresh) is reported. NB the `screen_regrown` shot advanced 450 sim-days (whole-scene move) and is supplementary, not the present/absent pair.
    - **(g) No regression** (fresh launches, `init_fov=340`, FISHEYE, `debug_layer=true`). Scenes A–D (`drive_scenes.py`+`predict.py`): P1–P5 mat-residuals float-eps ≤ **1.09e-07** everywhere (the deterministic witness); P4 observer parity is the settle-ease noise class B16 documented — **run 1: 14.36/13.30/27.24/55.04/25.94 km, run 2: 2.86/44.47/41.87/46.01/25.94 km** (varies run-to-run by design, cf. §11.55(k)'s 16.32/13.44/27.14/55.06/14.17). Scene E (`scene_e_spine.py`, fresh launch): **13/13 OK**, both second entries, Mars landing 2.270821e-05 AU. `orientation_check.py`: **17 parity-restored / 48 named-divergent, P-d 0.0000 deg** — identical to §11.54/§11.55. Build exit 0, binary mtime advanced 2026-07-21 23:57 → 2026-07-22 00:31. `config.ini` restored byte-identically [md5 **03fbee59bc3ec506c58f0a3f1e1d73df** in and out; the app rewrites it on shutdown, so restore is from the pre-run backup].
    - **(h) One design edge recorded, not reachable via the command interface.** The rising-edge fresh-start detects the flag transition through `recording` being cleared by an OFF-frame's `update()`; if a flag OFF and a flag ON were processed with ZERO rendered frames between them, no OFF-frame would clear `recording` and the re-enable would not restart. This cannot happen through any channel (commands are interleaved with main-loop renders; the 0.5 s sub-fade case in (d) is the tightest realistic timing and starts fresh). Left as documented, not guarded — guarding it would mean threading the flag transition into the static `setGlobalShow`, a larger change for an unreachable state.
