58. **B18 — the EQUATORIAL-MOUNT SKY-LOCK (old `flag_lock_equ_pos`) ported to the new-path Camera: with the lock ON the view holds FIXED in the reference body's equatorial frame as sidereal time advances, instead of the default horizon-locked drift (2026-07-22, Claude Opus 4.8, dispatch-2026-07-21-opus §1 task 6). PORTS the §12 "Equatorial-mount sky-lock" seam that was OLD-ONLY** (§11.19c / §12 row, "unported — an unexercised legacy feature"), answered by Vixy at §11.48(a) A25 / USER_QUESTIONS Q4, verbatim: ***"Yes, we still use it."*** — the "unexercised" premise was WRONG. Files: `experimentalModule/Camera.{hpp,cpp}` (`setSkyLock`/`getSkyLock`, `skyLocked`+`lockedSkyRot` state, the per-frame hold in `update()`, `skyLocked` in `dumpTrace`); `coreModule/core.hpp` (`setFlagLockSkyPosition` de-inlined to a declaration) + `coreModule/core.cpp` (its both-paths mirror). Old path UNTOUCHED (`navigator.cpp` byte-clean). Harness: `harness/b18_{run.sh,probe.gdb,skylock.py,analyze.py}` (new); artifacts `harness/artifacts/b18/`.
    - **(a) THE OLD BEHAVIOR, characterized at source — this is the spec [observed: navigator.cpp:123-135].** `Navigator::updateVisionVector`, precedence `flag_auto_move > flag_traking > flag_lock_equ_pos > default`: lock ON and neither auto-moving nor tracking ⇒ **`local_vision = earthEquToLocal(equ_vision)`** — the equatorial vision vector (`equ_vision`, direction in earth-equatorial coords = fixed to the celestial sphere) is HELD and the local (alt-az) vector is recomputed each frame from the sidereal-advancing `mat_earth_equ_to_local`. Default (lock OFF) is the mirror (`equ_vision = localToEarthEqu(local_vision)`, local held, sky drifts). The ROLL/up is set SEPARATELY by `updateViewMat` from the MOUNT [observed: navigator.cpp:267-288]: `VIEW_EQUATOR` ⇒ up = equatorial pole (north-up), `VIEW_HORIZON` ⇒ up = local zenith. Under the SHIPPED config (`viewing_mode = equator`, config.ini:265) + lock, direction AND up-reference are both equatorial ⇒ the WHOLE eye orientation is frozen in the equatorial frame. Channels: **command `flag lock_sky_position on|off|toggle`** [observed: base_command_interface.hpp:459 → app_command_interface.cpp:948-952 → Core::setFlagLockSkyPosition → navigation->setFlagLockEquPos]; turn/drag keys UNLOCK (core.cpp:1582-1685); select-while-tracking auto-ENABLES (core.cpp:938,1979, direct `setFlagLockEquPos(1)`).
    - **(b) THE PORT — hold the composed rotation via the view-composition authority.** New Camera stores the view as (alt,az) in the param frame; the horizon-locked default IS old's `local_vision`-held behavior (measured baseline: unlocked, (alt,az) constant while the mat's equatorial orientation drifts the full sidereal angle). `setSkyLock(true)` captures `lockedSkyRot = viewRotation()·placementRotation()` — the body→eye rotation, the new-path analog of old's held `equ_vision`, roll included. `update()`, when holding, calls `recoverParams(lockedSkyRot)` every frame — the SAME deduce-identical-view primitive `setMount`/`setFreeMode`/`switchToBody` use — re-deriving (alt,az,heading) against the CURRENT (sidereal-advanced) `placementRotation()` so `viewRotation()·placementRotation()` stays == `lockedSkyRot`. The spin enters through `computeSurfaceToBody() = zrotation(−axisRotation−π/2)`, `axisRotation = get_apparent_sidereal_time(jd)` [observed: ModularBody.hpp:282,304], so alt/az drift to hold the RA/DE. Because the WHOLE composed rotation is held, the terminal observable matches old VIEW_EQUATOR+lock EXACTLY and is MOUNT-INDEPENDENT (the new Camera runs ALTAZ internally, (g)). Placed AFTER the observer-move block (holds the sky under observer moves too); guarded `!freeMode && !target && viewT<=0` (old precedence: auto_move/tracking dormant the lock); non-holding frames re-capture so a resumed hold starts from the present view; error does NOT accumulate (each frame re-derives from the fixed `lockedSkyRot`, not the previous params).
    - **(c) THE OBSERVABLE, MEASURED — the DoD discriminator [measured: `harness/b18_skylock.py`+`b18_analyze.py`, one fresh launch under gdb, anchored Earth 48.85°N/2.35°E, sidereal advance Δjd=0.05 day = 18.0493° (= 360.9856°/day × 0.05), FISHEYE, config `init_fov=180`].** Per t0/t1 pair, the view-direction change in two frames — equatorial (new `mat` rotational part; old `helioToEye` rotational part) and alt-az (Camera alt,az):
        - **OFF** (default local lock): new alt-az **0.0000°**, new equ **18.0493°**, old equ **18.0493°**, old local_vision **0.0000°**. Composed screen **px>32 = 142 566** (star field + equatorial grid swept 18°).
        - **ON** (sky-lock): new alt-az **11.8504°** (drifts to hold the sky), new equ **0.0000°**, old equ **0.0000°**, old local_vision **11.8504°**. Composed screen **px>32 = 0** (max|d|=4, px>8=0). A no-op "lock" would leave the OFF pattern (alt-az 0 / equ 18°); the alt-az delta became NON-ZERO and the equ delta COLLAPSED — the lock does exactly what it claims. (alt-az 11.85° < the 18.05° pole rotation because the held direction sits off the pole — geometry, predicted.)
        - **CROSS-PATH PARITY**: old-equ == new-equ to all printed digits in BOTH states, and old-local-vision == new-alt-az — the one `flag lock_sky_position` command drives BOTH paths (the mirror), old (VIEW_EQUATOR) and new (ALTAZ internally) reproduce identical frozen-equatorial / drifting-local magnitudes.
    - **(d) BOTH REVERSIBLE ENTRIES + the flag-spelling trap.** on→off→on→off traversed; each `on` re-freezes equ (ON new_equ 0.0000, ON2 new_equ 0.0000 — the second `on` from the state the first `off` produced), each `off` restores the horizon lock (OFF2/OFF3 new_altaz 0.0000, new_equ 18.049x, screen ~143k), bit-consistent both entries. **Flag spelling verified from the running process**: bogus `flag lock_sky_positionX on` → dump `skyLocked=false`, ZERO effect; the gdb breakpoint on `Camera::setSkyLock` fired **exactly 4× (b=1,0,1,0)** for the 4 real commands and **0** for the bogus (§11.54(j)/b11 swallowed-misspelling class). Screen noise floor (same frozen state twice) = **0 px>0** (bit-stable), so the ON residual (max|d|=4, 0 px>8) is the `recoverParams` sub-pixel Euler residual (~1e-7 rad, attributed, sub-threshold).
    - **(e) THE CONFIG CHANNEL DOES NOT EXIST IN THE OLD PATH — source-verified negative result, correcting the tracker (I2).** §11.48(h) contrasted B16 against "B17/B18, where 'both channels' does mean config + command, because those are settings." Re-verified: `flag_lock_equ_pos` has **no** config reader — set to 0 unconditionally at every `Core::init` [observed: core.cpp:396], mutated only by the command + the internal tracking transitions; no `SCK_*` key, no `conf.get*` (grep clean). The row title "Equatorial-mount sky-lock" **conflated two features**: the MOUNT (`viewing_mode = equator`, config.ini:265 → `setViewingMode`, core.cpp:473 — a genuine startup setting) and the SKY-LOCK (`flag_lock_equ_pos` — a transient runtime state). Only the mount has a config channel; the sky-lock is command-only. Inventing a startup key the old path never had would be an unrequested user-visible addition (an architectural decision, not mine) — NOT done; the parity default is no key.
    - **(f) SUSPENDED FOR VIXY — the Camera-orientation compositions the DoD reserved.** (i) **VIEW_HORIZON mount + sky-lock**: old holds direction-only and lets the roll follow the local zenith (the sky ROLLS around the held direction as the body spins); this port holds the WHOLE orientation (no roll). Shipped config is VIEW_EQUATOR where the two COINCIDE exactly, and the new mount is unwired ((g)), so ALTAZ+lock is not a shipped configuration — which behavior the new path should give under a mount split is Vixy's call once the mount is wired. (ii) **free-mode + sky-lock** (hold dormant in freeMode). (iii) the **select-while-tracking auto-enable** (old core.cpp:938,1979 bypass `Core::setFlagLockSkyPosition`, so they do NOT mirror to the Camera) — whether selecting a new body while tracking should auto-engage the new-path sky-lock depends on how it composes with Camera `target` tracking (structurally different from old `flag_traking`). (iv) whether a startup config default should be ADDED ((e)).
    - **(g) OUT-OF-SCOPE FINDING, not fixed: the mount (`viewing_mode`) is NOT wired to the new Camera.** **⚠ CORRECTED — this premise is FALSE, see §11.61(f) [dispatcher-verified 2026-07-22]:** `camera->setMount(viewing_mode=="equator" ? EQUATORIAL : ALTAZ)` IS called at `ssystem_factory.cpp:147` (since commit 96a1b896, the §11.19c Camera rework — predates this wave), and every runtime dump reads `mount:equatorial` [measured]. The "no caller / runs ALTAZ" claim below was a **stale grep** (B18's grep scope missed `bodyModule`; the dispatcher independently reproduced the miss by truncating a `grep | head`, then confirmed the call at source + `git log 96a1b896`). B18's mount-INDEPENDENCE conclusion is unaffected — the sky-lock holds the whole composed rotation either way — only its "runs ALTAZ internally" premise is retracted. Original (wrong) text preserved for provenance: `Camera::setMount` has **no caller** [grep clean] — the shipped `viewing_mode = equator` reaches only the old path (core.cpp:473). So the new Camera always runs ALTAZ; its absolute screen roll (up = the lock-time zenith when locked, vs old's equatorial-pole up) differs from old by a constant present WITH OR WITHOUT the lock (a pre-existing gap, not introduced here — the DELTA observable this port reproduces is mount-independent). **This interacts with B13** (reference-change absolute-sky-direction continuity): `switchToBody`/`warpToBody` do NOT re-capture `lockedSkyRot`, so a reference switch WHILE LOCKED would hold the OLD body's equatorial orientation — **untested, noted**; whoever wires the mount and whoever does B13 inherit `skyLocked`/`lockedSkyRot` as Camera-orientation state to carry.
    - **(h) NO REGRESSION** (fresh launches, FISHEYE). **Scenes A–D** (`drive_scenes.py`+`analyze.py`+`orientation_check.py`+`predict.py`, `init_fov=180`): every camera dump `skyLocked=false` (the new field emitted, default off ⇒ my hold block never runs), deltas = the recorded baseline classes — **P4 observer parity 13.25–55.04 km** (the 13–55 km settle class), Earth dEcl 1.45e-8 / dPos 4.2e-9, Moon dEcl ≤8.7e-11 / dPos ≤3.4e-9, Mars dPos 2.66e-5–2.88e-5 (1.4-ulp), Sun rotAng 163.90° (§11.34), TNO STALE class (§11.39); **orientation 17 parity-restored / 48 named-divergent, P-d 0.0000°** (exact §11.54–57 match). **Scene E** (`scene_e_spine.py`, `init_fov=340` config-edited then restored): **13/13 OK, Mars landing 2.270821e-05 AU** (exact recorded match). 60. **B9 — AZIMUTH-CONVENTION divergence fixed at the ModularObject surface with ONE conversion authority; the probe REFUTED the recorded `3π−az` formula and measured the true surface delta `az_old = π/2 − az_new` (2026-07-22, Claude Opus 4.8, dispatch-2026-07-21-opus §1 task 8).** Closes §11.4 item 4 (az convention + the getShortInfoNavString label-order swap). Harness: `harness/b9_{run.sh,azconv.py}` (new), artifacts `harness/artifacts/b9_{prefix,postfix,horizon}/`.
    - **(a) PROBE — the delta is a CLEAN single convention, but NOT the recorded one [measured: harness/b9_azconv.py, dual_dump altaz_old (Body::getAltAz) vs altaz_new (the REAL ModularObject::getAltAz), one fresh launch, FISHEYE, 4 samples = 2 dates (jd 2461233.5 / 2461321.25) × 3 surface observers (Paris 48.85N/2.35E, Sydney −33.9/151.2, North 60/−45) × tracking ON (S1,S2) and OFF (S3,S4)].** Over **234/234 non-degenerate bodies** the relation is `az_old = π/2 − az_new (mod 2π)` — a reflection about 45° — at **max |dev| = 0.00003°, mean 0.000009°** (a float32 residual: az_old is double via Navigator::earthEquToLocal, az_new is float via Camera Vec3f). **No per-body, per-hop, per-observer or per-date component.** The only 4 outliers are all **Earth** = the observer's home body at the NADIR (dist 4.26e-5 AU ≈ surface; alt matches old EXACTLY, az undefined at the pole for both paths — a degeneracy, excluded, not a residual).
    - **(b) CONFLICT with the tracker, RESOLVED not silently [observed: body.cpp:381 vs measurement].** §11.4/§13.B B9 recorded `az = 3π − az (mod 2π)`. That constant is CORRECT for the OLD path's INTERNAL raw→report step (`Utility::rectToSphe(earthEquToLocal)` → `3π − raw`, body.cpp:380-382), which I re-confirmed at source. But the NEW raw az sits in a frame whose zero is **−π/2** from the old raw frame [derived from the measurement: `3π − raw_old = π/2 − raw_new ⇒ raw_new = raw_old − π/2`]. So the surface conversion that reproduces the old REPORT is **`π/2 − raw_new`**, not `3π − raw_new` (which would land 90° off). This is EXACTLY the risk §11.4 flagged in its own probe note (line 753): *"assuming the 3π−az transform transfers unmeasured is acting on an unverified frame model."* Measurement resolved the layer confusion; the recorded formula was for the wrong frame.
    - **(c) THE FIX — one conversion authority (I2), NOT per-caller [observed: ModularObject.cpp:119-137, .hpp:39-52].** New private `ModularObject::altAz()` returns `(alt, az)` with `az = fmod(M_PI_2 − raw, 2π)` normalized to [0,2π). The THREE alt/az exits of the surface all route through it: `getAltAz` (feeds `getSelectedAZ/ALT` once the bridge is wired), `getInfoString` ("Alt/Az:" line), `getShortInfoNavString` ("Az/Alt/coA:" field). Grep-verified: `Camera::observedPosToAltAz` is now called at exactly ONE site — inside `altAz()` — so no consumer can desync (the old path itself DUPLICATED `3π−az` at body.cpp:381 AND :431; the new path removes that duplication). **This also fixes the §11.4-flagged label-order swap**: the new `getShortInfoNavString` had printed alt↔az swapped AND coA=90−az (garbage) under the "Az/Alt/coA" label; it now prints `az/alt/(90−alt)` reproducing old body.cpp:433 exactly (old is the parity spec — a port defect corrected, not a design choice).
    - **(d) POST-FIX PARITY — measured [measured: harness/b9_azconv.py on the fixed binary].** az_old vs az_new (post-fix) **max |Δaz| = 0.00002°**, **max |Δalt| = 0.00001°** (equator mount) / **0.00002°** (altaz mount) — az now matches old to the float32 floor, altitude untouched. The `b9_azconv.py` lock DISCRIMINATES (traversed both binaries): PRE-fix az_new is raw ⇒ direct |Δaz| up to 179.8° (FAIL), the π/2 hypothesis ~0; POST-fix direct |Δaz| ~0 (PASS), the π/2 hypothesis flips to 179.8°. An assert never seen to fail is decoration; this one fails on the pre-fix binary and passes on the fixed one.

61. **B13 — REFERENCE-CHANGE VIEW CONTINUITY: `warpToBody` (`set home_planet`) now KEEPS the absolute sky direction across a reference switch instead of holding alt/az frame-relative; free-mode entry/exit already did (2026-07-22, Claude Opus 4.8, dispatch-2026-07-21-opus §1 task 9). CLOSES the §11.19c / §11.19b "free-mode transition subsystem" suspension** (*"revive `view` as the smoothing quaternion, or derive transitions from alt/az directly — that decision owns the correct inverse formulas"*), answered by Vixy at §11.48(a) A11 / USER_QUESTIONS Q2, verbatim: ***"Keep looking at the same absolute direction in the sky"*** (chosen over the offered "same framing relative to the new body" and "re-center on the new body"). Files: `experimentalModule/Camera.{hpp,cpp}` (`warpToBody` compensation, `switchToBody`/`warpToBody` sky-lock re-expression, `lastAbsFwd` instrument + `absFwd` in `dumpTrace`); `harness/scene_e_spine.py` (view-continuity assertions), `harness/b13_viewcont.py` (new); artifacts `harness/artifacts/b13/`.
    - **(a) MECHANISM = the existing deduce-identical-view primitive; the `view` quaternion stays retired [derived + observed: Camera.cpp:77-108].** The §11.19c rework already removed the Rotator and made `recoverParams(totalRot)` the ONE transition primitive (ZXZ Euler extraction reproducing `viewRotation()·placementRotation() == totalRot` under the current modes/mount/placement); §11.19b's "the machinery will snap the view" premise was already OBSOLETE — `switchToBody` (free-flight auto-transitions) and `setFreeMode` have composed correctly through `recoverParams` since §11.36. The ONE path that still re-centred was `warpToBody`, which held (alt,az,heading) UNCHANGED and only re-based `distance` — i.e. "keep the same framing relative to the NEW body" (a REJECTED Q2 option). **The inverse formula it owns**: capture `R = viewRotation()·placementRotation()·comp` where `comp = reference->calculateSwitchCompensation(dst)` maps dst-equatorial→old-ref-equatorial coordinates [ModularBody.cpp:451]; then `recoverParams(R)` under `reference=dst` reproduces the SAME `eye←root` orientation because `mat_new = mat_old·comp` and `comp == accumulatedBodyToBodyPos_old·accumulatedBodyPosToBody_dst` (the exact inter-frame rotation) [derived; algebra in the code comment]. Done DIRECTLY (one `recoverParams`), NOT via switchToBody's `setFreeMode`/`setBoundToSurface` round-trip — those toggles rewrite `longitude` by `getAxisRotation()` and, across a reference change, would subtract dst's axis rotation from a longitude that added the old body's, corrupting the observer LOCATION warpToBody must keep exact (a bug avoided, not hit; the toggle is safe in switchToBody because its only caller runs in freeMode where lon/lat are not the position authority). Position (teleport) and look direction are orthogonal — the altitude re-base is kept, the view is compensated.
    - **(b) THE DEFECT MAGNITUDE, MEASURED FIRST [measured: harness/b13_viewcont.py, absFwd = ROOT-aligned look direction added to Camera::dumpTrace, anchored Earth 48.85°N/2.35°E, look_at az 60 alt 30, FISHEYE].** Instrument added first (inert observability): `lastAbsFwd = (−r[2],−r[6],−r[10])` of `mat·reference->accumulatedBodyToBodyPos(jd)` = eye-forward (−z) in the single common inertial frame every body shares (frame-independent; `mat`'s own frame is body-specific). Per transition the ABSOLUTE-frame delta (should be ~0) and the alt/az-frame delta (the discriminator): **PRE-FIX ref switch Earth→Mars: absDelta 78.60°, altazDelta 0.00°** — the sky JUMPED 78.6°, alt/az held (30/60 unchanged) = frame-relative hold. **Free-mode entry/exit: absDelta ≤1e-5°** (already correct, no defect). **POST-FIX (settled): ref switch Earth→Mars absDelta ≤5e-6° / altazDelta 78.4–90.2°; Mars→Earth (reversible pair, second from the first's end) absDelta ≤5e-6°; free enter→exit→enter absDelta ≤1e-5°.** The absDelta and altazDelta SWAPPED exactly — the signature of holding the ABSOLUTE direction, not the frame-relative one.
    - **(c) RESIDUAL CLASS, ATTRIBUTED (two floors, both predicted).** **Settled ≤6e-6° = the `recoverParams` ZXZ Euler-extraction floor (~1e-7 rad)**, SHARED with `switchToBody` (measured 8.45e-6° on a freeMode Earth→Sun auto-transition, same run) — B13 introduces NO new residual class. Single-switch, well-settled `warpToBody` deltas: Earth→Sun 0.000000°, Earth→Moon 6e-6°, Earth→Mars 2e-6°, Earth→Venus 5e-6° (up-hop / down-hop / cross-branch / cross-branch — topology-independent). **The larger ≤5e-3° seen on the FIRST switch of a FRESH launch = the B30 frame-reconstruction run-to-run non-determinism** (§11.53(e)/B30: the new path is not bit-stable on a frozen scene) — measured INDEPENDENTLY as the ~0.003° spread between two fresh-launch absFwd samples of the IDENTICAL Earth/look_at 30/60 state; a pre-existing measurement floor, not a warpToBody artifact. Both ≪ the 0.05° scene-E tolerance and ≪ 0.024 px at init_fov=180.
    - **(d) SCENE E GAINED A DISCRIMINATING VIEW-CONTINUITY ASSERTION (B19/B20 standard) — 13→21 OK.** 8 new asserts on a controlled Earth-anchored block: absFwd HELD (<0.05°) across `set home_planet` Earth→Mars→Earth and free-mode enter→exit→enter→exit, plus alt/az MOVED (>10°, measured 90.24°) across the ref switch = the discriminator, plus a switchToBody positive control (ladder e_in→e_out 1e-5°). **Proven discriminating by temporary mutation** (recoverParams(R) DISABLED in warpToBody, rebuilt exit 0): the 3 ref-switch asserts FLIP to **FAIL** (abs held 90.24° > 0.05, discrim 0.00° < 10 — the EXACT swap of the passing values), while the free-mode and switchToBody asserts stay green (localized failure, instrument chain intact); reverted, rebuilt, 21/21 restored. An assert never seen to fail is decoration; these were seen to fail exactly when the re-centring behavior was reintroduced.
    - **(e) SKY-LOCK COMPOSITION HANDLED (the B18 §11.58(g) handoff) [derived from Q2 + measured].** B18(g) flagged that `switchToBody`/`warpToBody` do not re-capture `lockedSkyRot`, so a reference switch WHILE LOCKED would hold the OLD body's equatorial orientation. Fix: both switches now `lockedSkyRot = lockedSkyRot·comp` — the SAME inter-frame rotation, so the held equatorial orientation is re-expressed in dst's frame and the lock keeps holding the SAME ABSOLUTE point. This is DERIVED, not invented: under an active lock the view IS the held orientation, so Q2 ("keep the same absolute direction across reference change") REQUIRES re-expressing it — not doing so would violate Q2 in the locked case. **Measured**: `flag lock_sky_position on` then `set home_planet Mars` → absDelta **0.000006°** (absFwd identical), alt/az drift 78.6° to hold the sky; reversible back to Earth 6e-6°. The alternative reading (re-LOCK to the new body's frame at the current view) is noted as a one-line change if Vixy prefers it, but Q2's literal reading is preserve-absolute, which is implemented. Composes with B18's still-suspended sky-lock items (free-mode+lock dormant, VIEW_HORIZON+lock, select-while-tracking auto-enable) — none of which this touches.
    - **(f) SOURCE CONFLICT SURFACED, not resolved silently (I2) — B18 §11.58(g) is FACTUALLY WRONG about the mount.** §11.58(g) states *"Camera::setMount has no caller [grep clean] ... the new Camera always runs ALTAZ."* Refuted: `ssystem_factory.cpp:147` calls `camera->setMount(viewing_mode=="equator" ? EQUATORIAL : ALTAZ)` and has since the §11.19c rework (commit 96a1b896); the shipped `viewing_mode = equator` DOES reach the new Camera, and every B13 dump reads **`mount:equatorial`** at runtime [measured]. B18's "mount-independent" DELTA conclusion still stands (the sky-lock holds the whole composed rotation either way), but its "runs ALTAZ internally" premise is a stale grep. Logged for whoever revisits the mount wiring; NOT fixed here (out of row).
    - **(g) NO REGRESSION (fresh launches, FISHEYE, init_fov=340).** `warpToBody` is exercised by `drive_scenes.py` (`set home_planet Moon`/`Mars`) but the following `flag track_object on` overrides the view, so the position/orientation dumps are unaffected: **Scenes A–D P4 observer parity 17.35–50.89 km** (gen_a 24.34, gen_b 47.36, gen_moon 27.34, gen_mars 50.89, gen_mars_2 17.35 — the 9–55 km class), zero UNMODELED/FAIL across all five, **orientation 17 restored / 48 named-divergent, P-d 0.0000°** (exact §11.54–60 match). **Scene E 21/21 OK, Mars landing 2.270821e-05 AU** on the final binary. **Build exit 0** (binary mtime advanced 2026-07-22 05:03:56). `config.ini` restored byte-identically [md5 **03fbee59bc3ec506c58f0a3f1e1d73df** in and out; scene E + drive_scenes edited init_fov 180→340 and restored by `cp` of a backup — NOTE the app rewrites config.ini on shutdown (init_fov persisted 340), so the restore `cp` must run AFTER the process is fully dead]. No data files touched. `.gitignore` +1 line (`artifacts/b13/`).

62. **B15 — AoI RE-DERIVATION ON DATE CHANGE: the launch-jd `areaOfInfluence` latch is a defect, fixed by recomputing the position-derived reach EVERY FRAME (2026-07-22, Claude Opus 4.8, dispatch-2026-07-21-opus §1 task 10). Closes the ~10% seasonal drift AND the scene-E `e_in` first-run flap — ONE cause, measured.** Vixy A19/Q10 (§11.48(a)): ***"Yes, I sometimes do jump dates while navigating"*** ⇒ the latch is a defect, not an accepted approximation. Fixing input STALENESS only — the AoI formula and the transition policy are untouched (threshold semantics = A15/A17, Vixy's). Files: `experimentalModule/ModularBody.{cpp,hpp}` (split `updateReach()` out of `updateCache()`, called every frame from `update()`); `harness/scene_e_spine.py` (+5 B15 asserts, 21→26), `harness/b15_{run.sh,aoi_stale.py}` (new); artifacts `harness/artifacts/b15*/`, `.gitignore` +5 lines.
    - **(a) THE LATCH, LOCATED [observed: ModularBody.cpp:289-330 pre-fix; ModularBody.hpp:290-291].** `areaOfInfluence` (the reference-transition threshold consumed by `findBetterReference`, Camera.cpp:377) was computed only inside `updateCache()`, which `update()` calls solely `if (uncached)`; `uncached` is raised ONLY by `setRadius`/`setScaling`/`invalidateCachedState` [observed: ModularBody.hpp:741,752,798] — **never by a date change**. So after the first full cache (launch jd) the AoI froze, even though the positions it derives from (the body's own `eclipticPos` + `forEachVisibleChild`'s `|ecl_child|+subsystemRadius`) are refreshed EVERY frame by `transformParentToBodyPos`. Earth's AoI is Moon-distance-dominated (`subsystemRadius·16 = 1.1·(|ecl_moon|+moon_sub)·16 ≈ 4.3e-2 AU` ≫ the `boundingRadius·128 ≈ 5.5e-3 AU` term), so it tracks the lunar distance — which the launch latch pinned.
    - **(b) STALENESS MEASURED [measured: harness/b15_aoi_stale.py, ref=Earth, surface observer; refAoI dumped from the process vs an offline `updateReach` transcription on the SAME dump's fresh ecl — formula layer, B30-safe].** Launch leg (dump at launch jd) err **0.000%** = the transcription instrument validated. After `date jday 2461233.5` (scene jd) the frozen refAoI **4.805605e-02 AU** vs the current-jd prediction **4.357605e-02 AU** = **10.281% error** — confirms §11.36's recorded "~10%". The frozen value is BYTE-IDENTICAL across five date jumps (launch / scene / +½yr / back / +½yr) = the latch signature; Moon distance drove it (2.665306e-03 launch vs 2.410758e-03 AU scene jd). **No source conflict**: measured 10.281% ≈ the recorded ~10%.
    - **(c) THE FIX + CADENCE — continuous, inside C3.** `updateReach()` (the `subsystemRadius` + `areaOfInfluence` block) is split out of `updateCache()` and called EVERY frame from `update()` (the `else` of `if (uncached)`); the module/radius part stays gated by `uncached` (it changes only on a radius/scaling/module event). **Cadence = continuous, NOT date-jump-event** — chosen because (i) it is threshold-free (an event cadence needs a jd-change threshold = a policy call I do not own, and would miss slow drift over a long session), and (ii) it is strictly inside **C3** *"draw thread never blocks"* / *"never build-on-first-use synchronously in the frame"* [INTENT §2.0:116, §10.2:399]: `updateReach` is a handful of float ops per visible body in the update walk that already runs per frame — no I/O, no allocation, no lock, no first-use build ⇒ cannot block. Post-fix err **0.000%** at every date; the AoI now DIFFERS with season — scene jd 4.357605e-02 vs +½yr 4.858009e-02 = **11.47%** (the transition altitude tracks lunar distance). Reversible pair: back-to-scene 4.357605e-02 == scene, +½yr-from-back 4.858009e-02 == +½yr (path-independent, both jumps from the prior end state).
    - **(d) COST OF THE CONTINUOUS RECOMPUTE [measured: temporary chrono probe in updateReach + per-frame counter in Camera::update, both REMOVED before commit].** ~50 `updateReach` calls/frame (visible bodies), **≤46 ns/call**, **≤2.4 µs/frame** — a CONSERVATIVE UPPER BOUND (two `high_resolution_clock` reads/call dominate the number; the true arithmetic cost is lower). = **~0.014% of a 16.7 ms / 60 fps frame**. Not expensive; no C3 concern.

63. **B17 — view_offset / zoom_offset: CHARACTERISED + SUSPENDED. The row's premise (a projection-space offset applied "at the projection stage") is FALSE at source — old applies view_offset in the NAVIGATOR/VIEW stage as a fov-coupled ROTATION, NEVER in the projector; the new pipeline has NO projection-stage offset hook; and old's mechanism IS the "camera rotation" the row forbids. Projection-space-vs-parity is a Vixy decision (2026-07-22, Claude Opus 4.8, dispatch-2026-07-21-opus §1 task 11). NO product code changed.**
    - **(a) THE TWO "CHANNELS" ARE ONE PARAMETER [observed + measured live].** Config `[navigation] view_offset` (SCS_NAVIGATION/SCK_VIEW_OFFSET [define_key.hpp:43/309]) → `Core::setViewOffset` [core.cpp:494-496]; runtime command `set zoom_offset <value>` (ACP_SC_ZOOM_OFFSET="zoom_offset" [base_command_interface.hpp:566]; `m_set[ACP_SC_ZOOM_OFFSET]=APP_ZOOM_OFFSET` [app_command_init.cpp:314]; case → `stcore->setViewOffset(evalDouble())` [app_command_interface.cpp:1781]) → the **SAME** `Core::setViewOffset`. There is NO separate "zoom_offset" behaviour: `view_offset` is the config-channel spelling and `zoom_offset` the command-channel spelling of ONE clamped [-0.5,0.5] scalar (core.cpp:2114-2117), commented "percent of fov radius" (core.cpp:2110), TUI label "Zoom Offset" (ui_tuiconf.cpp:594) — resolving the DoD "distinguish zoom_offset from view_offset": they are the §2(c) two channels of ONE offset, not two offsets. **Measured** [b17_probe.gdb break on Core::setViewOffset, running DEFAULT/new path]: config fired 2 hits at startup (offset=**0.000000**, config value 0); command `set zoom_offset 0.2/0.35/0` fired 3 hits (offset=**0.200000/0.350000/0.000000**); the `=`-form fires **ZERO** args (the 8× spelling trap — `set NAME VALUE` is SPACE-separated: parseCommand reads command then (key value) pairs [app_command_interface.cpp:144-166]); two BOGUS names (`zoom_ofset`, `zoomoffset`) fired **0** hits.
    - **(b) WHERE IT ACTUALLY APPLIES — the NAVIGATOR/VIEW stage, NOT the projector [observed; grep: `view_offset` in navigator.{hpp,cpp}, core.cpp, coreLink.hpp, ui_tuiconf.cpp, define_key.hpp, base_command_interface.hpp, app_command_interface.cpp — NEVER in projector.cpp].** `Core::setViewOffset` [core.cpp:2111] calls ONLY the old navigator: `navigation->setViewOffset(off)` (stores) + `navigation->setLocalVision(InitViewPos)`. TWO application sites, both in Navigator, both scaled by `view_offset_transition`: (1) `setLocalVision` [navigator.cpp:159] `local_vision = yrotation(−view_offset·(π/2)·transition)·_pos` — a scene-space (fixed 90°-scaled, fov-INDEPENDENT) re-aim of the look vector; (2) `updateViewMat` [navigator.cpp:309, per frame] `mat_local_to_eye = xrotation(view_offset·(fov/2)·(π/180)·transition)·mat_local_to_eye` — a fov-COUPLED rotation of the local→eye VIEW matrix. `view_offset_transition` is 0 at rest, driven to 1 only by an auto_move zoom-in / back to 0 by zoom-out [navigator.cpp:73-78] ⇒ **the offset is ARMED by a commanded view move, inert at a fresh un-moved view** (hence "zoom_offset"). Corrects/refines §11.45(d)'s "the yrotation view offset": the PRIMARY per-frame site is the xrotation; the yrotation is a secondary re-aim.
    - **(c) "PROJECTION-SPACE" IS IN EFFECT ONLY, VIA A VIEW-MATRIX ROTATION [derived].** The primary site (updateViewMat xrotation) is ∝ fov/2; on the fisheye transfer (r = θ/halfFov) a rotation of view_offset·(fov/2) shifts the projected center by view_offset·(fov/2)/(fov/2) = **view_offset FRACTION of the dome radius — fov-INDEPENDENT** (the "percent of fov radius"). SCREEN result = a constant fractional dome shift (projection-space-in-effect), but the MECHANISM re-aims the optical axis (the look direction moves by view_offset·(fov/2)) and the visible cone ROTATES in world. A fixed-angle re-derivation would give a fov-DEPENDENT shift (the actual scene-space trap). Old is thus neither pure scene-space nor pure projection-space: it is a fov-coupled VIEW rotation + a secondary scene-space yrotation of the look vector.
    - **(d) THE NEW PIPELINE HAS NO PROJECTION-STAGE OFFSET HOOK [observed].** The new-path projection is GPU fisheye (custom_project.glsl `fisheyeProjectCustom` + fisheye.glsl `fisheyeProject`), replicated across ~8 shader functions, driven ONLY by `ModelViewMatrix` + `clipping_fov = (zNear, zFar, halfFov)` — no NDC-offset input in any of them. The only CPU projection authority (ProjectionTransfer.hpp) computes radial transfer for screenPos consumers (halo/hint/pointer/label anchors), NOT the render matrices. Camera [Camera.hpp] exposes `viewRotation() = Z(heading+π)·X(π/2−alt)·Z(az−π/2)·F` + `placementRotation` — NO offset member; `Renderer::getClippingFov()` returns a Vec3f (no room for an offset). A TRUE projection-space view_offset (image slides on the dome, optical axis UNCHANGED — the DoD discriminator) **cannot be expressed in the Camera matrices** (any matrix-level offset IS a rotation ⇒ changes the look direction); it would need an NDC-offset uniform added to the SHARED GPU projection shaders — which ALSO touch the OLD path and would DIVERGE from old at the dome edge (a pure NDC shift leaves a blank crescent + clips where old's fov-coupled rotation FILLS the disc with a re-aimed cone). The Vulkan-viewport-offset alternative is rejected: it is a GLOBAL state that shifts UI/overlays too (old shifts only the scene view matrix, not the ortho2D UI).
    - **(e) THE CONFLICT (reported, not resolved) → SUSPENDED FOR VIXY.** **Source A** (old-path implementation = the parity spec): view_offset is a fov-coupled VIEW-matrix ROTATION [navigator.cpp:159,309] — it DOES change the optical-axis world direction; the visible cone ROTATES; armed by a commanded move; and the tester's tilted-dome shows use THIS today. **Source B** (this row's spec / §13.B B17 / the DoD): view_offset is PROJECTION-SPACE, "must NOT re-derive as a camera rotation", optical axis must stay FIXED. On flat FISHEYE they give the same center displacement but differ at the dome edge and in the look-direction observable (Camera forward moves under A, fixed under B). **Irreconcilable without a Vixy decision** — implementing A defies the DoD; implementing B is a new architectural shader hook that touches the old path and diverges from old at the edge (user-visible-semantics beyond old's surface). Per the row's own "Where to stop" (*"if the new path lacks the projection-center hook … suspend rather than approximate it as a rotation"*) and the I-rules (an improvised architectural / user-visible-semantics decision = failure): **SUSPENDED** (question + 3 options in §13.B B17). No product code changed — the seam stays OLD-ONLY (the `render_path = old` fallback, §11.48, remains the tilted-dome mitigation). config.ini md5 **03fbee59bc3ec506c58f0a3f1e1d73df** in/out (no key added; migration contract untouched). Instruments: `harness/b17_{probe.gdb,seam.py,run.sh}` (new); artifacts `harness/artifacts/b17/` (gitignored).

64. **B22 — SYSTEM-COLLAPSE CROSS-FADE at the resolved↔dot threshold: MECHANISM IMPLEMENTED + COST BOUNDED (~22.6 ns/frame, 0 added draw calls); the composed-screen RENDER is SUSPENDED (§6.9 runtime-unexercised, source-re-verified). (2026-07-22, Claude Opus 4.8, dispatch-2026-07-21-opus §1 task 12).** Vixy A15/Q9 (§11.48(b)): cross-fade the collapse *"if possible without costing too much"* — **the cost bound is the decision input**; the threshold constants stay OPEN (A15, tester-judged against a working fade). Files: `experimentalModule/ModularBody.{hpp,cpp}` (`drawAlpha` static + `SYSTEM_COLLAPSE_CROSSFADE_BAND` constant + the `cmag *= drawAlpha` scale in `drawHaloCore`), `experimentalModule/ModularSystem.cpp` (`drawNested` cross-fade branch). Harness: `harness/b22_crossfade.py` (ramp verification, 11/11 exit 0); instrument `b22_halocost.cpp` (cost microbench, scratchpad — NOT committed). Artifacts `harness/artifacts/b22*/` (gitignored).
    - **(a) THE HARD SWITCH, LOCATED + CHARACTERISED [observed: ModularSystem.cpp:558 pre-fix; ModularBody.hpp:85].** A nested ModularSystem drawn as an entry of an enclosing system (`drawSystemBodies`→`drawNested`) switched on ONE px comparison: `screenSize·2·viewportRadius >= SYSTEM_VISIBILITY_SUBSYSTEM_SIZE` (16). **RESOLVED** (px≥T) = the full interior — `updateSystem` (sort + set the node's star as light) + `computeShadows` + `drawSystemBodies` (every child's own `draw`, per-child screenSize-gated). **DOT** (px<T) = ONE `drawStarProxy`→`drawHaloCore` halo (mag `-26.73−2.5·log10(d²)` at the NODE's fresh distance, star disc floor `getScaledRadius/(d·halfFov)·2·vpR`, star halo color, no satellite rules). **The pop [derived]**: the halo MAGNITUDE is nearly continuous across T (drawStarProxy's node-distance mag == the resolved star's `computeMagnitude` star-branch `-26.73−2.5·log10(d²)` once `updateSystem` refreshes the star, node≈star for a distant dot); the discontinuity is (i) the whole interior (star + children halos) APPEARING at full brightness in one frame while (ii) the proxy dot VANISHES, plus a small `screen_r` disc-floor step. **The pop's on-screen magnitude is NOT measured — the path is runtime-unexercised (e).**
    - **(b) THE MECHANISM — a cost-minimal cross-fade over a band [T, T+B).** `ModularBody::drawAlpha` (new static, default 1.0) is multiplied into every halo's `cmag` in `drawHaloCore` (the last op before the skip rule + `drawHalo`); `drawNested` sets it across the band (save/set/restore, multiplicative so nested-in-band compounds). Over [T, T+B): the RESOLVED interior runs for px≥T **exactly as the hard switch did** (its expensive draw region is UNCHANGED ⇒ NO resolved cost added), with its halos scaled by `t=(px−T)/B` ⇒ they FADE IN; the DOT also runs, scaled by `(1−t)` ⇒ it FADES OUT. This makes the **only** added work one `drawStarProxy` per band frame. Band width = **`SYSTEM_COLLAPSE_CROSSFADE_BAND` [ModularBody.hpp]**, a **NAMED TUNABLE constant — A15's set, NOT tuned here**: default = `SYSTEM_VISIBILITY_SUBSYSTEM_SIZE/2` (8 px, the fade completes over +50% apparent size), derived FROM the threshold so it is not an independent magic number; the value is A15/tester territory (a tester cannot judge the width without seeing the fade — which is exactly why the fade had to exist first, A15's ordering).
    - **(c) THE RAMP VERIFIED at the decidable formula layer [measured: harness/b22_crossfade.py, 11/11 checks, exit 0; reads the constants from ModularBody.hpp so a re-tune re-validates].** Transcribes `drawNested`'s exact branch + the `cmag *= drawAlpha` scale. **Endpoints (DoD 3) match the pure representations**: px=T ⇒ resolved α=0 + dot α=1 == pure dot; px=T+B ⇒ resolved α=1 + no dot == pure resolved — and these are **pixel-exact by construction [derived]**: a resolved halo at α=0 emits `color·cmag·0 = 0` under **BLEND_ADD** [PipelineRegistry.cpp:899] ⇒ zero framebuffer contribution (skipped or black-additive), and at 16–24 px system size the children are sub-pixel so NO disc appears (discs are not α-scaled — the flagged residual, only reachable for pathologically compact authored systems, A30). **Continuous at both boundaries**; **resolved α monotonic ↑ (fades in), dot α monotonic ↓ (fades out); α_resolved+α_dot=1 within the band (a true cross-dissolve)**. **Reversible pair (DoD 5)**: α is a PURE function of px (no hysteresis) ⇒ crossing OUTWARD (resolved→dot) and INWARD (dot→resolved) give identical α at each px, second crossing from the first's end state bit-identical — verified on a shared grid. **Inert outside the band** (px<T pure dot, px≥T+B pure resolved, both drawAlpha=1).
    - **(d) COST — the deliverable's point [measured: b22_halocost.cpp, g++ -O2, 1e8 iters ×2 runs].** The cross-fade adds, per frame and ONLY while px is inside the band (a transient during a zoom), exactly **ONE `drawHaloCore` call** (the fading dot) = **22.4–22.8 ns/call** = **~0.00014 % of a 16.7 ms / 60 fps frame** (a conservative upper bound — the two `steady_clock` reads dominate the count; the pure arithmetic is less), **+ ZERO added GPU draw calls** (halos are `batchPush`'d [PipelineRegistry.cpp:927] to ONE instanced draw — the dot is one 24-byte instance in the existing batch), **+ one float multiply per resolved halo** (`cmag *= drawAlpha`, negligible). The RESOLVED subsystem draw is NOT run any earlier than the hard switch ran it (region [T,∞) unchanged) ⇒ the "both representations rendered" band cost is the pre-existing resolved cost + one halo. **Outside the band: ~0 added** (two float comparisons → the original hard branch) — inert, as DoD 4 requires. **Verdict [derived]: NOT too costly** — but the verdict is **Vixy's to ratify** now that it is measured.

So body-scoped runtime overrides (scale, hide/show, colour — the B16/B29 uniform ledger) are part of the saved state, AND the save persists across process lifetime (to disk), AND scripts can push **persistent bodies** that survive sessions (a resource-saving mechanism: authored once, kept). The harness verifies the FRAME CONVERSION, not value-correctness — value-correctness comes ONLY from this cross-source agreement.
    - **(b) Iapetus PILOT — verified end to end [fetched + measured].** α₀=**318.16**, δ₀=**75.03** (J2000; pure secular pole, NO periodic terms ⇒ the J2000 value IS the verbatim constant). Loaded → the loader marks `absoluteTiltFrame=True`, converts to obliquity **17.276199°** / ascNode **139.607939°**; the rendered absolute axis == the declared IAU J2000 pole to **0.000008°**, and is **0.000000° across two dates** (J2000 vs +10 yr, Saturn swept ~122° of orbit) — Saturn-position-independent, as an absolute pole must be. Discriminator intact: the parent_relative interpretation of the same pole is **19.62°** off. Commutator moved **30.87° → 21.36°** (the 21.36° is Iapetus's REAL inclination to Saturn's frame, not zero — Iapetus is an inclined outer satellite; the residual old↔new is now the CLEAN NAMED absolute-vs-parent-relative divergence, §11.68(b)'s prediction). `harness/b14_run.sh` + `b14_analyze.py axis Iapetus Saturn --ra 318.16 --de 75.03`.
    - **(c) The 14 landed values [fetched: pck00011 + pck00010 + Archinal report → value]. Iapetus = verbatim J2000 constant; the other 13 = the fetched formula EVALUATED at J2000 (`scratchpad/eval_poles.py`, reproducible arithmetic on the fetched coefficients — periodic nutation terms at d=0), a `[derived-from-fetched]` value, NOT recall.** (α₀, δ₀ deg, J2000 equatorial): Iapetus 318.16/75.03 · Amalthea 267.2508/64.3867 · Thebe 267.2013/63.6693 · Juliet 257.2684/−15.1367 · Portia 257.2221/−15.1608 · Rosalind 257.1984/−15.4384 · Belinda 257.2806/−15.1861 · Puck 257.0919/−15.4127 · Naiad 302.9178/39.0390 · Thalassa 299.5156/43.1000 · Despina 299.3427/42.8707 · Galatea 299.2660/42.9077 · Larissa 299.1775/42.7373 · Proteus 299.2134/42.4321. Written as `rot_pole_ra`/`rot_pole_de` + `rot_frame = absolute_pole`; garbage `rot_obliquity`/`rot_equator_ascending_node` REMOVED (§11.68(d), I2). **Precision note**: the inner satellites' periodic terms are published to ~0.01°; the 4-decimal writes carry the J2000 nutation offset but the true pole is time-varying (see (e)).
    - **(d) Commutator A/B — success signal is per-moon, count-metric is inert here [measured, baseline backup vs landed].** `orientation_check.py` count **17/48 → 17/48 UNCHANGED**, P-d 0.0000° both — because "parity-RESTORED" is defined `cur>0.01 AND fix<1e-3` (commutator driven to ~0 = ZERO own tilt), and the landed moons have REAL non-zero tilts (all >1e-3), so they stay in the "divergent" bucket. The SUCCESS is the per-moon commutator VALUE dropping from garbage to physical, on **exactly the 14 corrected moons (75 others unchanged, verified)**: Uranus inner **154.7452° → 0.03–0.21°** (Juliet/Portia/Rosalind/Belinda/Puck — worst garbage because Uranus's pole is near the ecliptic), Neptune inner **54.3137° → 0.08–3.13°** (Naiad's 3.13° residual = its large ±6.5° N1 nutation the static slot can't hold), Jupiter inner **5.4719° → 0.18/0.30°** (Amalthea/Thebe), Iapetus **30.87° → 21.36°**. The §11.68(b) refinement holds ("63–155° ≠ exactly the cluster"): the still-large rows are the NON-landed irregulars + non-cluster Saturn garbage-tilt moons.
    - **(e) W0 (`rot_rotation_offset`) SUSPENDED FOR VIXY — referential mismatch, NOT written [observed + derived].** The pole slot is safe to write verbatim because the loader CONVERTS it (J2000-equ → ecliptic, `ModularSystem.cpp:737-748`, verified). `rot_rotation_offset` is added RAW to the spin [ModularBody.hpp:306] about a pole/equatorial frame whose meridian reference is the body-equator node **on the ecliptic** (`ascendingNode = ra_ecliptic+90°`), whereas the IAU **W₀** is measured from the node on the **ICRF equator** — a genuine per-body node-difference offset. Evidence the file's own convention is NOT raw IAU W₀: Mars file offset **136.005** vs IAU W₀ **176.630**, Jupiter **107** vs **284.95** (the node correction, largest where the pole is far from the ecliptic pole; the Moon matches, 38 vs 38.32, because its pole is near the ecliptic pole). Writing raw IAU W₀ into that slot is exactly Vixy's "good values → invalid measurement" (§11.49(e)/A31). The harness/commutator verify the POLE axis, NOT the meridian, so leaving `rot_rotation_offset` unchanged does not affect any verified metric. **Fetched IAU W₀ (deg, for Vixy to convert or accept meridian-as-is)**: Iapetus 355.2 · Amalthea 231.67 · Thebe 8.56 · Juliet 302.56 · Portia 25.03 · Rosalind 314.90 · Belinda 297.46 · Puck 91.24 · Naiad 254.06 · Thalassa 102.06 · Despina 306.51 · Galatea 258.09 · Larissa 179.41 · Proteus 93.38.
    - **(f) STOPPED-for-no-pole — the other 14 shipped cluster bodies [fetched: absent from ALL THREE sources].** The irregular/chaotic satellites carry NO pole in pck00011, pck00010, or the report text (only size/physical data — non-synchronous or chaotic rotation, no defined static pole): Jupiter **Himalia, Elara, Carme, Pasiphae**; Uranus **Caliban, Sycorax, Prospero, Setebos**; Neptune **Nereid(Neried), Halimede, Sao, Laomedeia, Psamathe, Neso** (Nereid explicitly chaotic). Left templated with garbage tilt in place — writing any pole for them WOULD be confabulation. 14 landed + 14 stopped = the 28 shipped. **Hyperion** (chaotic tumbler, also absent from all sources) is a non-cluster garbage-tilt Saturn moon (§11.68 §5) — stays suspended.
    - **(g) Pole-drift / periodic-nutation unrepresentable [§11.49(e), flagged per task item 4].** Iapetus's secular drift (dα/dt=−3.949°/cy, dδ/dt=−1.143°/cy) and every inner satellite's periodic nutation (Naiad worst: ±6.5°, ~210-day period) are a 2-DOF pole motion that does NOT map onto the loader's single scalar `rot_precession_rate` (a 1-DOF node precession). The written poles are the J2000 SNAPSHOT — correct at that epoch only. Not omitted; flagged.
    - **(h) Both files edited, encodings preserved, no-regression [measured].** `data/default_ssystem.ini` (tracked, committed) still **ASCII**; loaded `~/.spacecrafter/ssystem.ini` still **ISO-8859** (the one French-comment latin-1 byte preserved); both LF; diff = ONLY the intended per-body lines (14 bodies × {remove 1–2 garbage, add 3 pole} — 42 ins / 27 del shipped). Loaded backed up to `ssystem.ini.b14bak` (md5 `fb87a774…`) before editing; new loaded md5 `62239656…`. Build **green** (data-only ⇒ binary mtime stays 09:39, nothing to recompile). Scenes A–D (`predict.py`): P1 ≤1.67e-16, P2 ≤8.47e-08, P3 old==new ≤1.08e-05°, P4 **3.07/3.71/13.58 km**, P5 ≤9.64e-08 named — corrected moons not in these scenes ⇒ unchanged. Scene E **26/26 OK / 0 FAIL**. **0 VUID** (debug_layer). config.ini md5 `03fbee59…` in==out (init_fov 180→340→180, restored after process death). Values/instruments: `scratchpad/eval_poles.py`, harness `b14_*`.

70. **Round-2 tester answers propagated (2026-07-22, recorded by Claude Fable 5).** Source: the ten live re-asks (R1, R3–R11, R13) + the two no-longer-questions (R2, R12) of `USER_QUESTIONS_ROUND2.md`, answered inline by the tester and relayed by Vixy; the verbatim answers stay in that file (raw record), this entry is what each answer *decides*. Same authority boundary as §11.48: an operator answer is decisive on *what the product must do* (and, where it reports what existing shows/scripts do, decisive by §2(b) immutability), never on mechanism (which stays derived). **One answer OVERTURNS a prior derivation** (R4 vs B10 (c) — the inward-asymptote), recorded as SUPERSEDED not as a conflict, per the dispatch mapping; **no NEEDS-VIXY tension surfaced**. R12 needs no propagation (informational, already reflected §11.52(c)).
    - **(a) R1 → A28 RESOLVED** [tester: *"The shadow of the planet on the rings should be activated everytime. Only desactivated for test purpose."*]. Ring shadows (Saturn's rings on the globe AND the globe on the rings) are **ALWAYS ON** — there is **no operator show/hide control**; the switch the question named is a dev/test-only gate that retires with the old path. Round-1's "must be separated" + "eclipse default on" stand. A28's remaining §11.50(a) residual ("does the new path expose operator show/hide for eclipse vs ring shadows — neither reading may be assumed") is answered: **no operator control, always on**. Row A28 → RESOLVED.
    - **(b) R3 → B4 design-deciding half ANSWERED** [tester: *"We use mostly (3), but we are lacking (2), having an object attached to a body keeping its angle to the planet which is very important for future scripts. (1) has no real interest… only useful in the 'Universe' mode… For the 'Galaxy' mode, the stars will move through time in the future."*]. The anchor kinds actually used: **(3) point-on-orbit-around-a-body is the primary**; **(2) body-attached-keeping-its-angle is a NEEDED GAP** (currently lacking, important for future scripts); **(1) fixed-point-in-space is useful only in Universe mode** (nothing moves there). B4's design-deciding half closes; implementation stays Fable-territory (port vs anchor.ini).
    - **(c) R4 → B10, TWO parts** [tester: *"A zone we fly into and also for transparent bodies, if the behavior change, we will adapt the scripts. We should stop if going to the lowest altitude. Slowing down will be annoying as it'll take time to go out."*]. (i) **Purpose of the two-body patch = fly-into zones + transparent/gaseous bodies** ⇒ the `datum_radius`/`ground_radius` split is now a **CONFIRMATION**, not merely a derivation (datum = nominal surface for atmosphere/landscape; ground = 0 enterable for a fly-into/transparent body); scripts will adapt if behaviour changes. (ii) At the lowest allowed altitude the observer must **STOP-AND-HOLD, not asymptotically slow down** — this **OVERTURNS** B10's derived *"inward must stay pure-multiplicative so it asymptotes to the ground and never crosses it"*: the tester wants a hard stop/hold at `ground_radius`, not an asymptote (*"Slowing down will be annoying… take time to go out"*). Marked **SUPERSEDED by R4** in B10; the anti-stuck OUTWARD-escape floor is unaffected (R4 endorses fast escape). The floor VALUE (`MIN_MOVEMENT_SPEED` 0.125) stays Vixy's (B10 carve-out (a), still open).
    - **(d) R5 → A17 RESOLVED** [tester: *"The biggest should win because it'll be the brightest in 99% of cases due to surface magnitude."*]. When brightest/biggest/main disagree inside the pick tolerance, **BIGGEST wins**. A17's disambiguation residual closes.
    - **(e) R6 → A18/B21 RESOLVED** [tester: *"Last selected."*]. Descent from far (galactic distance) aims at the **LAST SELECTED body**, not merely into the general vicinity. A18's far/galactic-distance residual closes; B21's far-distance case (which the row said must not be improvised) is unblocked.
    - **(f) R7 → A4/B23 RESOLVED** [tester: *"Planet rotation axis is the toggle to make it appear onto the planet. It must stay associated. Visible at any altitude."*]. The planet grid **stays coupled to the rotation-axis toggle** (NO independent on/off) and is **visible at any altitude** (the old >10 km near-surface-only regime does NOT return). CONFIRMS B23's carve-out (§11.57 already keyed tropics/polar to the sky-line flags, with no independent toggle and no >10 km regime). A4's tester-facing residuals close; the ±30/±60-generic-parallels and Sun-gets-no-grid items remain as non-Vixy implementation detail.
    - **(g) R8 → A3/B29/B31 INFORMED (no change forced now)** [tester: *"It must reset to the body default or color asked in the script. The user must reload the rule to be applied to it as well."*]. Color-on-reload PREFERENCE: reset to the body default (or the script-asked colour), and a runtime rule must be **re-applied on reload**. This MATCHES the new path's current reset-on-reload behaviour (§11.65), so nothing is forced now; it is design input for the exhaustive-save/override-ledger row **B31** — the "rule" the user re-applies is exactly what B31's ledger would persist. Noted on B29 + B31 as informing, not a mandate.
    - **(h) R9 → A27 RESOLVED** [tester: *"Decide by built-in rules. Until they land somewhere else or if the user decide to change landscape by script."*]. Landscape association is decided by **engine built-in rules** (NOT the body's data file), and a hand/script pick **sticks until the next landing or a script change**. A27's residual (does the association live in the data + "sticks until when") closes.
    - **(i) R10 → A29/B27 completeness input** [tester: *"Nothing will have to change if we go back."*]. The linked-behaviour set ("Earth/Moon/Sun behavior") must be such that **returning changes nothing** — the code-extracted hardcoded-behavior list is the completeness target and the tester expects no surprise on return; anything that WOULD change on return is a gap in B27's inventory.
    - **(j) R11 → B17 CONFIRMS both channels** [tester: *"Yes, it can change during a show."*]. view_offset **is changed at runtime during shows** ⇒ both channels (config + command) are needed (already built regardless). Does NOT resolve B17's open projection-space-vs-camera-rotation choice (still suspended, §11.63).
    - **(k) R13 → B20 DIRECTION (answers the R13 question posed in §11.66(e))** [tester: *"The anchor must hold when we get back to this mode. So memorize it for each mode."*]. The B20 anchored-mode-descent finding (§11.59, deferred at §11.66(e)) gets its direction: the anchor **must be remembered PER MODE and restored on return**, so an out-and-back round trip lands you re-anchored where you left. Implementation remains an escalation-policy/§6.9 change (a work item, not this wave) — whoever does §6.9/B31 inherits this intent.
    - **(l) R2 (no-longer-question) → A9 tester-CONFIRMED** [tester: *"Yes."* — the camera falls back to the **parent body** on drop, both cases]. Confirms A9's resolution (§11.49(a), already handled by `ModularBodyPtr::redirect` transparently — zero code). No new work; recorded as tester-confirmed against the veto window the question offered.

71. **B10 landed — `datum_radius` + `ground_radius` supersede the `solid` flag (2026-07-22, Claude Opus 4.8).** Two per-body navigation scalars, **both defaulting to `radius`** ⇒ bit-identical to today for every shipped body and legacy script; the split expresses the enterable/transparent body (datum=ground=0, R4 §11.70(c)) and the terrain-clearance body (datum=radius, ground=radius·1.002) a single reference could not. Full derivation §5.2; scope (i)–(vi) of §13.B B10.
    - **(i) members + the CTOR-COPY** [observed: ModularBody.cpp:69] — the omission this whole entry traced to (the old boolean was inert because the ctor never copied it): `datumRadius`/`groundRadius` raw members are in the ctor member-init list, `scaledDatumRadius`/`scaledGroundRadius` are recomputed at the single scaling site [observed: ModularBody.cpp:299-307, updateCache] beside `scaledRadius`. createInfo defaults to `radius` via `Utility::strToFloat(param["datum_radius"], radius)/AU` [observed: ModularSystem.cpp:815-823] — the SAME expression as `.radius`, so `datumRadius==radius` bit-exactly (`strToFloat` returns the default unchanged on an absent key [observed: utility.cpp:409-415]).
    - **(ii) `getAltitudeReference()` → `scaledDatumRadius`, no branch** [observed: ModularBody.hpp]. All 9 consumers (Camera ctor/distanceToReference/warpToBody/moveTo/setAltitude, EnvironmentManager, LandscapeEnv, AtmosphereEnv) ride it unchanged ⇒ landscape/atmosphere thresholds key off `datum_radius` [observed: LandscapeEnv.cpp:14-19, AtmosphereEnv.cpp:11, EnvironmentManager.cpp:73].
    - **(iii)+(iv)+(iv-b) the shared `proximityFactor(bool escaping)` authority (I2)** [observed: Camera.cpp] consumed by `multAlt` + `moveRelLon` + `moveRelLat`: base = `distance − scaledGroundRadius` (== `distanceToReference()` for a default body, bit-identical), floored to `ANTISTUCK_ESCAPE_FLOOR·scaledRadius` on the OUTWARD/lateral (escaping) step so height 0 is never a fixed point (§5.18). **Free-mode descent CLAMP** added in `update()` — the FIRST descent floor in Camera (§5.2 finding ii): free-mode `position` held at `scaledGroundRadius` (R4 **STOP-AND-HOLD**, not asymptote), gated `!isSystem()` (a system has no landable surface — the EnvironmentManager onBody rule, I4) so flying into a galaxy/solar-system stays free; ground=0 ⇒ no clamp (descent to centre).
    - **(v) `altitudeRelativeToRadius` fully deleted** — member, createInfo field, its only data reader (`isFalse(param["solid"])`) [observed: grep `altitudeRelativeToRadius`/`param["solid"]`/`scaledInnerRadius` over src = 0 code hits]. **Three factory system/galaxy nodes** (Universe/MilkyWay/per-system) had authored `.altitudeRelativeToRadius = false` [was ssystem_factory.cpp:95/114/322] — the ONE place the flag's non-default was set (center-relative INTENT). The inert ctor never realized it (today's runtime = `getAltitudeReference()==scaledRadius`), so migrating to `datum=ground=radius` is **bit-identical to that runtime** (Universe/per-system radius 0 ⇒ 0 either way; MilkyWay keeps 3.2e9 AU). The `=false` intent (only MilkyWay bites) is SUSPENDED below, not silently chosen (I6/never-specialize-silently).
    - **(vi)** createInfo fields + `getAltitudeReference` + both keys header-documented (I1); script recipe = one body with `ground_radius 0` (enterable) replaces the two-body anchor-and-enter patch.
    - **BIT-IDENTICAL regression (primary gate) [measured: harness/b10_nav.py, baseline@HEAD 27195532 vs post]**: Earth `moveto altitude {100,50000,0}` distances IDENTICAL to 10 sig figs (a0 4.263523440e-05 AU both), DefaultTest (radius 6000, no keys) IDENTICAL, ClearTest anchored altitudes IDENTICAL (datum=radius ⇒ legacy-exact). Config.ini restored byte-identical (md5 03fbee59…); ssystem.ini untouched (runtime `body action load`, no file edits).
    - **DISCRIMINATING cases (the row's TRAP-avoiding set) [measured: b10_nav.py]**: EnterTest (datum=ground=0) `moveto altitude 0` → distance **6000.000 km → 0.000 km (CENTRE)**; altitude 100 m → 0.100 km, 100 km → 100.000 km (from centre). ClearTest (datum=6000, ground=6012) free-mode `moveto altitude 0` → |pos| **6000.000 km → 6012.000 km** = ground_radius exactly (HARD STOP, not asymptote; baseline had no clamp); reversible pair ×2 identical (desc→6012 hold, asc→6100 escape, twice — 2nd entry from the 1st exit's state).
    - **Anti-stuck floor [derived + measured on the formula]**: Earth height 0, scaledRadius 4.2635e-5 AU (6378.1 km); `proximityFactor(false)=0` (the STUCK fixed point pre-fix), `proximityFactor(true)=1e-6·scaledR=4.264e-11 AU=6.38 m`; `multAlt(1.1)` outward step 0.638 m/call vs 0 ⇒ escapable in every direction. Live multAlt/moveRelLon demonstration is BLOCKED (finding below): Camera::multAlt has zero callers in the tree; moveRelLon/Lat are wired only to UI keys (not TCP), so escapability is argued from the shared floored authority + a measured step (the row's fallback).
    - **No-regression [measured]**: scene E **26/26 OK** (the free-mode ladder exercises the clamp with ref=Sun/systems — all pass, clamp inert at AoI scale, systems gated off; the mars-landing EXACT-position assert 2.270821e-05 AU passes ⇒ positions unchanged). A–D **P4 dDist ≤35.2 km** (Mars; inner planets 0.001–35 km, scaling with distance as the class predicts), **orientation 17/48** (MEASURED post-B14, not assumed), **P-d 0.0000**. Positions/orientations are orthogonal to datum/ground (they don't feed the dispatchUpdate walk).
    - **SUSPENDED FOR VIXY** (carve-outs — decisions not mine): **(a) the FLOOR VALUE** — `ANTISTUCK_ESCAPE_FLOOR = 1e-6` (radius-fraction) is a DEFENSIBLE PLACEHOLDER (≈6.4 m on Earth; escapes in ~1-2 s without wrecking surface nav), deliberately NOT `MIN_MOVEMENT_SPEED`'s 0.125 (≈797 km, an AoI-traversal SPEED — a different concept/unit). Open: one constant with MIN_MOVEMENT_SPEED (re-unit it) or two (as landed)? **(b) DATA-KEY vs COMMAND word order** — data keys kept `datum_radius`/`ground_radius`; the command was requested `radius datum`/`radius ground` (Q12). NO runtime command added this row (spelling undecided ⇒ nothing to spell); the keys flow through the existing `body action load` param map. **(c) MilkyWay center-relative intent** — keep 3.2e9 AU (bit-identical, landed) or honour the authored `=false` (`datum_radius=ground_radius=0`)? A user-visible galaxy-scale free-mode navigation change; one line if wanted.
    - **Findings recorded, not fixed (out of scope)**: `Camera::multAlt` is UNWIRED (0 callers — the new-path keyboard altitude control is not dual-routed; old core.cpp:1737 `observatory->multAltitude` has no `Camera::instance->multAlt` sibling); free-mode `distance` member is STALE (not updated per-frame in free mode) so `proximityFactor`/`distanceToReference` free-mode velocity uses the free-mode-entry distance — PRE-EXISTING, kept for parity (the descent clamp uses live `position.length()`).
    - **B21 inherits**: the single `proximityFactor()` authority (its view-directed descent rides it) + the free-mode descent clamp; R6 far-distance "aim at the LAST SELECTED body" (§11.70(e)) sequences after this. Harness: `harness/b10_run.sh` + `harness/b10_nav.py`; artifacts `harness/artifacts/b10_{baseline,post,sceneE,AD}`.

72. **B21 landed — VIEW-DIRECTED FREE DESCENT: in free flight "down" points along the VIEW RAY near a body and at the LAST SELECTED body when far (2026-07-22, Claude Opus 4.8).** New descent-geometry authority `Camera::descend(float coef)` (`multAlt` forwards to it, I2) + the command-reachable driver `camera action descend coef <c>` (the geometry was UI-key-only — B10 §11.71 finding). Rides B10's proximityFactor/clamp and changes ONLY the descent DIRECTION, never WHEN a reference transition fires (dispatch §2 carve-out: the escalation/anchor policy is B20/§6.9 territory). All measurements: `harness/b21_descent.py` (fresh launch, FISHEYE init_fov=340, numeric/vector layer per dispatch §2, `body action dual_dump`).
    - **(a) Authority + driver [observed: Camera.{hpp,cpp}, coreLink.hpp:857, app_command_interface.cpp commandCamera].** `descend(coef)` (coef<1 descends, coef>1 ascends) replaces `multAlt`'s untested free-mode body — which was **sign-INVERTED AND used the free-mode-STALE `distance` member** (§11.71 finding (v)/(995); 0 callers, so never exercised). Anchored keeps the legacy proximity-scaled radial altitude verbatim. `camera action descend coef <c>` → `coreLink->cameraDescend` → `Camera::descend`; new-path only (old free navigation is the anchor-point observatory — nothing to mirror, same seam as `camera action free_mode`, §11.36). Tokens `W_DESCEND`/`W_COEF`.
    - **(b) NEAR-FIELD = the VIEW RAY, on LIVE geometry [measured].** Eye-frame ray–sphere: cast origin+s·(0,0,−1) against the reference's ground sphere (centre = `getObservedPosition()`, radius = `getScaledGroundRadius()`), move (1−coef) of the way to the near hit; ray-misses / sphere-behind fall back to a radial step toward the centre. Does NOT read the stale `distance` (proximityFactor keeps it for lateral-velocity parity, §11.71). Sign confirmed by measurement: descend LOWERS the observer — |pos| **6200.000→6119.999→6072.000→6043.200→6025.920→6015.552 km** monotonically toward ground 6000 (coef 0.6, DescB radius 6000).
    - **(c) VIEW-DIRECTED DISCRIMINATOR PROVEN [measured].** Observer at |pos|=6200 km above DescB, view azimuth ±35° off the (tracked-then-frozen) centre-line, descend ×10 coef 0.6: the two views LAND AT DIFFERENT surface points **(5999.6, ±140.4, −0.0) km — |ΔE|=280.70 km, 2.680° surface separation, 140.4 km / 1.34° off the sub-observer point per view**. The CENTRE-directed control (`moveto altitude 60000`, view-independent by construction) lands at (6060.0, 0, 0) km for BOTH azimuths — **|ΔE|=0.0000 km, 0.00000°**. Descent depends on the view; the centre-directed control does not — exactly the discriminator the row demanded (a centre-directed descent lands at the sub-observer point regardless of view).
    - **(d) FAR CASE = the LAST SELECTED body [measured, R6 §11.70(e)].** At a SYSTEM reference (no landable surface underfoot) descend aims at `getSelected()`: `moveEyeRel(getObservedPosition()·(coef−1))` moves the observer (1−coef)·|d| TOWARD it. Instrument added: **`selDist`** in `Camera::dumpTrace` = `|getSelected()->getObservedPosition()|` (the exact quantity the far-case moves along; frame-independent scalar — a system-distance dump can NOT read a runtime-loaded target off the old-system-driven per-body list, which collapses at galactic distance). Two off-centre targets FarA (ecl +y 12000 AU) / FarB (ecl +z 20000 AU), **selected while NEAR** (the selection persists through the fly-out + escalation; `select planet X` needs the body in the current old-path system, which the galactic view has collapsed), flown to `sun_aoi·1.07` (**ref=SolarSystem, isSystem verified**): **selDist FOLLOWS the selection — FarA 3.80894e5 AU vs FarB 3.77171e5 AU (differ 3723 AU)**; descend **coef 0.96** (small step ⇒ ref stays SolarSystem, no de-escalation re-basing) drives **selDist ×0.960000 EXACTLY for BOTH** targets ⇒ the observer moves EXACTLY toward the selected body (a centre-directed step would NOT hit coef for an off-centre body). At coef 0.5 the same holds as an APPROACH (selDist ×~0.47–0.49; the residual = the SolarSystem→Sun de-escalation that fires once a half-step drops the observer below sun_aoi — the reference-transition machinery, NOT the geometry, and NOT touched here). The existing distance-driven transition then captures the body and the near-field view-ray descent takes over.
    - **(e) COMPOSES WITH B10's R4 CLAMP + reversible ×2 [measured].** DescClear (datum 6000, ground 6012), free-mode radial descent tracked: descend coef 0.4 ×14 HOLDS at |pos|=**6012.0004 km** (R4 stop-and-hold, does not cross); ascend coef 2.0 ×14 escapes to **6065.500 km**; 2nd entry HOLDS at **6011.9999**, 2nd escape **6061.042** — the reversible pair traversed TWICE, the 2nd entry starting from the 1st exit's state. Enter body (datum=ground=0): descend → |pos|=**0.0000 km** (reaches the centre). The near-field descent settles-and-holds at the ground rather than fighting the clamp.

75. **DECISIONS_PENDING D1–D3 answered by Vixy (2026-07-22, recorded by Claude Fable 5). D1 RESOLVED (overrides the rec), D2 RESOLVED-by-criterion (new domain constraint §2.0 D8), D3 PARTIAL — a third data repository exists; completion re-asked.** Source: Vixy's inline answers in `DECISIONS_PENDING.md` (the raw record, per that file's protocol); this entry is what each answer decides. Key disambiguation: DECISIONS_PENDING's D-numbers are decision keys, distinct from §2.0's domain-constraint D-numbers.
    - **(a) D1 → B10 suspended (c) RESOLVED — option (2) GENERALIZED [vixy]: system nodes default `datum_radius = ground_radius = 0`, for ANY ModularSystem, not only MilkyWay** (*"those represent a system and as such, it is intended to go inside them. As a default, it can be trivially overrided by user-provided value if needed."*). The authored `=false` center-relative intent is honored as the CLASS default — keyed off the node's system nature (I4), never a per-name list; user-provided values override trivially. Bit-effect: Universe/per-system nodes have radius 0 (0 either way); **MilkyWay changes 3.2e9 AU → 0** — exactly the user-visible galaxy-scale free-mode navigation change the suspension named, now DECIDED, overriding the runtime-parity rec (the rec's premise "authored but never experienced" stands, Vixy chose to introduce it knowingly). Implementation = small (factory/createInfo default rule) but **scheduled as B10-(c) follow-through, NOT landed in this consolidation pass** — it needs its own discriminating harness case (MilkyWay datum=0 descent-to-centre + escape; `b10_nav.py` has the pattern). §11.71 suspended (c) closes; (a) floor value and (b) key word-order stay open (= DECISIONS_PENDING D12/D9, unanswered this round).
    - **(b) D2 → RESOLVED BY CRITERION, and the criterion is bigger than the question [vixy]: *"Everything must be physically exact, or visually identical to the physically identical."*** Extracted to **§2.0 D8** (domain constraint, with the performance-mechanism rationale + the §11.54 tension flag). Application to the B14 adjacents [derived from D8 — veto any part if wrong]: **(i) the 6 pole-bearing non-cluster garbage-tilt Saturn moons (Telesto, Pandora, Janus, Helene, Epimetheus — `rot_obliquity=150` — and Prometheus — `=50`; the LARGE 63–121° commutator errors, §11.68(b)) are IN SCOPE: B14 WIDENED** — garbage tilt on a resolvable body is neither exact nor visually identical; same acquisition discipline as the landed 14 (3-source cited fetch, §11.51(d) red line), not yet landed. **(ii) Hyperion stays UNWRITTEN**: chaotic tumbler, no defined pole in any of the 3 sources — a static "nominal" would be confabulation-adjacent (§11.51(d) outranks; same unrepresentability class as §11.69(g)); instead it gets an **inline comment in the data files** (the §11.66(b) discipline: annotation lands next to the erroneous datum) declaring the value a placeholder for a chaotic rotator. **(iii) the 6 loaded-only Jupiter moons fold into D3** (they are a data-source-authority question, not a physics one).
    - **(c) D3 → PARTIAL — a THIRD data source exists: the `spacecrafter-data` repository, holding more recent data than both the shipped `data/default_ssystem.ini` and the loaded `~/.spacecrafter/ssystem.ini`** [vixy — the answer sentence is incomplete in the file (*"…which hold more recent datas,"*); completion re-asked]. What it already changes: the shipped-vs-loaded question was posed TWO-source; it is THREE-source, and neither previously-examined file is presumptively authoritative. **NEW PROPAGATION HAZARD [derived]: B14's landed corrections live only in the shipped + loaded files — if `spacecrafter-data` is the upstream authority, the corrections must land there too, or a future data refresh silently reverts 14 cited IAU poles.** OPEN, re-asked with the completion: (i) authority order among the three sources; (ii) does an install/refresh flow `spacecrafter-data` → shipped → `~/.spacecrafter`; (iii) must B14 (and the (b) widening) land in `spacecrafter-data`; (iv) does the 6-loaded-only-moons divergence dissolve into a sync from `spacecrafter-data`? B14's suspended (ii) now points here; **B31's write-back design pass inherits the three-source reality** (its file-rewrite writer must not fight an upstream refresh channel).

76. **§2.0 D8 COMPLETED — the AS-IF RULE is the intent (2026-07-22, Vixy, recorded by Claude Fable 5).** Verbatim [vixy]: *"the intend it the as-if rule. As long as, from the user standpoint, the as-if rule is respected, deviation is valid. As soon as the position is used (fetched from script, warped to) it should be computed, and if previously frozen, recomputed with 4 extra iterations."*
    - **(a) Generalization**: "physically exact, or visually identical to the physically identical" (§11.75(b)) was the VISUAL projection; the rule itself is **observable-equivalence over every user-reachable channel** — script fetch and warp-to named explicitly, the class [derived] extends to draw/show, selection, save. Unifies with §11.66's save semantics (*"as-if we continued from the time we saved"*) — one product principle, stated independently in two domains; D8 now carries both.
    - **(b) Use-site barrier + the convergence constant**: any lazy/frozen state is valid IF a use triggers computation; a previously-frozen body recomputes with **+4 extra iterations** of the multi-frame iterative calculation (convergence restoration; the value is [vixy]-specified). Forward constraint for any freeze/lazy optimization: enumerate its use channels and install recompute-at-use — the barrier is part of the optimization, not an afterthought.
    - **(c) SUPERSEDES the §11.75(b)/§2.0 [derived] tension reconciliation** (original preserved there, struck): the B19/§11.54 tension resolves by the barrier, not by mandatory continuous ticking — zero-update between uses is valid, Q13's "where it should be now" is satisfied at the use (show = a use). §11.54's landed continuous translation-only tick REMAINS VALID as one licensed mechanism (it trivially maintains as-if); a future freeze optimization is licensed too, IF barriered. The `harness/b19_hidden_tick.py` asserts stay discriminating under either mechanism — they read positions through a use channel, so a correct barrier passes them and a stale read fails them, which is exactly the observable the tester mandated. Process note: the wrong mechanism carried a `[derived]`+veto flag, which is what made this a one-message correction — the labeling protocol functioning as designed.
    - **(d) B14/data applications unaffected**: garbage data fails as-if on every channel (fetch a moon's orientation → wrong answer); the §11.75(b) widening and Hyperion handling stand.

77. **D3 COMPLETED — the data-ecosystem model lands, and it reframes more than D3 (2026-07-22, Vixy, recorded by Claude Fable 5).** Facts [vixy]: `~/.spacecrafter` currently holds the latest data version (to Vixy's knowledge); deliveries are **frozen individually**, updates **offline** — no automated fetch in the general case — done **by hand, taking the latest sources (not data)**; *"this is why I requested backward compatibility"*. Business model: code open-source, **the data is what is paid** — pro = spacecrafter + data feeding features/capabilities (the funding, though small). A few enthusiast users write powerful scripts and push past tested/thought capabilities — expected and wanted; the modular refactoring exists to give that minority every combination trivially AND to enable composing richer pro data.
    - **(a) The four §11.75(c) re-asks, answered by the model**: (i) authority = `spacecrafter-data` (repo), materialized as `~/.spacecrafter` which is currently its latest [derived reading of the two statements — veto if wrong]; shipped `data/default_ssystem.ini` = older subset (version lag, not a fork); (ii) NO refresh flow exists — the field is frozen; (iii) corrections reach future deliveries only by **hand-landing in `spacecrafter-data`** (Vixy's channel — the B14 pole block is ready to carry over on request); (iv) the 6-loaded-only-moons divergence **DISSOLVES** (shipped lag; reconciles at the next hand-refresh).
    - **(b) The B14 propagation hazard DISSOLVES as stated, replaced by the forward-only rule**: no automated refresh ⇒ no silent-revert path. The real model: **fix upstream, tolerate in-field garbage forever** — backward compatibility carries it (B28's derived-default frame keeps old data rendering exactly as always; the commutator divergence on uncorrected field data is accepted by design). The D2 widening (+6 Saturn moons) takes the same channel.
    - **(c) Backward compatibility now carries its forcing argument.** It was a standing request (§11.52 legacy guarantee, Q26, B24's untouched legacy path); it is now a *forced consequence* of the delivery model (frozen field; hand updates take sources, not data). Extracted to **§2.0 D9** with edges: capability keys = product surface; the §11.66(b) writer touches PAID hand-authored data (corruption = product-destruction class); forward-only correction flow.
    - **(d) The potential-optimization criterion extracted to §2.0 D10**, Vixy's uncertainty marker preserved verbatim. Carried with it [derived, veto-able]: the structural-generality qualifier (combination space via orthogonal primitives, not feature width — I1–I6 the counterweight), the already-landed cost bound (§11.50(b): median path stays simple), and falsifiable-in-flight indicators — the loop has already fired once (surprising scripts → this refactoring), so time is the terminal judge but not the only available signal.
    - **(e) Market structure (same-day completion) [vixy]**: target = the **small interactive planetarium** — a structurally small niche, existing and not otherwise filled. Large planetarium software is structurally EXCLUDED from these features: large audience ⇒ no real interactivity ⇒ no need for the flexibility — **the niche is defended by structural mismatch, not by a moat; it is why spacecrafter exists**. Segment economics: small planetariums have small funds; the lower plan is the FREE version, sufficient for entry (D9 refined).
    - **(f) The two populations + the documentation gate [vixy]**: enthusiasts do not need the paid data for their "crazy things" — *"they only need the capability to be there and well documented"*; pro venues fund via data — two distinct populations, one loop (discovery feeds capability, capability feeds pro-data richness, data funds development). **D10 indicator (iv) refined: entry cost is already minimal (free tier) — the gating variable is DOCUMENTATION**, a known weakness [vixy: *"not a great strength"*], sequenced deliberately AFTER core-ready. Consequences: population-growth flatness before docs exist is CONFOUNDED, not invalidating (the clean test starts when capability + documentation both exist); documentation is the D10-completing work item — capability present but undocumented is potential locked, recorded here so the sequencing is a decision on record, not a drift.

Replacement criterion [vixy mandate + §11.19a; **sharpened §11.52(b)**]: every script-reachable seam in §9 routes new-path AND legacy commands produce visually identical output (A/B screenshot channel) — identity at the **reasonable-vision-capabilities** level, and **only where old was physically exact**: old defects that didn't reliably represent reality must NOT be reproduced and are tracked as defects (the named-divergence channel is that tracking). Then: `drawModularSystem` permanently new → old-path update off (A/B stays as harness mode) → ~~removal as separate Vixy-gated cleanup~~ **REVISED [vixy 2026-07-21, §11.51(f)]: both paths kept for backward compatibility *"in emergency"* through genuine public use — removal horizon extends past public burn-in.** Every "retires with the old path" item keeps its trigger (the trigger fires later, nothing reschedules silently), and keeping the old path building + A/B-testable is a standing capability, not scaffolding.
