### 13.D External inputs (companion files, single authority — 2026-07-20)

**[2026-07-22 repo split: this file and every companion file below now live together in the `claude/` nested repository (branch `CC-harness`, same host) — "repo root" in the paragraph below reads as pre-split history; layout + conventions in `README.md`. Companion-file refs to `src/experimentalModule/INTENT.md` were corrected to `INTENT.md` (same folder) the same day. A fourth companion, `DECISIONS_PENDING.md` (2026-07-21-wave residuals view, compiled 2026-07-22), follows the same authority rule: it owns no decision, this ledger wins on divergence.]**

Three files at repo root, outside this tree, so they're reachable without going through `experimentalModule`: **`USER_QUESTIONS.md`** — the rows that are genuinely user-observable behavior (not internal-architecture questions) translated to plain language, each tagged back to its `Axx`/`Bxx` ref for exact re-matching; answers resolve the corresponding row here, not a copy of it. **Coverage as of 2026-07-20 (expanded pass, addressed to a heavy early-release tester — planetarium/sky expertise assumed, implementation knowledge NOT assumed)**: 26 of the 29 §13.A rows — A1, A2, A3, A4, A5, A7, A8, A9, A10, A11, A12, A13, A14, A15, A17, A18, A19, A20, A23, A24, A25, A26, A27, A28, A29 — plus **B4**; A23's question narrowed to a completeness check the same day (row resolved [vixy 2026-07-20], but "do your scripts use the two-body patch, and does a per-body boolean reproduce them?" is answerable only by a script author and validates B10's scope) (CameraAnchors: which anchor kinds are actually used decides the port's design, and only a user can answer it). Deliberately excluded, and the exclusion is stated *inside* the companion file so the tester can challenge it: A6, A16, A21, A22 (no user-visible surface) and the coordinate/threading B-rows. Two factual corrections landed in the same pass — the first draft's A12 question described the copy-paste cluster as *orbital* data causing *positional* error (it is `rot_obliquity`/`rot_equator_ascending_node` axial-orientation data causing up-to-155° *orientation* error, 28 bodies, Iapetus the one observable case), and its A5 question described `apparent_magnitude`/`slope` as brightness parameters (they are H/G driving coma diameter + tail length via the comet-tail formula, and no shipped body sets them — so the decisive sub-question is whether the tester's *scripts* set them). Both were derived-doc drift of exactly the §5.2 class: a companion file is a cached conclusion and needs re-verification against source, not recall. **`FEATURE_REQUESTS.md`** — the landing place for requests originating outside this tracker; created empty (no backfill — nothing invented to fill it), log format self-documented in the file. Triage direction is one-way and explicit: a logged request that turns into real work gets a row in §13 (A or B) with a pointer back to its `FEATURE_REQUESTS.md` entry; the entry's `Status` is then updated to point here — it does not get silently duplicated into a second untracked TODO. **Round 1 answered 2026-07-21 (tester, relayed by Vixy) and propagated the same day — full record §11.48**: 13 rows closed (§13.C list), 11 narrowed in place, 1 new row (A30), 13 implementation rows opened (B11–B23), 2 entries logged in `FEATURE_REQUESTS.md`. Round-2 questions ~~live at the bottom of `USER_QUESTIONS.md`~~ **moved to the dedicated `USER_QUESTIONS_ROUND2.md` (2026-07-21, Vixy's direction)** — live re-asks R1, R3–R11 (R8/R10 reframed per §11.51's resolutions: R8 = future-preference input under the parity meta-rule, R10 = completeness check on B27's code-extracted list; R11 stakes-dropped, both channels build regardless per B17), plus a no-longer-questions section: R2 (resolved §11.49(a), outcome stated with an explicit veto window — the tester's original answer was ambiguous, so stating the adopted behavior back is the cheap verification) and R12 (withdrawn, informational, updated with §11.52(c)'s naming). **Round 2 answered 2026-07-22 (tester, relayed by Vixy) and propagated — full record §11.70**: A28/A17/A18/A4/A27 resolved; B4's design-deciding half answered; B10 confirmed (patch purpose = fly-into/transparent bodies) with one derivation OVERTURNED by R4 (stop-and-hold at the lowest altitude, not the inward asymptote); B17/B20/B27/B29/B31 informed; R2 confirms A9. Numbering stable, `(ref:)` keys unchanged; the old round-2 section in `USER_QUESTIONS.md` is now a pointer (single authority, no fork). The split by **who can answer** (tester-only = what shows/scripts actually do; Vixy = design authority) was round 2's correction of a round-1 defect — some round-1 questions went to the party who could not answer them. Round 1's own two failures are carried in §11.48(d) and constrain round 2: **name the observable, never the engine's internal vocabulary** (the `ring_shadow` failure — a document that declares "implementation knowledge not assumed" and then assumes it), and **give options labels distinct from case labels** (the A9 failure — an answer's ambiguity is the question's defect). The tester also asked two questions back; those are answered at the top of round 2 — a companion file that only asks is a one-way channel, which is a self-imposed blind spot on the one party holding the shipped-show evidence. Neither file is authoritative over anything in §13.A–13.C; on disagreement (a question answered here but not reflected in USER_QUESTIONS.md's traceability note, or vice versa) this ledger wins per I2, and the divergence itself is a staleness bug to fix, not a fork to maintain.
